# AnglePlates 1.1 release spec

Status: revision 3 of the 1.1 spec, 2026-10-14. Revision 2 folded in the owner's requirements O11 (culture setting), O12 (docs name the mechanism behind a default), and O13 (the icon draft goes now). Revision 3 folds in the owner's review of revision 2 ([O14 to O19](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)):

- **O14.** The column-0 emitter layout (S39) is approved. Generated files must not cause analyzer warnings because of it. The owner chose the mechanism ([S54](#26-spec-decisions-for-11); its residual gap is accepted as documented, O20): the `// <auto-generated>` header and the `.g.cs` hint names keep analyzers that exclude generated code off them, and a new `StrictAnalyzers` package consumer proves it.
- **O15.** No `//` handling anywhere; embedded C# is always copied verbatim (S31 confirmed).
- **O16.** The `#encode` directive and the static `TemplateOptions` presets are removed. Encoding is set only through `TemplateOptions.Encoder` (`HtmlEncoder.Default` unless the options set it), and `TemplateOptions.Culture` is independent of it. This settles S43 and withdraws S47.
- **O17.** Directive values are no longer validated with Roslyn (S40 overturned). SLAP001 and SLAP002 are retired, and the simplification goal now covers directive parsing.
- **O18.** Agents may commit and push (S41 resolved).
- **O19.** No agent credential and no Issues permission for now. Agents don't file GitHub issues (S36 withdrawn); progress is tracked in this spec's [implementation checklist](#implementation-checklist), and the work can land in as many commits as it needs.

This spec supersedes `plans/release-1.0-spec.md`, which is deleted in the same commit that adds this file, together with `plans/icon-draft/` (the draft icon and its review renders, [O13](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)). Everything from the 1.0 spec that still applies is carried here. Items that 1.1 changes are kept and marked **No longer in scope (superseded by 1.1)** with a link to what replaces them, so nobody has to dig the old file out of history to understand a decision.

**What 1.0 delivered.** Phases 0 through 7 of the 1.0 spec are implemented and merged to `main` through [pull request #1](https://github.com/SlusserLabs/AnglePlates/pull/1) (merged 2026-10-14 as `6d59e69`, CI green on all four legs, no PR review or comment recorded). That work gave the repository: line-ending and editorconfig hygiene; CI on four legs with a 90% coverage gate; readable hint names and a versionless header; a generator split by concern; a Roslyn-lexer C# block scanner; the stable consumer API (immutable `TemplateOptions` with presets, `#encode`, optional `#type` and `#namespace`, `sealed partial` classes, `RenderToString`, three `Write` overloads); a clean SLAP001-SLAP011 registry with help links; the analyzer-only package with `build/` assets and 14 package consumers; the approved icon; the full doc set; and `release.yml` with the publishing guide. `release.yml` has never been dispatched. Phases 8 and 9 (owner inputs, int rehearsal, stable release) haven't started. (1.1 removes the presets, `#encode`, SLAP001, and SLAP002 from that list again: [O16](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11), [O17](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11).)

**What 1.1 changes.** The owner wants the template parser to stop understanding C#. The Roslyn-lexer scanner goes away entirely. In its place, openers and closers can repeat their brackets (up to four), and a block ends at the first exact match of its closer. Backslash escapes go away too. The class writer stops counting braces and stops looking for `//` comments. The same scaling back reaches the directives ([O16](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11), [O17](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)): the `#encode` directive and the four static `TemplateOptions` presets go away, so encoding is chosen only through `TemplateOptions.Encoder`, and `DirectiveReader` stops validating directive values with Roslyn, so a bad `#model`, `#using`, `#namespace`, or `#type` value (given or derived) becomes a compiler error in the generated file and SLAP001 and SLAP002 are retired. `TemplateOptions` gains a `Culture` property, `CultureInfo.InvariantCulture` unless the options set `Culture`, which C# variables convert with ([4.11](#411-culture)). Human-facing docs that describe a default name the directive, option, property, parameter, or MSBuild setting that changes it ([15.7](#157-defaults-name-their-mechanism)). The first stable version becomes **1.1.0**. Everything else in the 1.0 plan (publishing, guardrails, docs, coverage, package contract) carries over.

**This spec doesn't approve publishing anything to nuget.org.** Every nuget.org release needs the owner's explicit approval for that release.

Line numbers drift. This spec cites symbols; when it cites a line, verify it against the code before relying on it.

## How to use this spec

This section is for the implementing agent (Claude Ultracode), which receives only this file.

1. Read [AGENTS.md](../AGENTS.md) first. It binds you: skill routing (`/csharp` for every C# change including emitted strings, `/dotnet-tunit` for tests, `/docs` for `docs/`, `/humanizer` for human-facing prose and code comments but never for AGENTS.md, plans, or the README's AI Disclosure), the emitted-code `global::` rule, the testing conventions, the validation commands, the publishing guardrails, and the rule against persisting agent-only tooling.
2. Work on the branch that holds this spec (expected name `release-1.1`). If this spec has already been merged to `main`, create `release-1.1` from `origin/main`. Agents may commit and push as they work ([O18](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)). Commit in as many small commits as the work needs; every commit must build. The work goes to `main` as **one pull request**, because the owner reviews the generated-code change once ([S35](#26-spec-decisions-for-11)). Open that pull request as a **draft** right after the first push (`gh pr create --draft --base main --head release-1.1`), because `ci.yml` runs only on pull requests and pushes to `main`; the draft makes CI run on every later push, as AGENTS.md requires.
3. **Track progress in the [implementation checklist](#implementation-checklist), not on GitHub** ([O19](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)). When you finish a checklist item, tick it (`- [x]`) in this file in the same commit as the work, and add a short note after the item when something deviated, was measured, or needs the owner (for example a measured line count, a CI run ID, or why a target was missed). Keep the checklist current at every push, so a later session that receives only this file can see what's done and resume at the first unticked item. Don't file GitHub issues and don't use issues, labels, or project boards for tracking. Pull request comments are fine for CI notes, but the checklist is the record.
4. Do B0 to B5 in order, ending at STOP P1. Each has acceptance criteria, mirrored as checklist items. B6 runs only after the owner merges the pull request, usually in a later session. Phases 8 and 9 need the owner and aren't yours to finish.
5. Stop where this spec says **STOP**. The full list:
   - **STOP R1** in Phase B6 if `gh secret list` shows any secret: the `release.yml` dispatch would then try to publish a preview, which needs the preview preflight first.
   - **STOP P1** at the end of Phase B5: the owner reviews the snapshots and the voice, then merges. Agents don't merge the 1.1 pull request.
   - **STOP G1** in Phase B0 if `gh auth status` fails, or if `git push` or `gh pr create` is refused. Use the git and `gh` credentials already on the machine; there's no separate agent credential ([O19](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)). Don't work around a refusal (no other remote, no other account, no token from the environment).
   - **STOP** anywhere this spec's instructions conflict with what you find in the repository in a way that would need a guess (for example a test that can't pass under the stated rule). Report the conflict instead of picking.
   - **STOP** if a CI leg fails for a reason the branch can't fix (a runner image, a hosted SDK, or a GitHub outage) after one rerun with `gh run rerun --failed`. Report the run ID and the failing step.
6. Line endings: follow `.editorconfig` and `.gitattributes` for each file (`*.md`, `*.cs`, `*.yml` CRLF; `*.ps1` LF; `*.verified.*` LF; `*.html` fixtures keep exact bytes). Template fixtures under `Templates` folders keep the bytes this spec specifies. This file is CRLF; keep it CRLF when you tick checklist items.
7. Never write report, notes, or helper files into the repository. Measurements go in the pull request description, with a one-line summary next to the checklist item they belong to.
8. Every GitHub action an agent may take is in [13.4](#134-agent-guardrails). In particular, `gh` may run as the owner's own login; never approve a deployment, change a variable, environment, ruleset, or repository setting, or dispatch the nuget.org login check, even though that login technically could.

## Implementation checklist

This is the progress record for the 1.1 work ([O19](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)); GitHub issues aren't used. Tick an item (`- [x]`) in the same commit as the work it describes, and add a short note after it when something deviated, was measured, or needs the owner (for example "- [x] ... (`DirectiveReader.cs` 184 lines)"). Push with the work, so a later session can resume at the first unticked item. Each item points at the section that defines it; the section is authoritative if they ever disagree. Items marked **Owner** aren't the agent's to tick.

(`BlockSyntaxTests`: escape tests replaced by `Backslash_BeforeBlock_*` and `LiteralOpener_ThroughVariable_*`, `HigherLevel_ClosersInsideCSharp_*` (brace-in-string row kept), `LevelEquivalence_*`, `MultiLineCSharp_*`, 11 open/close pairs in `EmptyBlockCases`, the level rows in the merged-literal and ordinary-bracket tests; the 15-closer test is gone. `LineMappingTests`: six rows raised to higher levels, six multi-line rows, `LineCommentVariable_*`, `ClosersInsideCSharp_*`. Generator `TemplateOptionsTests` has `Encoder` and `Culture` rows for CS8852 (both names have seven letters, so one span serves both). `NameCollisionTests`: `ToInvariantString` is now `ToText`, plus `Culture`. **Second look, as the spec invites:** the expectations for `LineCommentVariable_*` and `ClosersInsideCSharp_*` were written by hand from the rules in 11.2, then compared with the compiler. The IDs, mapped columns of the syntax errors, and the generated-file CS1026 matched the derivation. Three things the spec text didn't predict, each explained and pinned: (1) the compiler also reports binding errors from the broken call (CS1501 "takes 1 arguments" on the whole variable for `Write(model` with its `)` swallowed; CS1503 "cannot convert from 'void'" in the generated file when the next statement becomes the second argument; CS1501 "'this' takes 2 arguments" and CS0022 for the bracket rows); (2) a tree lists all syntax errors in source order before any binding errors, so a UTF-8 file reads Render's syntax errors, RenderAsync's, then both methods' binding errors; (3) a missing token is zero width at the end of the previous token when the next token is on a later line (the `// note` rows), but spans the next token when it shares the line (the `]` rows, 1 character at the `)` and `;`). After a C# block, RenderAsync writes the variable with `await WriteAsync(` and a token argument, so those columns differ there (a block-then-variable row pins both).)

Support types, all eight, as 11.2 lists: `TemplateOptions` (x2) lose the four presets and `#encode` text, gain the `_culture` field and `Culture` property with docs, and the class summary now says "convert and encode"; `TextTemplate` and `Utf8Template` (x4) rename `ToInvariantString` to `ToText` with a culture parameter, call it with `Options.Culture`, point the `Write<T>(T)` summary at `TemplateOptions.Culture`, and carry the S37 comment; `TemplateExtensions` (x2) create the `StringWriter` with `template.Options.Culture`. ## 1. Goals and non-goals

### Goals

1. Prepare `SlusserLabs.AnglePlates` **1.1.0** for nuget.org as an analyzer-only development dependency, with `1.1.0-preview.<run>` previews rehearsed on int.nugettest.org first. The nuget.org publish happens only after the owner explicitly approves that release.
   - The 1.0 wording ("Prepare 1.0.0") is **No longer in scope (superseded by 1.1).** No 1.0.0 ships; the first stable version is 1.1.0 ([owner decision O8](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)).
2. Lock a template syntax, generated API, MSBuild surface, and diagnostic set that we're happy to keep stable for the 1.x line, starting at 1.1.0. The locked syntax is the bracket-level syntax in [4.1](#41-template-syntax): levels 1 to 4, a block ends at the first exact match of its closer, no escapes, and no C# awareness.
3. Keep generated code Native AOT and trimming safe, with no reflection and no runtime package.
4. Make the generator and its tests easy for a junior engineer to follow: small classes with one job each, purpose comments, and no clever code. Don't add a type, helper, or layer unless it removes more complexity than it adds. **For 1.1 this goal is measured**: the parser change and the directive simplification ([O17](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)) must make the generator measurably smaller and simpler ([6.7](#67-simplification-targets)), and the pull request reports before and after line counts. Scaling back C# parsing covers directive values too: the generator copies them into the generated code and lets the compiler judge them.
5. Prove the library, the emitted code, and the packed package with tests, and keep coverage measured.
6. Ship documentation that sounds like the owner wrote it and that a junior engineer can follow without being over-explained to.
7. Replace the icon with a `[ ]` angle-plate mark in SVG and PNG. **Done and approved** on 2026-10-14; the bracket mark still fits the bracket-level syntax.
8. Let consumers pick the culture C# variables convert with, through `TemplateOptions.Culture`, keeping `CultureInfo.InvariantCulture` as the default ([O11](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11), [4.11](#411-culture)).
9. One place to choose an encoder: `TemplateOptions.Encoder`. No `#encode` directive and no static presets ([O16](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)).
10. Generated files never cause analyzer warnings in a consumer's build because of their layout, while compiler warnings from template C# keep surfacing at the template location ([O14](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11), [8.7](#87-generated-code-and-consumer-analyzers)).

### Non-goals

These are permanent scope rules, not deferrals. Each one goes into both AGENTS.md's Scope section and the README's "What AnglePlates doesn't do" section. At `6d59e69` the README lists the first four, and AGENTS.md's Scope lists only T4, `.tt` files, and runtime compilation; Phase B4 fills both gaps ([15.4](#154-documentation-that-changes-with-11)).

- **No T4 syntax or compatibility.** AnglePlates won't read `.tt` files or `<# #>` blocks. The README may still compare the idea to T4, in the owner's voice, as long as it says the syntax is different.
- **No runtime template compilation.** Templates compile with the consumer's project. There's no API to compile a template string at run time, and there never will be.
- **No runtime package.** Everything the compiled templates need is emitted into the consumer's assembly.
- **No IntelliSense inside template files.** Errors map back to templates through `#line`, which is the supported editing story for 1.x.
- **No understanding of embedded C#** (new in 1.1). The parser and the class writer never lex, parse, or search the C# inside a C# variable or C# block for strings, comments, brackets, braces, slashes, or preprocessor directives. The only thing the generator does with that C# is copy it into the render method under a `#line` mapping, following the layout rule in [8](#8-emitting-c-without-reading-it). The same holds for directive values ([O17](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)): `#model`, `#using`, `#namespace`, and `#type` values, given or derived, are copied into the generated code without Roslyn validation, and the compiler reports what's wrong with them. The generator checks only the values AnglePlates itself defines (`#generate`, `#access`), that a value isn't blank, and that a derived `#type` isn't empty ([4.2](#42-directives)).

### Deferred past 1.1

**Dropped by the owner on 2026-10-14 ([O20](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)).** These items are out of scope. They aren't tracked anywhere, aren't filed as issues, and aren't kept after release prep deletes `plans/`. They stay listed here only as the record of what was dropped. The 1.0 plan's "each one gets a GitHub issue in Phase B0" is **No longer in scope (superseded by 1.1).**

- A `RenderToBytes` convenience for UTF-8, and a `Stream` overload of `RenderAsync`.
- A static facade (`OrderLineTemplate.Render(model, writer)`) that hides the Text/UTF-8 split for child templates.
- `TemplateOptions.FormatProvider` for culture-specific formatting. **No longer in scope (superseded by 1.1).** `TemplateOptions.Culture` ships in 1.1.0 ([O11](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11), [S43](#26-spec-decisions-for-11), [4.11](#411-culture)), so it isn't deferred.
- A project-wide default for `#generate` (`AnglePlateGenerate`). The `#encode` half (`AnglePlateEncode`) is **No longer in scope (superseded by 1.1)**: there's no `#encode` directive to default ([O16](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)).
- Span-based `ISpanFormattable`/`IUtf8SpanFormattable` formatting for value types (see [9.3](#93-considered-and-skipped)).
- A TextMate grammar or VS Code extension for template files. The bracket-level syntax makes this easier: openers and closers are regular, and nothing depends on C#.

## 2. Decision log

### 2.1 Owner decisions (2026-10-13)

| Topic | Decision | 1.1 status |
| --- | --- | --- |
| T4 | Rejected permanently. No T4 syntax will be supported. | Unchanged |
| Runtime generation | Never supported. Compile-time only. | Unchanged |
| Preview channel | Previews publish only to int.nugettest.org. Agents may publish previews there whenever they want (after the preview preflight). | Unchanged; previews are `1.1.0-preview.<run>` |
| Stable channel | Stable versions publish only to nuget.org, and never without the owner's explicit approval for that release. **This spec doesn't grant that approval.** | Unchanged |
| Access checks | The plan must include checks that agents have the access they need to publish to both feeds. | Unchanged ([13.5](#135-publishing-access-preflight)) |
| Publishing mechanism | GitHub Actions with NuGet trusted publishing (OIDC), plus CI on every pull request. | Unchanged |
| Commits and pushes | Agents may commit and push to the remote repository as they work through the release spec. Since CI exists, work goes up as a branch and pull request. | **Widened by [O18](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11).** Agents may commit and push as they work, not only while working through a release spec. AGENTS.md's Commits bullet loses "while working through the release spec" in Phase B0, so release prep needs no new wording. |
| Approval authority | **Only the owner** approves making the repository public and publishing to nuget.org. Agents never do either. | Unchanged |
| Repository visibility | Public before the first stable release, and only when the owner approves it. | Unchanged; the first stable release is 1.1.0 |
| AI disclosure | The README uses the same AI Disclosure section as AssemblyInfo, word for word ([15.3](#153-readme)). | Unchanged and binding on every README edit |
| Copyright | `Copyright (c) 2026 SlusserLabs, Jacob Slusser. All rights reserved.` in the csproj and LICENSE. | Done |
| Owner inputs | The GitHub plan, NuGet secrets and policies, the agent credential, the CODEOWNERS handle, and similar inputs are collected at the very end (Phase 8). Nothing earlier waits on them. | Unchanged, except that no agent credential is collected for now ([O19](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)); the 1.1 phases need no owner input except the final review (STOP P1) |
| First stable version | 1.0.0. | **No longer in scope (superseded by 1.1).** The first stable version is 1.1.0 ([O8](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)). |
| Delimiter scanning | C# variables and C# blocks become C#-aware through Roslyn's lexer. | **No longer in scope (superseded by 1.1).** Implemented in 1.0 Phase 3 and now removed. Replaced by bracket levels and the first-exact-match rule ([O1](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11) to [O6](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)). |
| Icon | Frontal-oblique, flat-shaded, solid angle-plate brackets with bolt holes and a soft blue glow. Approved 2026-10-14 as committed (`icon.svg` and the 256x256 `icon.png` at the repo root). | Done |
| `TemplateOptions` | `sealed` with `init`-only properties. `BufferPool` and the hand-rolled encode loop are removed. | Done; binding |
| C# variable emission | One-line `Write(value, writer);` calls against a base-class overload set. `[[ null ]]` compiles and writes `NullVariable`. | Kept. 1.1 removes the `//`-comment special case ([O15](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11), [S31](#26-spec-decisions-for-11)); a multi-line expression follows the layout rule in [8](#8-emitting-c-without-reading-it). |
| `Model` alias | Kept. The generated `Model` local stays next to `model`. | Done; binding |
| Writer guard | Generated `Render` and `RenderAsync` start with `ArgumentNullException.ThrowIfNull(writer)`. | Done; binding |
| Base classes | Constructors and the flush helpers (`WriteAsync`, `FlushAsync`) are `private protected`. `Write` and `WriteRaw` stay `protected`. | Done; binding |
| Sealing | Generated template classes are emitted `sealed partial`. | Done; binding |
| `RenderToString` | An extension method on `TextTemplate<TModel>`. | Done |
| `Raw()` helper | Skipped. `[% WriteRaw(value, writer); %]` stays the way to write unencoded output. | Unchanged. It's also one way to write a literal opener ([4.1](#writing-openers-as-text)). |
| `#encode` directive | In 1.0. | **No longer in scope (superseded by 1.1).** Implemented in 1.0 and now removed; encoding is chosen only through `TemplateOptions.Encoder` ([O16](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)). |
| Optional `#type` and `#namespace` | In 1.0. | Done, and kept: "1.0" now means 1.1.0 (O8). Derivation stays, but neither a given nor a derived value is validated anymore; an invalid name is a compiler error in the generated file ([O17](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)). |
| Misspelled-directive warning | In 1.0. | Done (SLAP011). Unchanged in meaning; "1.0" now means 1.1.0 (O8). |
| Per-assembly support namespace | **Not in 1.0.** Support types stay in `SlusserLabs.AnglePlates`, and the cross-assembly collision stays documented. | Unchanged in meaning; with no 1.0.0 shipping, "not in 1.0" now means not in 1.1.0 (O8). |
| Coverage | Manual and CI. CI doesn't need 100%; it fails below 90%. The owner checks 100% before every stable release. | Unchanged |

### 2.2 Owner decisions (2026-10-14, from the 1.0 review)

| Topic | Decision | 1.1 status |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Benchmark slowdown | **Accepted.** Writing a value that needs escaping got about 19% slower (577 to 686 ns for a 220-character encoded string) in exchange for the simpler framework-call encoding. HTML encoding stays the default. | Done; binding: none of the old encode loop comes back |
| Scanner fallback | **Removed** (overturned S15). When the scanner couldn't find a closer it accepted, the parser reported SLAP004 at the opener. The 16-closer window stayed. | **No longer in scope (superseded by 1.1).** There's no scanner and no window. SLAP004 fires only when the exact closer for the opener's level never appears after it, and the fix is to add the closer or raise the bracket level ([S28](#26-spec-decisions-for-11)). |
| Duplicate template classes | **Keep the current behavior** (S21). The generator reports nothing; it numbers the hint names so `AddSource` never throws, and the compiler reports CS0111, CS0102, and CS0121 at the generated files. | Unchanged; binding |
| Icon | **Approved** as committed. | Done |
| Snapshot file names | Short snapshot names through the `GeneratorSnapshot` helper, and `RepositoryPathTests` holds every repository path to 100 characters. | Done; binding. Every new or renamed 1.1 snapshot uses `GeneratorSnapshot` short names and must pass `RepositoryPathTests`. |

### 2.3 Owner decisions (2026-10-14, 1.1)

These are the owner's requirements for 1.1, with the details the owner settled when asked.

| # | Topic | Decision |
| --- | --- | --- |
| O1 | Parser simplicity | The template parser must not be aware of embedded C#. Delete `CSharpBlockScanner` (`FindCloser`, its 16-closer windowing, `CountBraces`, `EndsWithLineComment`) and every attempt to understand C# strings, comments, brackets, or directives inside blocks. The owner will look for a measurable reduction and simplification of code. |
| O2 | Bracket levels | An opener may repeat its `[` and its closer repeats `]` the same number of times. `[* A comment. *]` and `[[[* A comment. *]]]` are functionally identical, and so are `[[ model.Message ]]` and `[[[[ model.Message ]]]]`. C# blocks work the same way: `[% ... %]`, `[[% ... %]]`, `[[[% ... %]]]`, `[[[[% ... %]]]]`. |
| O3 | Limit | At most **four** consecutive `[` at the start of a block and four `]` at the end, so the code never has to anticipate an unbounded count. |
| O4 | Closer rule | **First exact match.** Once the bracket level `N` is known, the closer is the first occurrence of the closer string built from `N`: `]` times `N` for a C# variable, `%` plus `]` times `N` for a C# block, `*` plus `]` times `N` for a comment. One ordinal `IndexOf`, no run-length checks, no C# awareness. So `[[ a[b[0]] ]]` ends after `0` and the author writes `[[[ a[b[0]] ]]]`, and `[% var s = "%]"; %]` ends inside the string and the author writes `[[% var s = "%]"; %]]`. |
| O5 | Over the limit | **Extra brackets are literal.** In a run of more than four `[`, only the last four form the opener; the earlier ones are literal text. `[[[[[ x ]]]]]` renders `[`, then `x`, then `]`. No new diagnostic. |
| O6 | Opener validation | A run of `k` consecutive `[` uses level `N = min(k, 4)`. Followed by `%` it's a C# block; followed by `*` it's a comment; otherwise it's a C# variable when `N >= 2`. A single `[` not followed by `%` or `*` is literal text. So C# variables have levels 2 to 4, and C# blocks and comments have levels 1 to 4. Consequence to document: `[[*ptr ]]` opens a level-2 comment, so write `[[ *ptr ]]`. Trim markers (`-` right after the opener, `-` right before the closer) work at every level, and `[[ count--]]` still trims. |
| O7 | Escapes removed | The bracket-level syntax supersedes all previous escape syntax. Backslash escapes (`\[[`, `\[%`, `\[*`, and backslash pairing) are removed. A literal opener sequence in template text is written through a C# variable, such as `[[ "[[" ]]` or `[[[ "]]" ]]]`, or with `WriteRaw` in a C# block. `\[[` now means a literal backslash followed by a C# variable. |
| O8 | First stable version | **1.1.0.** `VersionPrefix` 1.1.0, previews `1.1.0-preview.<run>`, local packs `1.1.0-local.<yyyyMMdd>.<n>`, tag `v1.1.0`, CHANGELOG `## 1.1.0`, AnalyzerReleases `## Release 1.1.0`. |
| O9 | Emitter hard line | In the owner's words: "The C# code should maintain the existing line breaks it has and be placed at the same indent level as the generated C# type. We should NOT search for brackets, comments, slashes, ... whatever. This spec draws a hard line making any sort of attempts to understand the embedded C# blocks out of scope. The ONLY thing the project is allowed to do is consume the embedded C# line by line (i.e. split on line breaks) and emit those lines with the same initial indent as the generated type; without that, the compiler/consumer is liable to see syntax warnings in the generated types." So: no brace-based indentation and no line-comment detection. `#line` mappings stay exact per AGENTS.md. The layout this spec reads from it is [E1 and S39](#24-emitter-layout-e1-resolved-by-o9): every line at the generated type's indent, column 0. The owner approved that reading in O14. |
| O10 | Breaking changes | The project hasn't shipped. Breaking changes are free, and notes or docs that only explained the old approach (the scanner, escapes, the 15/16-closer limit) are deleted, not preserved. Everything (docs, README, tests, snapshots, diagnostic messages, code generation, package consumers, benchmarks, samples, AGENTS.md, lexicon, CHANGELOG, `exclusion.dic`) is updated to the new behavior. |
| O11 | Culture setting | Consumers control the culture used for C# variable string conversions, defaulting to `CultureInfo.InvariantCulture`, and it's passed to every applicable string operation. The owner doesn't prescribe the API: the spec compares a `CultureInfo` property on `TemplateOptions` with an argument to `Render`, `RenderAsync`, `RenderToString`, and the rest, recommends one as a spec decision the owner can overturn, and the implementer builds that recommendation unless the owner changes it before handing off. The recommendation was the property ([S43](#26-spec-decisions-for-11), [4.11](#411-culture)), and the owner settled it that way in O16. |
| O12 | Docs name the mechanism | Statements like "C# variables go through `HtmlEncoder.Default` unless you say otherwise" are too casual, because they don't tell the reader what to change. When human-facing prose describes a default, it names the exact directive, option, property, parameter, or MSBuild setting that changes it. No "unless you say otherwise", "unless you set it", or bare "by default" without the mechanism. This becomes an AGENTS.md Author Voice rule, and the B4 docs pass fixes every current offender ([S46](#26-spec-decisions-for-11), [15.7](#157-defaults-name-their-mechanism)). |
| O13 | Icon draft | `plans/icon-draft/` (the draft icon and its review renders) is deleted together with `plans/release-1.0-spec.md` in the commit that adds this spec. The committed root `icon.svg` and `icon.png` stay and remain the source ([14](#14-icon)). |
| O14 | Emitter layout approved; no analyzer warnings from it | The owner approved S39: every line of a C# block or C# variable is emitted verbatim at column 0, the generated type's indent under the file-scoped namespace. Added requirement: generated types must not cause analyzer warnings (the built-in IDE and CA analyzers, or third-party analyzers a consumer enables) because of the indent mismatch between the generator's own lines and the verbatim column-0 C#. The owner's words (review of 2026-10-14): "Please take care to make sure that any #pragma directives in the generated types are excluded from traditional analyzers (or custom analyzers enabled by the consumer) so that the indent mismatch (between the generated type and the verbatim C# blocks) does not cause warnings." The owner then chose the mechanism (2026-10-14, answering the spec's question): [S54](#26-spec-decisions-for-11) relies on the existing `// <auto-generated>` header and `.g.cs` hint names, which make Roslyn treat the file as generated code so analyzers that exclude generated code skip it, **proves** it with a new `StrictAnalyzers` package consumer ([8.7](#87-generated-code-and-consumer-analyzers), [11.4](#114-package-validation)), and emits no `#pragma warning disable`, because a blanket one would hide real compiler warnings from template C#, which must keep surfacing at the template location. S54 and its no-pragma rule are the owner's choice. The residual gap, analyzers that opt into generated code anyway, is accepted as documented ([O20](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)). |
| O15 | `//` in embedded C# | Confirmed S31: no specific handling of `//` anywhere in the C# code. The C# is always used verbatim, so `[[ x // note ]]` fails to compile, and the docs say so. |
| O16 | Encoding and culture live only in `TemplateOptions` | Remove the `#encode` directive entirely, and remove the static `TemplateOptions` instances (`Html`, `Unencoded`, `JavaScript`, `Url`). Encoding is chosen only through `TemplateOptions.Encoder`, which stays `HtmlEncoder.Default` unless the options set it. Culture is a `TemplateOptions` property too (`Culture`, `CultureInfo.InvariantCulture` unless the options set it) and works independently of the encoder. This settles S43 as an owner decision and withdraws S47: there's no preset for explicit options to replace, so a template created with options gets exactly those options. |
| O17 | Directive values aren't validated | S40 is overturned. Remove all Roslyn validation of directive values: `#model`, `#using`, `#namespace`, and `#type` values (given or derived) are emitted unvalidated, and the compiler surfaces the errors. The 1.1 goal of scaling back C# parsing and complexity extends to directive parsing and must show significant code removal in `DirectiveReader` and related code ([6.7](#67-simplification-targets)). The owner also chose (2026-10-14, answering the spec's questions) that SLAP001 and SLAP002, which only reported failed name checks, are **retired** and that SLAP011 stays. The retirement details are spec decisions: SLAP001 and SLAP002 are **retired** (their headings stay in `docs/diagnostics.md` marked "Retired in 1.1", and the IDs are never reused, [S51](#26-spec-decisions-for-11)); SLAP011 stays as it is; and SLAP007 stays only for values AnglePlates defines (`#generate`, `#access`), blank values, and an empty derived `#type` ([S49](#26-spec-decisions-for-11)). |
| O18 | Commits | Agents are allowed to commit and push. The permission is no longer scoped to "while working through the release spec". S41 is resolved and STOP Q is gone. |
| O19 | Credentials, issues, and tracking | No agent credential is specified at this time, and no Issues read/write permission is given. Agents don't file GitHub issues (S36 withdrawn). The spec can be implemented in multiple commits, and tracking is done in this spec's [implementation checklist](#implementation-checklist), not on GitHub. The `nuget-org` guardrails stay: agents never approve a deployment and never change `NUGET_ORG_PUBLISH_ENABLED`. |
| O20 | Q1 and Q5 answered | Every Q1 item is approved as implemented and documented: S25 and S26, every remaining 1.1 spec decision, S48 to S53 as the spec wrote them, and S54's residual gap accepted as documented. The deferred items (Q5) are dropped as out of scope. |

### 2.4 Emitter layout E1 (resolved by O9)

**E1. How C# lines are laid out in the generated code. Status: resolved by O9 as layout C0 ([S39](#26-spec-decisions-for-11)), approved by the owner in [O14](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11). Nothing waits on the owner here.**

The owner's rule (O9): split the embedded C# on line breaks and emit every line "with the same initial indent as the generated type", keeping its line breaks, with exact `#line` mappings. The class writer emits a file-scoped namespace (`TemplateClassWriter` writes `namespace {Namespace};`), so the generated type sits at **column 0**. The literal reading is therefore:

> **C0.** Each C# block and each C# variable token is written with every line, the first included, at the generated type's indent, which is column 0. Each line keeps its own leading whitespace and its original line break (CR, LF, or CRLF). The class writer implements this by setting `_writer.Indent = 0` around the token's own lines and restoring the previous indent afterward. The `#line` character offset is 0 for a C# block and the call-prefix length (for example `Write(`, 6 characters) for a C# variable, so every line maps to its exact template column.

Why C0 satisfies every constraint at once. Measurements with Roslyn 4.14.0 and the .NET 10.0.402 SDK ([8.2](#82-what-line-does-on-continuation-lines)) show that a `#line` span directive applies its character offset only to the first generated line, and later lines report their generated column unchanged. Under C0 every continuation line sits at the same column it has in the template, so the reported columns are exact. Copying lines unchanged keeps multi-line verbatim, interpolated, and raw string content byte for byte. And nothing reads the C#.

Alternatives considered and rejected, for the record (O14 closed the choice):

| Option | Layout | Why it isn't the rule |
| --- | --- | --- |
| B | First line at the render method's statement indent (8 spaces), later lines copied as written | Exact columns and unchanged strings, but the first line isn't at the type's indent, so it doesn't match O9's wording. |
| A | Every line at the statement indent | Columns on lines 2 and later shift right by 8, which breaks AGENTS.md's exact `#line` rule, and every continuation line of a multi-line `@"..."` string gains 8 spaces of output. |
| Per-line `#line` | One `#line` per C# line | The directive text lands inside multi-line strings (CS1010, CS1002, CS8999), interpolation holes (CS1056), and `/* */` comments. Avoiding that needs C# awareness. |

What C0 means for the work: implement [8.3](#83-the-layout-rule) and [8.4](#84-c-variables) as written. AGENTS.md's `#line` bullet doesn't change. Snapshots show C# lines at column 0 and the generator's own statements at the method-body indent. The mixed indentation never reaches a consumer's analyzers, because the generated file is marked as generated code ([8.7](#87-generated-code-and-consumer-analyzers), O14).
