### 2.6 Spec decisions for 1.1

These follow from O1 to O19. The owner can overturn any of them, except where the 1.1 status says the owner already decided it. Revision 3 marks S31, S39, and S43 **owner-approved** (O15, O14, O16), S40 **overturned** (O17), and S36, S41, and S47 **withdrawn** (O19, O18, O16); their rows stay so the reasoning is on record. S48 to S54 are new in revision 3 and follow from O14 to O19.

| # | Topic | Decision | Section |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| S27 | Opener details | The marker check happens on the character right after the run of `[`. A trim marker belongs after the full opener (`[[[%-`), so `[[-x]]` is a level-2 C# variable with a trim marker, never a level-2 opener followed by `-x`. A `[` run at the very end of the text is literal when `N == 1`, and an unclosed C# variable when `N >= 2`. | [7.2](#72-the-algorithm) |
| S28 | Unclosed blocks | When the closer for the opener's level never appears, the parser reports SLAP004 with the opener as its span (the last `N` brackets plus the `%` or `*` marker; not the extra literal brackets, not the trim marker), `{0}` set to the level's closer (`]]`, `]]]`, `]]]]`, `%]` to `%]]]]`, `*]` to `*]]]]`), and consumes the rest of the template, as 1.0 did. A closer of a different level doesn't count (`[[[ x ]]` reports `]]]`). Over-limit runs and level mismatches get no new diagnostic. | [5.1](#51-rules), [7.2](#72-the-algorithm) |
| S29 | Roslyn-free parsing | Delete `Parsing/CSharpBlockScanner.cs` and `Parsing/TemplateReader.cs`. The parser works on `(string text, int position)` with `IndexOf`. Nothing in `src/AnglePlates/Parsing` or the class writer's C# emission path uses `SyntaxFactory.ParseTokens`, `SyntaxKind`, or any lexer. `Microsoft.CodeAnalysis.CSharp` stays referenced, because `TemplateGenerator` (`CSharpParseOptions`, `LanguageVersion`), `GeneratorSettings`, and `SyntaxFactory.Literal` in the class writer still use it. After [O17](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11), `DirectiveReader` doesn't. | [6.7](#67-simplification-targets), [7](#7-the-bracket-level-parser) |
| S30 | Emitter | No brace-based indentation, no lasting indent changes inside the token loop (only the temporary `Indent = 0` around each C# token's own lines), no comment detection. The writer copies each C# token under one `#line` mapping, every line at column 0 ([S39](#26-spec-decisions-for-11)). | [8](#8-emitting-c-without-reading-it) |
| S31 | `//` at the end of a C# variable | **Owner-approved ([O15](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)).** Not special-cased. `[[ x // note ]]` comments out the generated `, writer);` and fails to compile. Which errors appear depends on what follows the call (measured with the .NET 10 SDK): if the next generated token is `}`, CS1026 ("`)` expected") and CS1002 map to the template; if another statement follows (a literal, another write, or `await FlushAsync` in `RenderAsync`), CS1003 ("Syntax error, ',' expected") maps to the template and CS1026 lands in the generated file. Docs say it fails to compile, that an error may point into the generated file, and to use `/* */` or no comment. The rejected alternative (always put the call's end on its own line, which makes `//` work with no detection but makes every `Write` call two lines) stays rejected: the owner wants the C# used verbatim with no `//` handling anywhere. | [8.4](#84-c-variables) |
| S32 | `ScannerParity` | Delete the consumer. Its reason to exist (the host compiler's lexer decided block ends) is gone. Replace it with a small `DelimiterLevels` consumer that renders bracket levels, extra brackets, first-exact-match closers, former escapes, and multi-line C# through the packed analyzer on both SDK legs. | [11.4](#114-package-validation), [Appendix B](#appendix-b-delimiterlevels-consumer-template) |
| S33 | Writing an opener as text | Documented as a C# variable holding the text (`[[ "[[" ]]`, `[[[ "[[table]]" ]]]`) or `[% WriteRaw("[[", writer); %]`. Measured on .NET 10: `HtmlEncoder.Default` and `JavaScriptEncoder.Default` leave `[[`, `]]`, and `%]` unchanged; `UrlEncoder.Default` gives `%5B%5B`, `%5D%5D`, and `%25%5D`. The docs say to use `WriteRaw` when the options' `Encoder` is `UrlEncoder.Default`, and a Runtime.Tests row, `SyntaxTests.LiteralOpener_UnderOtherEncoders_WritesEncodedOpener`, pins these strings through options ([O16](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11) moved it there from `BlockSyntaxTests`, which can't set an encoder). | [4.1](#writing-openers-as-text) |
| S34 | Phase numbering | Phases 0 to 7 keep their 1.0 numbers as history. Phase 8 (owner inputs and int rehearsal) and Phase 9 (stable release) keep their numbers and meanings, so `AGENTS.md`, `release.yml`, and `docs/releasing.md` need no phase edits now. The 1.1 work is Phases B0 to B6 ("B" for bracket levels), which run before Phase 8. The 1.1.0 release-prep pull request removes every committed phase citation when it deletes `plans/`. | [18](#18-execution-plan) |
| S35 | One pull request | The 1.1 work lands as one pull request with one consolidated snapshot review. It also carries the consolidated review that 1.0's Phase 4 never recorded, so the owner reviews every current snapshot once: the checklist covers every file in `tests/AnglePlates.Tests/Snapshots` (added, changed, deleted, or unchanged, including the eight support-type files, `UnclosedBlock.verified.txt`, and `DirectiveErrors.verified.txt`), each with a one-line rationale. | [18](#18-execution-plan) |
| S36 | Deferred-work issues | **Withdrawn ([O19](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)).** Revision 2 had the agent file the deferred items as GitHub issues with `gh`. Agents don't file issues; the deferred items stay listed in [Deferred past 1.1](#deferred-past-11), and Q5 is the owner's again. | [Deferred past 1.1](#deferred-past-11) |
| S37 | "pre-1.0" wording | No 1.0 release will exist (O8), so every "pre-1.0" and "1.0" reference that means the prototype or the 1.0 plan goes. `SupportTypes.cs`'s two comments above the conversion helper (`ToInvariantString` at `6d59e69`, in both base classes) lose "pre-1.0"; because [S45](#26-spec-decisions-for-11) rewrites that helper to take the culture, the first sentence becomes "Works like Convert.ToString(value, culture), which the prototype called with the invariant culture: IConvertible first, then IFormattable, then ToString." (the rest of the comment unchanged). This comment is emitted into every consumer's support types, and the four support-type snapshots that contain it (`SupportTypes.internal#TextTemplate`, `SupportTypes.internal#Utf8Template`, `SupportTypes.public#TextTemplate`, `SupportTypes.public#Utf8Template`) change in the Phase B2 batch together with the S45 culture changes; their checklist rationale is "culture conversion (S43 to S45) and comment wording (S37)". The two `TemplateOptions` snapshots also lose the four presets ([O16](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)), in the same batch. `ConversionTests.cs`: "captured from the pre-1.0 conversion" becomes "captured from the prototype's conversion", "pin the 1.0 overload set" becomes "pin the overload set", and "as Convert.ToString did before 1.0" becomes "as the prototype's Convert.ToString did". `OutputTests.cs`: "Captured from the pre-1.0 code" becomes "Captured from the prototype's code". The test method `Render_ImplicitStringConversion_UsesToStringLikePre10` is renamed `Render_ImplicitStringConversion_UsesToStringLikePrototype`. (The 1.0-era choice to keep "pre-1.0" comments to avoid snapshot churn is overturned here, because S35 already puts every support-type snapshot in front of the owner.) | [16.8](#168-version-wording) |
| S38 | Simplification is acceptance | The size and grep targets in [6.7](#67-simplification-targets) are Phase B5 acceptance criteria, and the pull request reports before and after line counts. | [6.7](#67-simplification-targets) |
| S39 | Emitter layout (E1) | **Owner-approved ([O14](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)).** O9 read literally: each C# block and C# variable token is split on CR, LF, and CRLF (or copied whole, which gives the same bytes), and every line is written at the generated type's indent, column 0 under the file-scoped namespace, with its original line break. `#line` offsets are 0 for blocks and the call-prefix length for variables, so every line maps exactly. The owner's added requirement, no analyzer warnings from the mixed indentation, is met by generated-code detection, not by a pragma ([8.7](#87-generated-code-and-consumer-analyzers)). | [2.4](#24-emitter-layout-e1-resolved-by-o9), [8](#8-emitting-c-without-reading-it) |
| S40 | Directive values | **Overturned ([O17](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)).** The owner wants directive parsing scaled back like C# parsing, so directive values are emitted unvalidated and the compiler reports what's wrong; [4.2](#42-directives) and [S49](#26-spec-decisions-for-11) say what's still checked. Revision 2's decision, for the record: Directive values (`#model`, `#using`, `#namespace`) aren't embedded C# blocks. `DirectiveReader` keeps its Roslyn name and type validation, including the whitespace-only trivia check, so a bad directive reports SLAP007 instead of producing broken generated code. The owner can overturn this, in which case directive values are emitted unvalidated and errors surface as compiler errors. | [6.2](#62-class-responsibilities) |
| S41 | Commit permission after release prep | **Withdrawn; resolved by [O18](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11).** Revision 2 kept AGENTS.md's "while working through the release spec" scope and had release prep wait for the owner's wording. The owner allows agents to commit and push without that scope, so Phase B0 changes the Commits bullet to "Agents may commit and push to the remote repository as they work. Only the owner makes the repository public or approves a nuget.org publish." and release prep has nothing to reword there. | [Phase B0](#phase-b0-baselines-and-housekeeping), [15.4](#154-documentation-that-changes-with-11) |
| S42 | Prototype-relative CHANGELOG entries | The CHANGELOG keeps prototype-relative Removed and Changed entries (backslash escapes, brace re-indentation, the new meaning of `[[%` and `[[*`), because the 0.1 prototype was tried from source. Everywhere else, docs describe only the 1.1 behavior. The owner can drop these entries under Q2. | [4.1](#breaking-changes-against-the-01-prototype), [15.4](#154-documentation-that-changes-with-11) |
| S43 | Culture API (O11) | **Owner-approved ([O16](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)). A property on `TemplateOptions`, not a render argument.** The support types emit `public global::System.Globalization.CultureInfo Culture { get; init; }`, defaulting to `global::System.Globalization.CultureInfo.InvariantCulture`, independent of `Encoder`. The short rationale and the rejected alternatives are in [4.11](#411-culture). | [4.11](#411-culture) |
| S44 | Null culture | Setting `Culture` to `null` in an object initializer throws `ArgumentNullException` with parameter name `Culture`, from the `init` accessor, through a private backing field. `null` doesn't mean "invariant" or "current culture": `Encoder = null` has a meaning (no encoding), but a null culture has none, and silently mapping it to the invariant culture would hide a bug such as an unset field. The property type stays non-nullable, so a nullable-aware caller gets a compiler warning first. | [4.11](#411-culture) |
| S45 | Where the culture applies | Every string operation that converts a value: the conversion helper behind `Write<T>(T)` in both `TextTemplate<TModel>` and `Utf8Template<TModel>` uses `IConvertible.ToString(Options.Culture)` and `IFormattable.ToString(null, Options.Culture)`, and keeps the plain `ToString()` fallback unchanged; the nullable `Write<T>(T?)` overloads and the `WriteAsync` overloads reach the same helper; `RenderToString` creates its `StringWriter` with `template.Options.Culture`. Strings, `NullVariable`, `WriteRaw`, literal text, and the encoders are unaffected. (Revision 2's "the presets stay invariant" is gone with the presets, [O16](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11); `new TemplateOptions()` is invariant.) C# in a template that calls `ToString()` or `writer.Write(1.5)` itself isn't covered; the docs say so and show `Options.Culture`. | [4.11](#411-culture) |
| S46 | Docs voice: name the mechanism (O12) | AGENTS.md's Author Voice gains a bullet, written by the implementer in Phase B4 as a new bullet at the end of the section, after the paragraph that starts "Keep the writing conversational": "When prose describes a default, name the exact directive, option, property, parameter, or MSBuild setting that changes it; don't write 'unless you say otherwise', 'unless you set it', or a bare 'by default'." It's a writing rule, so `.editorconfig` can't express it (the CLAUDE.md rule about mirroring formatting rules in `.editorconfig` doesn't apply). The B4 docs pass fixes the offenders in [15.7](#157-defaults-name-their-mechanism), every new 1.1 sentence the spec dictates follows it, and a Phase B5 grep checks it with judged exceptions. | [15.7](#157-defaults-name-their-mechanism) |
| S47 | No options copy helper | **Withdrawn ([O16](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)).** Revision 2 warned that options passed to a constructor replace the preset `#encode` picks, so setting `Culture` meant copying `Encoder` and `NullVariable` from `Default.Options`. With no `#encode` and no presets, a template created with options gets exactly those options, and a template created without them gets `new TemplateOptions()`, so the trap, its docs warning, and its Runtime.Tests row are gone. `TemplateOptions` still has no copy method (no `WithCulture` and no `with` support) in 1.1; one would be additive. | [4.11](#411-culture) |
| S48 | Constructor fallback | The generated constructor becomes `: base(options ?? new global::SlusserLabs.AnglePlates.TemplateOptions())`. Why this one: it's a one-line change in `TemplateClassWriter`; the base classes keep their `ThrowIfNull`; no shared static state remains; `Default` is already one shared instance per template, so only `new X()` without options allocates one small immutable object, as the 0.1 prototype did. Rejected: an `internal static` default on `TemplateOptions` (visible to consumer code in the same assembly, so a de facto preset); a private static in each generic base class (one copy per `TModel`, and an extra member); moving the `??` into the base constructors (changes support-type constructor semantics and snapshots for nothing). | [4.5](#45-generated-template-class) |
| S49 | What `DirectiveReader` still checks | After O17, a directive value causes a generator diagnostic only when AnglePlates itself defines it or when it's blank: SLAP007 for a blank value (empty after trimming) of any directive, for `#generate` other than `Text` or `Utf8`, and for `#access` other than `public` or `internal` (both case-insensitive, as today). One more SLAP007 case guards derivation: a derived `#type` that comes out empty (a file named `.html` or `-.html`) reports SLAP007 at line 1 with its own `{2}` label, so the message is exactly "Directive '#type' has invalid value ''; expected a file name that gives a class name; add #type". A test pins that text, and the label is listed under Q3. It's a length check, not C# parsing, and without it the class would silently be named `TextTemplate` (and, in namespace `SlusserLabs.AnglePlates`, its hint name would equal a support type's, so `AddSource` would throw and fail the whole generator with CS8785). A given `#type` with a `.` can't collide that way, because [S50](#26-spec-decisions-for-11) sanitizes the type part's dots. SLAP003, SLAP005, SLAP006, SLAP008, and SLAP011 are unchanged. `{2}` keeps today's labels ("a C# identifier", "a C# type name", "a namespace name such as 'MyApp.Templates'", "a namespace name such as 'System.Linq'") for blank values, so `DirectiveErrors.verified.txt` doesn't change; the owner can reword them under Q3. | [4.2](#42-directives), [5.1](#51-rules) |
| S50 | Hint names without Roslyn | `TemplateGenerator.CreateHintPrefix` stops calling `SyntaxFactory.ParseName`. That call wasn't validation: it stripped a verbatim `@` and decoded `\uXXXX` so hint names stayed clean, and it was safe only because the namespace was pre-validated. Unvalidated values can hold characters `AddSource` rejects (for example `<` and `>` from `#type Foo<T>`, `:` from `#namespace global::X` or a `C:` full-path fallback, `"`, `*`, `?`, `|`), and an `ArgumentException` from `AddSource` fails the generator for every template (CS8785). So the `{Namespace}.{Type}{Target}Template` prefix is sanitized with plain string work, one part at a time: `Sanitize(Namespace, keepDots: true) + "." + Sanitize(Type, keepDots: false) + target + "Template"`, where every character that isn't `char.IsLetterOrDigit` or `_` (or `.`, in the namespace) becomes `_`. A `.` in `#type` becomes `_` so a template can't produce a support type's hint name: unvalidated `#namespace SlusserLabs` with `#type AnglePlates.` would otherwise give `SlusserLabs.AnglePlates.TextTemplate.g.cs`, the same as the support type, and `FindDuplicates` numbers only template prefixes, so `AddSource` would throw (CS8785); with per-part sanitizing it gives `SlusserLabs.AnglePlates_TextTemplate.g.cs`. Sanitizing happens before `FindDuplicates` groups prefixes, so two templates that sanitize to the same name are still numbered. Hint names only name files; the class and namespace in the source are the unsanitized values. | [4.9](#49-generated-file-names) |
| S51 | Retired rules and AnalyzerReleases | An ID retired before any release has no AnalyzerReleases entry at all: SLAP001 and SLAP002 are deleted from `AnalyzerReleases.Unshipped.md`, and nothing goes under "Removed Rules", which is only for IDs that appeared in a shipped release (RS2003 otherwise). Their `## SLAP001` and `## SLAP002` headings stay in `docs/diagnostics.md` with "**Retired in 1.1.**" and a one-sentence reason, and the IDs are never reused. | [5.3](#53-help-links) |
| S52 | No mapping for directive values | Directive values get no `#line` mapping, and no new mapping machinery is added. An error in a `#model`, `#using`, `#namespace`, or `#type` value lands on the generated file's `using`, `namespace`, or class line. Mapping `#using` and `#namespace` would be simple, but `#model` appears mid-line in four places (the base type, the `Render` and `RenderAsync` parameters, and the `Model` local), and the owner wants less complexity. The docs say where such errors show up and that the fix is in the directive. | [4.2](#42-directives) |
| S53 | A leftover `#encode` line | No special detection. After O16 an `#encode ...` line is an ordinary non-directive line: after other directives it ends the directive section (so later directives become body text, and a missing `#model` reports SLAP003); as the first line, the file isn't a template (skipped quietly when discovered, SLAP008 when added explicitly). SLAP011 doesn't fire, because "encode" isn't near any directive name. A `DirectiveReaderTests` row pins it; the CHANGELOG doesn't mention `#encode` at all, because the prototype never had it ([15.4](#154-documentation-that-changes-with-11)); that's consistent with O10 and the simplification goal. | [4.2](#42-directives) |
| S54 | Generated-code detection is a contract | Every generated file keeps the `// <auto-generated>` header as its first line (a UTF-8 BOM before it is fine) and every hint name keeps the `.g.cs` suffix. Either one makes Roslyn treat the file as generated code; keeping both is how the spec meets O14. No `#pragma warning disable` is emitted. The owner chose this mechanism (header and proof, no pragma) on 2026-10-14 ([O14](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)); the narrower per-span pragma option in [8.7](#87-generated-code-and-consumer-analyzers) was considered and not chosen; the residual gap is accepted as documented (O20). An analyzer that opts into generated code or never configures generated-code analysis isn't covered. A consumer who overrides detection (for example `[*.g.cs] generated_code = false` in `.editorconfig`) isn't supported and isn't guaranteed a warning-free build. | [8.7](#87-generated-code-and-consumer-analyzers) |

## 3. Where the project stands

As of 2026-10-14, checked live:

- `main` is `6d59e69`, the merge of pull request #1. The local and remote `release-1.0` branch (`bb3294e`) has a tree identical to `main`. Deleting `release-1.0` is the owner's choice; agents don't delete it.
- CI run 38627910273 (the push to `main`) passed on all four legs. The pull request's run 38621469699 recorded 765 generator tests and 315 runtime tests per full leg, generator branch coverage 99.50% and emitted-code branch coverage 99.73%, and Native AOT executables of 1,752,064 bytes (Windows), 1,948,024 bytes (`ubuntu`), and 1,968,544 bytes (`sdk-floor`).
- The repository is private. It has no secrets, no variables, no environments, no rulesets (the rulesets API returns 403 on a private repo without a paid plan), and private vulnerability reporting returns 404. There's no `.github/CODEOWNERS`.
- `release.yml` is registered and active and **has never run**.
- `gh` is authenticated as the owner's own login (`jacobslusser`). No separate agent credential is planned for now ([O19](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)), so agents keep using the credentials already on the machine. **Because this login is a would-be reviewer, the agent must never dispatch the nuget.org login check unless the owner asks, touch environments, variables, rulesets, or repository settings, or approve anything,** even though the login technically could.
- No GitHub issues exist, and 1.1 files none ([O19](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)).
- The generator: `src/AnglePlates/**/*.cs` is 3,062 lines. `Directives/` is 556 of them: `DirectiveReader.cs` 352, `DirectiveSpellCheck.cs` 90, `TemplateDirectives.cs` 47, `DirectiveKindExtensions.cs` 31, `DirectiveKind.cs` 13, `GenerationTarget.cs` 12, `TemplateEncoding.cs` 11. `TemplateGenerator.cs` is 311, `Diagnostics/DiagnosticDescriptors.cs` 73, `Emit/SupportTypes.cs` 483. `Parsing/` is 809 of them: `CSharpBlockScanner.cs` 329, `TemplateParser.cs` 262, `TemplateReader.cs` 100, `TemplateSyntax.cs` 34, `ParsedTemplate.cs` 28, `Token.cs` 25, `DirectiveToken.cs` 21, `TokenType.cs` 10. `Emit/TemplateClassWriter.cs` is 449. Related tests: `CSharpBlockScannerTests.cs` 189, `TemplateParserTests.cs` 145.
- What 1.1 replaces: the Roslyn-lexer scanner (`CSharpBlockScanner`, called from `TemplateParser.TryParseBlock`, `TemplateClassWriter.WriteVariable`, and `TemplateClassWriter.WriteCodeBlock`), backslash escapes (`TemplateParser.TryParseText`, `GetOpenerLength`, `CountBackslashes`), the `ScannerParity` consumer, and every doc passage that describes them. What 1.1 removes on the directive side: the `#encode` directive (`TemplateEncoding`, `DirectiveKind.Encode`, `DirectiveReader.ReadEncode`), the four `TemplateOptions` presets, the Roslyn checks in `DirectiveReader` (`SyntaxFacts.IsValidIdentifier`, `SyntaxFactory.ParseName`, `SyntaxFactory.ParseTypeName`, the trivia check, `FindNamespaceProblem`), SLAP001 and SLAP002, and `SyntaxFactory.ParseName` in `TemplateGenerator.CreateHintPrefix`. `VersionPrefix` is still `1.0.0`.

The 1.0 spec's "state before Phase 0" list (commit `8410d49`) is history and isn't repeated. Its eight problems were fixed in 1.0 Phases 0 to 7, except item 3 ("closers inside C# end blocks early"), which is **No longer in scope (superseded by 1.1)**: first-exact-match closing is the intended rule again, and authors raise the bracket level.

## 4. Consumer-facing design

This section is the 1.1.0 consumer contract. [4.1](#41-template-syntax) and [4.11](#411-culture) are new in 1.1. [4.2](#42-directives) to [4.10](#410-generated-header) were implemented in 1.0 and stay binding; they're summarized, with 1.1 changes called out. [4.2](#42-directives) and [4.3](#43-templateoptions) change the most ([O16](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11), [O17](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)).

### 4.1 Template syntax

#### The blocks

| Syntax | Levels | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Literal text | n/a | Written unchanged, byte for byte. That includes a single `[` not followed by `%` or `*`, any `]`, any backslash, and the extra brackets of an over-limit run. |
| `[[ expr ]]`, `[[[ expr ]]]`, `[[[[ expr ]]]]` | 2 to 4 | C# variable. The value is converted to a string with `TemplateOptions.Culture` (`CultureInfo.InvariantCulture` unless the options set `Culture`, [4.11](#411-culture)), encoded, and written. |
| `[% statements %]` through `[[[[% statements %]]]]` | 1 to 4 | C# block. Copied into the render method ([8](#8-emitting-c-without-reading-it)). |
| `[* text *]` through `[[[[* text *]]]]` | 1 to 4 | Comment. Not written. |
| `-` right after the full opener or right before the full closer | all | Trim marker. Removes the adjacent literal whitespace (`[ \t\r\n]+`). |

The **bracket level** of a block is the number of `[` in its opener. Its closer repeats `]` the same number of times: a level-3 C# variable is `[[[ ... ]]]`, a level-2 C# block is `[[% ... %]]`, a level-4 comment is `[[[[* ... *]]]]`. Blocks of different levels behave identically.

| Kind | Level 1 | Level 2 | Level 3 | Level 4 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| C# variable | (not a block) | `[[` ... `]]` | `[[[` ... `]]]` | `[[[[` ... `]]]]` |
| C# block | `[%` ... `%]` | `[[%` ... `%]]` | `[[[%` ... `%]]]` | `[[[[%` ... `%]]]]` |
| Comment | `[*` ... `*]` | `[[*` ... `*]]` | `[[[*` ... `*]]]` | `[[[[*` ... `*]]]]` |

#### How an opener is recognized

1. At a `[`, count the whole run of consecutive `[`; call it `k`. The level is `N = min(k, 4)`. The first `k - N` brackets are literal text.
2. Look at the character right after the run. `%` makes a C# block of level `N`. `*` makes a comment of level `N`. Anything else (including the end of the text) makes a C# variable when `N >= 2`.
3. A single `[` (`N == 1`) not followed by `%` or `*` is literal text, and the search continues after it.
4. If the next character after the opener (after the `%` or `*` for blocks and comments) is `-`, it's a trim marker.

#### How a block ends

The closer is built from the kind and the level: `]` times `N` for a C# variable, `%` plus `]` times `N` for a C# block, `*` plus `]` times `N` for a comment. The block ends at the **first exact occurrence** of that string after the opener (after the trim marker, if there is one). Nothing inside the block is read. A `-` right before the closer is a trim marker.

There's no C# awareness: a closer inside a C# string, character literal, comment, or indexer ends the block. When your C# contains the closer for its level, raise the level until it doesn't.

If the closer never appears, the template reports SLAP004 at the opener, and the rest of the template is consumed ([5.1](#51-rules)).

#### Worked examples

| Template | Result |
| --- | --- |
| `[[ model.Message ]]` and `[[[[ model.Message ]]]]` | Identical: one C# variable. |
| `[* A comment. *]` and `[[[* A comment. *]]]` | Identical: nothing written. |
| `[[ a[b[0]] ]]` | Ends at the first `]]`, after `0`. The C# is `a[b[0`, a compiler error at the template. Write `[[[ a[b[0]] ]]]`. |
| `[[ model[0..1]]]` | Ends at the first `]]`, leaving `model[0..1` and a literal `]`. Write `[[[ model[0..1] ]]]`. |
| `[% var s = "%]"; %]` | Ends inside the string; the C# is `var s = "`, a compiler error. Write `[[% var s = "%]"; %]]`. |
| `[[[ "]]" ]]]` | Writes `]]`. |
| `[[[* hidden [[ x ]] and *]] *]]]` | One comment; `*]]` isn't its closer. |
| `[[ x ]]]` | A C# variable `x` followed by a literal `]`. |
| `[[[[[ model ]]]]]` | Literal `[`, a level-4 C# variable, literal `]`. |
| `x[[[ i ]]]` | A level-3 C# variable; no bracket is written. A literal `[` right before an opener raises the level. Write `x[ [[ i ]] ]` or `x[[ "[" ]][[ i ]][[ "]" ]]` to keep the brackets. |
| `[a][b][ c ]` | All literal text. |
| `[[*ptr ]]` | Opens a level-2 comment that needs `*]]`; without one later in the file it reports SLAP004, and with one it silently swallows text. Write `[[ *ptr ]]`. |
| `[[ count--]]` | Trims and leaves `count-`, as in 1.0. Write `[[ count-- ]]`. |
| `[[-1]]` | Trims and writes `1`, as in 1.0. Write `[[ -1 ]]`. |
| `[[[- x -]]]`, `[[%- x; -%]]` | Trim markers at level 3 and level 2. |
| `[% // note %]tail` | Works: the block is `// note` and its line ends before `tail`'s code. |
| `a[% #if DEBUG %]b[% #endif %]c` | Works, as in 1.0: each block lands on its own generated line. |
| `[[ model // note ]]` | Fails to compile: the comment swallows the generated call's end (S31). Which errors appear depends on what follows the call: if the next generated token is `}`, CS1026 and CS1002 map to the template; if another statement follows (a literal, another write, or `await FlushAsync` in `RenderAsync`), CS1003 maps to the template and CS1026 (`) expected`) lands in the generated file. Use `[[ model /* note */ ]]`. |
| `\[[ model ]]` | A literal backslash followed by the C# variable. |
| `C:\out\[[ model.Name ]]` | Writes `C:\out\` and the value, as written. |

#### Writing openers as text

There are no escapes. Closers in literal text (`]]`, `%]`, `*]`) are already plain text, and so is a single `[` that isn't followed by `%` or `*`. To write an opener sequence as text, put it in a C# variable or write it raw:

- `const grid = [[ "[[" ]]1, 2], [3, 4]];` writes `const grid = [[1, 2], [3, 4]];`.
- `[[[ "[[table]]" ]]]` writes `[[table]]` (level 3, because the string contains `]]`).
- `[% WriteRaw("[[", writer); %]` writes `[[` with no encoding at all.
- Markdown `[*Important*](https://example.com)` opens a comment. Write `[[ "[" ]]*Important*](https://example.com)`.

Measured on .NET 10: `HtmlEncoder.Default` (the default) and `JavaScriptEncoder.Default` leave `[[`, `]]`, and `%]` unchanged. `UrlEncoder.Default` writes `[[` as `%5B%5B`, `]]` as `%5D%5D`, and `%]` as `%25%5D`, so when the options' `Encoder` is `UrlEncoder.Default`, use `WriteRaw` (S33).

#### Breaking changes against the 0.1 prototype

These go in the CHANGELOG ([15.4](#154-documentation-that-changes-with-11)), under spec decision [S42](#26-spec-decisions-for-11):

- Openers and closers can repeat their brackets up to four times.
- `[[%` and `[[*` now open level-2 C# blocks and comments. They used to open a C# variable whose C# started with `%` or `*`, so `[[*ptr ]]` (a pointer dereference) must now be written `[[ *ptr ]]`.
- A literal `[` right before an opener raises its level.
- Runs of more than four `[` keep the extras as literal text.
- Backslash escapes are gone. `\[[ x ]]` writes a backslash and then `x`.
- Generated C# blocks are no longer re-indented around braces.

The prototype had no `#encode` directive, no presets, and no Roslyn validation of directive values (checked at `8410d49`), so their removal isn't a prototype-relative breaking change; the CHANGELOG simply doesn't list them as Added ([15.4](#154-documentation-that-changes-with-11)).

#### Superseded 1.0 syntax rules

Each of these 1.0 rules is **No longer in scope (superseded by 1.1).** The replacement is the first-exact-match rule above.

- "A closer inside a C# string, character literal, or `/* */` comment doesn't end the block."
- "In a C# variable, `]]` only ends the variable when every `[` inside it has its `]`. `[[ grid[row[0]] ]]` and `[[ model[0..1]]]` both work."
- "A `//` comment or a `#` preprocessor line ends at the first closer on that line." (It still behaves that way, trivially, because every block ends at its first closer.)
- "Broken C# ends at the first closer; a verbatim or raw string or `/*` comment that never ends gets the same rule only when fewer than 16 closers follow it."
- "If the scanner can't find a closer it accepts, SLAP004 at the opener, including C# that hides more than 15 closers; the fix is to refactor the C#."
- "Comments keep the plain text search." (Every block uses the plain search now.)
- The `\[[`, `\[%`, `\[*` escape row and backslash pairing ("Escaped opener. Backslashes before an opener pair up").
- Docs addition: "`[*` collides with Markdown emphasis; the escaping section shows `\[*Important*](…)`." Replaced by the variable form above; the warning itself stays.
- Docs addition: "A `//` comment as the last thing in a C# variable used to comment out the closing parenthesis; the writer now puts a line break before the `)`." Replaced by S31.

Docs additions that still apply: `[[ count--]]` trims (write `[[ count-- ]]`), and doc examples use `[% // note %]`, not a to-do comment, so the README's to-do check stays simple.

### 4.2 Directives

Directive parsing doesn't involve brackets, so the bracket-level work doesn't touch it. 1.1 changes the directives in two ways: `#encode` is removed ([O16](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)), and values are no longer validated with Roslyn ([O17](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)).

- Directives sit on consecutive lines at the top of the file, and a file is a template only when its first line is a known directive. Unchanged.
- `#model` is required. `#type` (default: the PascalCased file name up to its first `.`, split on `-`, `_`, and spaces), `#namespace` (default: `RootNamespace` plus the folder path), `#generate` (`Text` or `Utf8`; default both), `#access` (`public` or `internal`, case-insensitive, emitted lowercase), and repeatable `#using` are optional.
- **Values are copied, not validated** (1.1 change). The trimmed values of `#model`, `#type`, `#namespace`, and `#using` go into the generated code as they are: `using {value};` for each `#using`, `namespace {value};`, the class name `{type}{Target}Template`, and the model type in its four places (the base type, the `Render` and `RenderAsync` parameters, and the `Model` local). The compiler reports anything wrong with them, in the generated file, not at the directive ([S52](#26-spec-decisions-for-11)). What that allows and breaks, for the docs:
  - `#using` accepts anything C# accepts after `using`: `static System.Math`, an alias `Json = System.Text.Json`, or `global::MyApp.Models`. A trailing `;` gives `using System;;`, and a trailing `// note` comments out the `;`; both are compiler errors.
  - A trailing `// note` on `#model` breaks the class line; on `#namespace` it breaks the namespace line. Comments don't belong on directive lines.
  - Templates are trusted C# (AGENTS.md), and directive values are part of that trust: a value can inject code into the generated file. The docs say so in one sentence next to the directive table.
- **Derivation is plain string work** (1.1 change). `#type` is derived as before (`order-line.html` becomes `OrderLine`, `hello-world.html` becomes `HelloWorld`, `welcomeEmail.txt` becomes `WelcomeEmail`, `order.line.html` becomes `Order`), with no identifier check: `2fa-code.html` gives `2faCode`, and the generated class `2faCodeTextTemplate` is a compiler error in the generated file; the fix is still `#type`. `#namespace` is `RootNamespace` (trimmed) joined with the folders by `.`, with no checks: an empty `RootNamespace` gives `.Templates` (or `namespace ;`), a template outside the project folder gives something like `MyApp....Shared` (for `../Shared/x.html`, `string.Join(".", ["MyApp", "..", "Shared"])`; tests derive the exact string from that join), the full-path fallback gives `MyApp.C:.src`, and a folder such as `my-folder`, `class`, or `2024` gives an invalid name. Each is a compiler error in the generated file; the fix is still `#namespace`. The one exception is an **empty** derived `#type` (a file named `.html` or `-.html`), which reports SLAP007 at line 1 ([S49](#26-spec-decisions-for-11)).
- **What still reports a generator diagnostic** ([S49](#26-spec-decisions-for-11)): SLAP003 (no `#model`), SLAP005, SLAP006 (duplicate directive), SLAP007 (a blank value for any directive, an empty derived `#type`, `#generate` other than `Text` or `Utf8`, `#access` other than `public` or `internal`), SLAP008, and SLAP011. SLAP001 and SLAP002 are retired ([5.1](#51-rules)).
- **`#encode` is gone** (1.1 change). Pick an encoder with `TemplateOptions.Encoder` ([4.3](#43-templateoptions)). A leftover `#encode none` line is an ordinary non-directive line and gets no special detection ([S53](#26-spec-decisions-for-11)): after other directives it ends the directive section, and as the first line it means the file isn't a template.
- `RootNamespace` is compiler-visible through the props, and `TemplateContext` carries it, so changing it re-runs every template and nothing else. Unchanged.
- SLAP011 catches misspelled directives ([5.2](#52-slap011-rule)). Unchanged, apart from `encode` leaving the directive list.

The 1.0 rules "A derived class name that isn't a valid identifier reports SLAP001", "A namespace that can't be derived reports SLAP002", "`SyntaxFacts.IsValidIdentifier` does the check", "`#using` takes plain namespace names only", and "`#encode` (`html`, `none`, `javascript`, `url`; case-insensitive)" are **No longer in scope (superseded by 1.1).**

### 4.3 `TemplateOptions`

Binding from 1.0: an `internal` (or `public` with `AnglePlateBaseAccessModifier=public`) `sealed class TemplateOptions` with `TextEncoder? Encoder { get; init; } = HtmlEncoder.Default` and `string? NullVariable { get; init; }`. No `BufferPool`. Emitted code `global::`-qualifies every type, and its XML docs follow the `/csharp` layout and went through `/humanizer`.

**1.1 changes** ([O16](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)):

1. **No presets.** The static `Html`, `Unencoded`, `JavaScript`, and `Url` instances and their XML docs (including "Templates use these unless their <c>#encode</c> directive picks another preset.") are deleted. `TemplateOptions` is the only place to choose an encoder: `new TemplateOptions()` encodes HTML; `new TemplateOptions { Encoder = null }` writes C# variables unencoded; `new TemplateOptions { Encoder = JavaScriptEncoder.Default }` and `UrlEncoder.Default` replace the other presets. The 1.0 binding sentence "a consumer who wants a preset with a different `NullVariable` writes `new TemplateOptions { Encoder = null, NullVariable = "-" }`" keeps its code and loses the word "preset".
2. **`Culture`.** A third `init`-only property, `CultureInfo Culture`, defaulting to `CultureInfo.InvariantCulture` and throwing `ArgumentNullException` on `null` ([4.11](#411-culture), S43 to S45). It's declared after `Encoder` and before `NullVariable`, so the two properties that decide how a value becomes text sit together. It's independent of `Encoder`: setting one never changes the other.

A template created without options uses `new TemplateOptions()` ([S48](#26-spec-decisions-for-11), [4.5](#45-generated-template-class)), so every template's defaults are the same: HTML encoding, the invariant culture, and a null `NullVariable` (which writes nothing). The 1.0 rule "the constructor falls back to the preset `#encode` picks" is **No longer in scope (superseded by 1.1).**

### 4.4 Support base classes

Implemented in 1.0 and binding: `TextTemplate<TModel>` and `Utf8Template<TModel>` with `private protected` constructors, public `Options`, `Render` (and `RenderAsync` for UTF-8), `protected` `Write(string?)`, `WriteRaw`, `Write<T>(T)`, `Write<T>(T?) where T : struct`, and `private protected` `WriteLiteral`, `WriteLiteralAsync`, `WriteAsync` overloads, and `FlushAsync`. `private protected` works from async methods, lambdas, and `dynamic` arguments, which Runtime.Tests pins. The rationale for the unconstrained `Write<T>` and the nullable overload is S25. Binding from 1.0: the literal-bytes overload is named `WriteLiteralAsync` rather than `WriteAsync(ReadOnlySpan<byte>)`, so a `byte[]` C# variable never binds a span overload. **1.1 change**: no signature changes; the private conversion helper takes `Options.Culture` (S45), and the `Write<T>(T)` XML docs say "with `TemplateOptions.Culture`" instead of "with the invariant culture".

### 4.5 Generated template class

Implemented in 1.0 and binding, with four 1.1 changes. For `Templates/greeting.html` containing `#model MyApp.GreetingModel` and `<h1>Hello, [[ model.Name ]]!</h1>`, the shape is:

```csharp
// <auto-generated>
//     Generated by SlusserLabs.AnglePlates from Templates/greeting.html.
//     Changes to this file will be lost when the project builds.
// </auto-generated>

#nullable enable

namespace MyApp.Templates;

/// <summary>
/// A text template that renders 'Templates/greeting.html'.
/// </summary>
internal sealed partial class GreetingTextTemplate : global::SlusserLabs.AnglePlates.TextTemplate<MyApp.GreetingModel>
{
    /// ... constructor falling back to new TemplateOptions(), and static Default

    /// <inheritdoc />
    public override void Render(MyApp.GreetingModel model, global::System.IO.TextWriter writer)
    {
        global::System.ArgumentNullException.ThrowIfNull(writer);
        MyApp.GreetingModel Model = model; // Allows alternate casing

        WriteLiteral("<h1>Hello, ", writer);
#line (2, 15) - (2, 24) 6 "C:\src\MyApp\Templates\greeting.html"
Write(model.Name, writer);
#line default
        WriteLiteral("!</h1>", writer);
    }
}
```

The example shows the 1.1 layout ([S39](#26-spec-decisions-for-11)): the C# variable's call starts at column 0, the generated type's indent, so the `#line` offset is just the call-prefix length (6 for `Write(`). At `6d59e69` the call sat at the method indent and the offset was 14.

Binding rules: `sealed partial`; the constructor falls back to `new global::SlusserLabs.AnglePlates.TemplateOptions()` ([S48](#26-spec-decisions-for-11)); `ThrowIfNull(writer)` first and outside any `#line` (`model` isn't checked, because a nullable `#model` type is legal); `RenderAsync` is an `async` method, so a null writer there surfaces as a faulted `ValueTask` when awaited, not as a synchronous throw, and Runtime.Tests asserts it with `await Assert.ThrowsAsync<ArgumentNullException>(...)` (binding from 1.0); the `Model` alias; one `Write(expr, writer);` per C# variable with one `#line` whose 0-based character offset points at the first character of `expr` (the call-prefix length under S39, because the call starts at column 0); `WriteLiteral` (UTF-8 uses `"…"u8`); `RenderAsync` keeps the flush rule (the first write after each C# block is an awaited flushing write, and the method ends with `await FlushAsync(...)`, all with `ConfigureAwait(false)`); reserved local names `model`, `Model`, `writer`, and `cancellationToken`.

1.1 changes:

1. Literal runs merge across comments and zero-length blocks when no trim marker sits between them. "And escaped openers" is **No longer in scope (superseded by 1.1)**: the parser never produces two adjacent literal tokens anymore ([7.3](#73-invariants)).
2. C# blocks and C# variables are copied under one `#line` mapping each, every line at column 0, with no brace-based indentation ([8](#8-emitting-c-without-reading-it)). The generator's own statements stay at the method-body indent throughout the render method.
3. A C# variable's `#line` mapping uses the content span after the level-`N` opener and any trim marker. `Token.Span` already holds that span, so the writer needs no level.
4. The constructor is `: base(options ?? new global::SlusserLabs.AnglePlates.TemplateOptions())` instead of `: base(options ?? global::SlusserLabs.AnglePlates.TemplateOptions.{Encoding})` ([S48](#26-spec-decisions-for-11)). The writer's whole comment above `WriteConstructorAndDefault` (lines 124 and 125 at `6d59e69`: "The constructor falls back to the preset that #encode picked, so creating a template allocates no options, and Default is a shared instance that uses that preset") is replaced, because both of its later clauses become false (`new X()` now allocates, and there's no preset), for example with "// The constructor falls back to new default options, so every template starts with HTML encoding and the invariant culture, and Default is one shared instance created that way" (run it through `/humanizer`); the constructor's XML doc "or with the template's default options when none are given" stays (it's still true). `Default` stays one shared instance per template class. Directive values reach the generated code unvalidated ([4.2](#42-directives)), and the header and hint name keep the file marked as generated code ([S54](#26-spec-decisions-for-11)).

### 4.6 How C# variables convert

Implemented in 1.0 and binding, with one 1.1 change. A string binds `Write(string?)`, a nullable value type binds `Write<T>(T?)`, and everything else binds `Write<T>(T)`, which converts like `Convert.ToString(value, Options.Culture)` (`IConvertible.ToString(Options.Culture)`, then `IFormattable.ToString(null, Options.Culture)`, then `ToString()`), without boxing non-nullable value types. **1.1 change**: 1.0 always converted with `CultureInfo.InvariantCulture`; that's now only the default of `TemplateOptions.Culture` ([4.11](#411-culture)). The 1.0 wording "converts like `Convert.ToString(value, CultureInfo.InvariantCulture)`" is **No longer in scope (superseded by 1.1).** `null`, `default`, and a `ToString()` that returns `null` write `NullVariable`. `dynamic` binds at run time to the same overloads and stays AOT-hostile; the framework's own IL2026/IL3050 warnings stay unsuppressed. Ref structs fail to compile at the template location. The output-parity matrix in `ConversionTests` (`int`, `long`, `double` including `-1.5E+20`, `decimal`, `float`, `DateTime`, `DateTimeOffset`, `TimeSpan`, `Guid`, `DateOnly`, `TimeOnly`, `bool`, `char`, an enum, a `[Flags]` enum, `int?` with and without a value, a custom class, a `null`-returning `ToString`, and `dynamic`) stays as the regression guard, and with the default options it must produce exactly the same text as in 1.0.

### 4.7 `RenderToString`

Implemented in 1.0: `TemplateExtensions.RenderToString<TModel>(this TextTemplate<TModel>, TModel)` emitted with the support types and their access modifier. Callers need `using SlusserLabs.AnglePlates;`. No UTF-8 counterpart in 1.1.0. **1.1 change**: its `StringWriter` is created with `template.Options.Culture` instead of `CultureInfo.InvariantCulture` (S45), so C# in a template that writes a number straight to `writer` agrees with its C# variables. The signature doesn't change.

### 4.8 Child templates

Implemented in 1.0: no include syntax. The README and `docs/templates.md` show the partial-class pattern where the parent creates its child with `new(Options)`, both templates use `#generate Text`, and a test renders a parent with `new TemplateOptions { Encoder = null }` (1.0: `TemplateOptions.Unencoded`, gone with the presets) and checks the child is unencoded too. The note that a loop made only of C# blocks gets no flush check inside `RenderAsync` lives in `docs/rendering.md`. Binding from 1.0: both templates use `#generate Text` because `writer` is a `TextWriter` only in the text target, so a UTF-8 parent needs a `Utf8` child and its own partial half. 1.1 check: the `docs/templates.md` order example's C# blocks (`foreach` with braces, in `Templates/Readme/order.html`) still compile and render byte-identically after the emitter change; `ReadmeExampleTests` proves it. (The order example moved out of the README in 1.0 Phase 6; the README marks only `Templates/Readme/greeting.html`.) 1.1 addition: because the child is created with `new(Options)`, it inherits the parent's `Culture` along with its encoder, and a Runtime.Tests case proves it ([11.3](#113-runtime-tests)). A child called through `Default` keeps the invariant culture, the same trap `docs/rendering.md` already describes for the encoder.

### 4.9 Generated file names

Implemented in 1.0 and binding: hint names `{Namespace}.{Type}{Target}Template.g.cs` and `SlusserLabs.AnglePlates.{TemplateOptions,TextTemplate,Utf8Template,TemplateExtensions}.g.cs`; clashes grouped with `StringComparer.OrdinalIgnoreCase`, sorted by ordinal path, and numbered (`.2.g.cs`); duplicate classes are compiler errors (S21); no SHA-256 code. **1.1 change** ([S50](#26-spec-decisions-for-11)): the template prefix `{Namespace}.{Type}{Target}Template` is sanitized with plain string work, the namespace and the type separately (every character that isn't a letter, a digit, or `_` becomes `_`; the namespace also keeps `.`, the type doesn't, so `#namespace SlusserLabs` with `#type AnglePlates.` gives `SlusserLabs.AnglePlates_TextTemplate.g.cs` instead of a support type's name) before duplicates are grouped, instead of going through `SyntaxFactory.ParseName`. So `#namespace @class` gives `_class.Example…` (1.0: `class.Example…`) and `#namespace My.\u0041pp` gives `My._u0041pp.…` (1.0: `My.App.…`); the class and namespace inside the file are unchanged. Every hint name keeps its `.g.cs` suffix ([S54](#26-spec-decisions-for-11)).

### 4.10 Generated header

Implemented in 1.0 and binding: `// <auto-generated>` with "Generated by SlusserLabs.AnglePlates from <path>." and no version; support types omit the path; the path uses forward slashes and is escaped for line breaks and XML. **1.1 addition** ([O14](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11), [S54](#26-spec-decisions-for-11)): the header is now also a contract with consumer analyzers. It stays the first thing in every generated file (template classes and support types), so Roslyn treats the file as generated code ([8.7](#87-generated-code-and-consumer-analyzers)).

### 4.11 Culture

New in 1.1 ([O11](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)). Consumers pick the culture C# variables convert with; the default stays `CultureInfo.InvariantCulture`, so a template that doesn't ask for a culture renders exactly what 1.0 rendered on every machine.

#### Why a property

**Owner decision [O16](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11) (S43): the culture is a `TemplateOptions.Culture` property, independent of `Encoder`.** Revision 2's two-column comparison with a render argument is settled, so only the reasons stay:

- The culture decides how a value becomes output text, the same job as `Encoder`, and it lives in the immutable options the template already holds. Generated template code doesn't change; the conversion helper reads `Options.Culture`.
- Child templates created with the documented `_line ??= new(Options)` pattern inherit it automatically. A render argument would have to be forwarded by hand in every `[% Line.Render(line, writer); %]`, and forgetting it would compile and silently use the invariant culture.
- `Render`, `RenderAsync`, and `RenderToString` keep their signatures. A render argument would add a culture parameter to every generated `Write` call and every `protected` `Write` overload, and sit awkwardly next to `RenderAsync`'s optional `CancellationToken`.
- The cost: per-request localization needs one options object (and template instance) per culture instead of `Default`; the pattern is below. With no `#encode` and no presets, setting `Culture` never changes the encoder: `new TemplateOptions { Culture = c }` encodes HTML because `Encoder` defaults to `HtmlEncoder.Default`, exactly like `new TemplateOptions()`.

Rejected alternatives, for the record:

- **A culture argument to `Render`, `RenderAsync`, and `RenderToString`**, for the reasons above.
- **`TextWriter.FormatProvider`.** There's no UTF-8 equivalent (`IBufferWriter<byte>` and `PipeWriter` carry no culture), so the two targets would disagree. And `Console.Out` reports the current culture, so reading it would silently replace the invariant default with the server's culture.
- **A render-argument overload** (for example `Render(model, writer, culture)` beside the existing methods). Not needed now; it's additive and can come later without breaking anything.
- **`IFormatProvider` as the property type.** `CultureInfo` is what people reach for, it's what `StringWriter` and the conversion calls accept, and it reads clearly in docs. A custom `IFormatProvider` stays possible through C# in the template.

#### The emitted property

```csharp
/// <summary>
/// Gets the culture that converts C# variables to text, such as numbers and dates.
/// It's <see cref="global::System.Globalization.CultureInfo.InvariantCulture" /> until you set it in a <c>TemplateOptions</c> initializer, so the output doesn't depend on the server's culture.
/// </summary>
/// <exception cref="global::System.ArgumentNullException">The value is <see langword="null" />.</exception>
public global::System.Globalization.CultureInfo Culture
{
    get => _culture;
    init => _culture = value ?? throw new global::System.ArgumentNullException(nameof(Culture));
}

private readonly global::System.Globalization.CultureInfo _culture = global::System.Globalization.CultureInfo.InvariantCulture;
```

A sketch: adapt it through `/csharp` (member order, field placement) and run the XML docs through `/humanizer`. The class `<summary>` ("Controls how compiled templates encode C# variables.") no longer covers everything the class does, so its first sentence becomes, for example, "Controls how compiled templates convert and encode C# variables." Every type stays `global::`-qualified per AGENTS.md. Null handling is [S44](#26-spec-decisions-for-11).

#### Where it applies

Per [S45](#26-spec-decisions-for-11):

| Operation | 1.1 behavior |
| --- | --- |
| `Write<T>(T)` in `TextTemplate<TModel>` and `Utf8Template<TModel>` | The private helper (`ToInvariantString` at `6d59e69`; rename it, for example to `ToText`, and give it a `global::System.Globalization.CultureInfo culture` parameter, or make it an instance method that reads `Options.Culture`) calls `IConvertible.ToString(culture)` and `IFormattable.ToString(null, culture)`. The plain `ToString()` fallback is unchanged. |
| `Write<T>(T?) where T : struct` | Unchanged code; it passes the unwrapped value to `Write<T>(T)`, so it gets the culture. |
| `WriteAsync<T>(T)` and `WriteAsync<T>(T?)` (UTF-8) | Unchanged code; they call `Write`, so they get the culture. |
| `RenderToString` | `new global::System.IO.StringWriter(template.Options.Culture)`. |
| `Write(string?)`, `WriteRaw`, `NullVariable`, literal text, the encoders | Unaffected. |
| `new TemplateOptions()`, which a template created without options uses ([S48](#26-spec-decisions-for-11)) | Invariant. (Revision 2's presets row is gone with the presets, [O16](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11).) |
| C# in a template that calls `ToString()` or `writer.Write(1.5)` itself | Not covered; the docs show `Options.Culture` for those (`[[ model.Total.ToString("C", Options.Culture) ]]`). |

#### Per-request localization

Options and template instances are immutable and thread safe, so create one per culture and reuse it. The docs show this pattern (`docs/rendering.md`, [15.4](#154-documentation-that-changes-with-11)):

```csharp
private static readonly ConcurrentDictionary<string, InvoiceTextTemplate> _invoices = new();

InvoiceTextTemplate GetInvoiceTemplate(CultureInfo culture) =>
    _invoices.GetOrAdd(culture.Name, name =>
        new InvoiceTextTemplate(new TemplateOptions { Culture = CultureInfo.GetCultureInfo(name) }));
```

The factory builds the culture from the key with `CultureInfo.GetCultureInfo`, which returns the cached read-only culture without user overrides, so every request for the same name formats the same way, whichever caller arrives first. A consumer who wants another encoder or a `NullVariable` sets it in the same initializer: `new TemplateOptions { Encoder = null, Culture = culture }` for a plain-text email. Revision 2's copy-the-defaults form (`Encoder = defaults.Encoder, NullVariable = defaults.NullVariable`) and its warning about the `#encode` preset are **No longer in scope (superseded by 1.1)** ([O16](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11), S47 withdrawn).

## 5. Diagnostics registry

The registry is the only place IDs are assigned. Every active row has a `## SLAPnnn` heading in `docs/diagnostics.md`, an entry in `AnalyzerReleases`, and at least one test. A retired row keeps its heading (marked "Retired in 1.1") and has no descriptor, no AnalyzerReleases entry ([S51](#26-spec-decisions-for-11)), and a test that checks the heading exists and no descriptor uses the ID. Nothing has shipped, so `AnalyzerReleases.Shipped.md` stays "No stable release has shipped" until the 1.1.0 release-prep pull request adds `## Release 1.1.0`.

### 5.1 Rules

| ID | Severity | Title | When it fires | Location | 1.1 change |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| SLAP001 | n/a | Template class name can't be derived | **Retired in 1.1** ([O17](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)). Was: `#type` is omitted and the file name doesn't produce a valid identifier. Now a derived name isn't checked, and an invalid one is a compiler error in the generated file; an empty one reports SLAP007. | n/a | Retired; descriptor `ClassNameNotDerived` deleted |
| SLAP002 | n/a | Template namespace can't be derived | **Retired in 1.1** (O17). Was: `#namespace` is omitted and `RootNamespace` plus the folders don't produce a valid namespace, or the file is outside the project folder. Now the derived namespace isn't checked, and an invalid one is a compiler error in the generated file. | n/a | Retired; descriptor `NamespaceNotDerived` deleted |
| SLAP003 | Error | Template has no `#model` directive | No `#model` directive. | End of the directive section | None |
| SLAP004 | Error | Unclosed template block | An opener's closer (built from its kind and bracket level) never appears after it ([S28](#26-spec-decisions-for-11)). | The opener: the last `N` brackets plus the `%` or `*` marker | Message argument is the level's closer; docs rewritten |
| SLAP005 | Error | Public template needs public support types | `#access public` while `AnglePlateBaseAccessModifier` is `internal`. | The `#access` value | None |
| SLAP006 | Error | Duplicate directive | A non-repeatable directive appears twice. | The repeat | None |
| SLAP007 | Error | Invalid directive value | A blank value for any directive; `#generate` other than `Text` or `Utf8`; `#access` other than `public` or `internal`; an empty derived `#type` ([S49](#26-spec-decisions-for-11)). | The value; the directive name for a blank value; line 1 for an empty derived `#type` | Narrowed: no Roslyn checks of `#type`, `#model`, `#namespace`, or `#using` values, and no `#encode` |
| SLAP008 | Error | File isn't a template | A file added with `<AnglePlate Include>` is empty, unreadable, or doesn't start with a directive. | Line 1 | None |
| SLAP009 | Error | Invalid MSBuild setting | `AnglePlateBaseAccessModifier` isn't `public` or `internal`. Stops all generation. | None | None |
| SLAP010 | Error | C# version too old | `LangVersion` below 11. Stops all generation. | None | None |
| SLAP011 | Warning | Possible misspelled directive | See [5.2](#52-slap011-rule). | The line | None |
| SLAP012 | n/a | Not assigned | The emitted code needs only `System.IO.Pipelines` beyond .NET 8, so a `net8.0` project fails with the compiler's own CS0234, which the `UnsupportedFramework` consumer pins. A new framework dependency reopens the question. The ID stays free. | n/a | None |

**SLAP004 in 1.1.** `DiagnosticDescriptors.UnclosedBlock` keeps its title ("Unclosed template block") and message ("Template block is missing its closing delimiter '{0}'"). `{0}` is now the closer for the opener's kind and level: `]]`, `]]]`, `]]]]`, `%]`, `%]]`, `%]]]`, `%]]]]`, `*]`, `*]]`, `*]]]`, or `*]]]]`. The location is `TextSpan.FromBounds(openerStart, contentStart)`. The 1.0 row's "an opener has no closer the scanner accepts" and "the scanner's -1 cases" are **No longer in scope (superseded by 1.1)**. If Q3's answer changes the wording (for example to "closer"), update `DiagnosticDescriptors`, `AnalyzerReleases.Unshipped.md` (only if the title changes), `docs/diagnostics.md`, `tests/AnglePlates.Tests/Snapshots/UnclosedBlock.verified.txt`, and both `BuildErrors/Slap004` expected lines in one commit.

Messages drop the absolute file path, titles are full phrases, and descriptors are declared in ID order through the one `Create` helper (all already true). There's no diagnostic for duplicate template classes (S21), none for over-limit bracket runs or level mismatches (S28), none for invalid C# in directive values (O17), and none for a leftover `#encode` line (S53).

**SLAP001 and SLAP002 in 1.1** ([O17](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11), [S51](#26-spec-decisions-for-11)). Delete `ClassNameNotDerived` and `NamespaceNotDerived` from `DiagnosticDescriptors` (keep the "in ID order" comment true), delete both rows from `AnalyzerReleases.Unshipped.md`, and add nothing under "Removed Rules" (the IDs never shipped). Confirm with a `-warnaserror` build that no RS20xx release-tracking warning appears. In `docs/diagnostics.md`, keep `## SLAP001` and `## SLAP002` with their bold titles, replace each body with "**Retired in 1.1.**" and one sentence (AnglePlates no longer checks derived names; an invalid name is a compiler error in the generated file, so add `#type` or `#namespace`), and never reuse the IDs. `DiagnosticTests` asserts both headings exist, say "Retired in 1.1", and that no descriptor uses either ID. Delete the `BuildErrors/Slap001` and `BuildErrors/Slap002` package-consumer cases ([11.4](#114-package-validation)).

### 5.2 SLAP011 rule

Implemented in 1.0 and unchanged. It checks the first body line and the first line of a file with no directives. It warns when the line matches `^#[A-Za-z]+` followed by whitespace or the end of the line, the name isn't a directive, and the name equals a directive name ignoring case or is within optimal-string-alignment distance 1 (2 when the directive name has more than four letters). The message is "'#modle' isn't a directive. Did you mean '#model'?" Its table test covers CSS (`#header {`, `#top {`), Markdown, shebangs, and C# preprocessor lines. A discovered near-miss is still skipped, and an explicit one still reports SLAP008.

### 5.3 Help links

Every descriptor's `helpLinkUri` is `https://github.com/SlusserLabs/AnglePlates/blob/main/docs/diagnostics.md#slapnnn`. Headings are exactly `## SLAPnnn` with the bold title on the next line, never renamed or removed; a retired rule says "Retired in x.y", which in 1.1 applies to SLAP001 and SLAP002 ([S51](#26-spec-decisions-for-11)). `DocumentationLinkTests` checks every anchor. The links 404 until the repository is public; checking them is part of Phase 9.

## 6. Generator internals

### 6.1 Folder layout

All types stay in `SlusserLabs.AnglePlates` (S8), one type per file. After 1.1:

```text
src/AnglePlates/
  AnglePlates.csproj
  AnalyzerReleases.Shipped.md
  AnalyzerReleases.Unshipped.md
  Properties/launchSettings.json
  build/
    SlusserLabs.AnglePlates.props
    SlusserLabs.AnglePlates.targets
  TemplateGenerator.cs                the incremental pipeline only
  Pipeline/
    TemplateFile.cs, TemplateContext.cs, TemplateResult.cs, GeneratorSettings.cs, GeneratorSetting.cs,
    TemplateOrigin.cs, TemplatePath.cs, EquatableArray.cs, DuplicateTemplates.cs, GeneratedTemplate.cs
  Parsing/
    TemplateSyntax.cs                 bracket, marker, trim, whitespace, and line-break constants
    TemplateParser.cs                 static; text in, ParsedTemplate out
    ParsedTemplate.cs                 directives, body tokens, errors, body start
    Token.cs, TokenType.cs, DirectiveToken.cs
  Directives/
    DirectiveKind.cs, DirectiveKindExtensions.cs, TemplateDirectives.cs, DirectiveReader.cs,
    DirectiveSpellCheck.cs, GenerationTarget.cs
  Emit/
    SupportTypes.cs
    TemplateClassWriter.cs
  Diagnostics/
    DiagnosticDescriptors.cs, TemplateError.cs, TemplateDiagnostic.cs, UnexpectedValue.cs
```

**No longer in scope (superseded by 1.1):** `Parsing/CSharpBlockScanner.cs` ("finds a C# block's closer with Roslyn's lexer") and `Parsing/TemplateReader.cs` ("was SpanReader<T>, now char-only"). Both are deleted ([S29](#26-spec-decisions-for-11)). `Directives/TemplateEncoding.cs` is deleted too ([O16](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)).

### 6.2 Class responsibilities

Binding from 1.0 unless noted:

- **`TemplateGenerator`**: a short numbered pipeline in `Initialize`; named static outputs `AddSupportTypes`, `AddTemplateSources`, `AddNumberedSources`; the caching-rule comment (every value between steps compares by value; nothing holds a `Compilation`, `SyntaxTree`, or `SourceText`); `sealed`; `[Generator(LanguageNames.CSharp)]`; `internal const` tracking names. It still calls `TemplateParser.Parse(text, cancellationToken)` and uses `TemplateSyntax.LineEnd` for SLAP011. **1.1 change** ([S50](#26-spec-decisions-for-11)): `CreateHintPrefix` sanitizes the prefix with plain string work instead of `SyntaxFactory.ParseName`, and `using Microsoft.CodeAnalysis.CSharp.Syntax;` goes (`Microsoft.CodeAnalysis.CSharp` stays for `CSharpParseOptions` and `LanguageVersion`). Its comment ("The namespace uses each identifier's value, so a verbatim '@' or a Unicode escape never reaches the file name") is rewritten to say why: directive values aren't validated, and `AddSource` rejects some characters, so anything but letters, digits, `.`, and `_` becomes `_`. No growth over 311 lines.
- **`TemplateParser`**: a static class with `static ParsedTemplate Parse(string text, CancellationToken cancellationToken)`, cancellation checked once per block, directives returned as a `DirectiveToken` list. **1.1 rewrite** per [7](#7-the-bracket-level-parser). The 1.0 description "plus the scanner call" is **No longer in scope (superseded by 1.1)**.
- **`TemplateSyntax`**: replaces the old configurable options. **1.1 rewrite** per [7.4](#74-templatesyntax). The 1.0 comment "The C#-aware scanner depends on these exact closers" and the six two-character constants are **No longer in scope (superseded by 1.1)**.
- **`Token`** (`readonly struct`: `Type`, `TextSpan Span` of the trimmed content, `TrimPrevious`, `TrimNext`), **`TokenType`** (`Literal`, `Variable`, `Code`, `Comment`), **`DirectiveToken`**, **`ParsedTemplate`**: unchanged. Don't add a level property; nothing downstream needs it.
- **`TemplateError(DiagnosticDescriptor, TextSpan, params string[])`**: unchanged.
- **`DirectiveReader`**: **1.1 simplification** ([O16](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11), [O17](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)). Its purpose comment becomes, for example, "Reads a template's directives, derives #type and #namespace when they're left out, and turns the result into TemplateDirectives, or reports why it can't" (no "Validates").
  - Remove the `Microsoft.CodeAnalysis.CSharp` and `Microsoft.CodeAnalysis.CSharp.Syntax` usings, and `Microsoft.CodeAnalysis` too unless a remaining member needs it; keep `Microsoft.CodeAnalysis.Text`, `System`, `System.Collections.Generic`, and `System.Linq` (`DeriveType` still uses `Select`).
  - Delete `IsNamespaceName`, `IsTypeName` (with the whitespace-trivia check and its "Comments would swallow the rest of the generated line" comment), `FindNamespaceProblem`, `ReadEncode`, `_encodings`, `_expectedEncoding`, and `_encoding`.
  - `#type`, `#model`, `#namespace`, and `#using` become direct assignments in the `ReadDirective` switch after a blank check (`value.Length == 0` reports SLAP007 through `AddInvalidValue` with today's label for that directive, [S49](#26-spec-decisions-for-11)); collapse their four `Read*` methods. Do the blank check inside the existing `ReadDirective` switch, in each case or through a helper that takes the label, so no second switch over `DirectiveKind` (and no second uncovered default arm) is added. `#using` keeps its ordinal de-duplication through `_distinctUsings` as today.
  - `DeriveType` keeps its string work (file name up to the first `.`, split on `-`, `_`, and space, `char.ToUpperInvariant` on each part) and drops `SyntaxFacts.IsValidIdentifier` and `ClassNameNotDerived`; an empty result reports SLAP007 at line 1 with the message "Directive '#type' has invalid value ''; expected a file name that gives a class name; add #type" ([S49](#26-spec-decisions-for-11)) (so `FirstLine()` stays, for that one case).
  - `DeriveNamespace` becomes `string.Join(".", [rootNamespace.Trim(), .. folders])` with no checks.
  - Keep `ReadGenerate`, `ReadAccess`, `ExpectedAccessModifier` (shared with SLAP009), SLAP006 detection, `ReportMissingModel` (SLAP003), `LineLength`, and `AddInvalidValue`. Reword the `Read` comment "Derivation and missing-directive errors go first", since only SLAP003 and the empty-`#type` case remain there; the reported diagnostic order stays deterministic.
  - Target: at most 200 lines, from 352 ([6.7](#67-simplification-targets)).
- **`TemplateDirectives`**: loses the `encoding` parameter and the `Encoding` property. **`DirectiveKind`** and **`DirectiveKindExtensions`**: lose `Encode` (from the enum, `All`, and `GetName`); `TemplateParser`'s directive prefixes and `DirectiveSpellCheck` follow `All` automatically. **`TemplateEncoding`**: deleted. **`DirectiveSpellCheck`** (SLAP011): unchanged.
- **`TemplateClassWriter`**: an instance class with small `Write*` methods. **1.1 edits** per [8](#8-emitting-c-without-reading-it), plus the constructor fallback ([S48](#26-spec-decisions-for-11)). It emits directive values as they are (no change there).
- **`DiagnosticDescriptors`**: `ClassNameNotDerived` (SLAP001) and `NamespaceNotDerived` (SLAP002) are deleted ([S51](#26-spec-decisions-for-11)); 73 lines become about 63.
- **`SupportTypes`**: raw string literals, one method per type, the snapshot is the contract. 1.1 changes: the four static presets and their XML docs are deleted from `TemplateOptions` ([O16](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)); the `Culture` property in `TemplateOptions`, the culture-taking conversion helper and the `Write<T>(T)` XML docs in both base classes, and `RenderToString`'s `StringWriter` ([4.11](#411-culture), S43 to S45); and the "pre-1.0" comment wording above the helper ([S37](#26-spec-decisions-for-11)). All eight support-type snapshots change.

### 6.3 Comments, style, and skills

Binding on every 1.1 change:

- Every type has a one-line `//` purpose comment. Internal types don't get XML docs (`/csharp`).
- Comments explain why, one idea per sentence, next to the code. Comment prose goes through `/humanizer`.
- `/csharp` for all C#, including emitted strings; AGENTS.md's `global::` rule overrides the skill's import preference.
- `/dotnet-tunit` for tests in both test projects.
- Seal what isn't designed for inheritance. Collection expressions consistently. No `AggressiveInlining`.
- **Every pull request's acceptance includes**: purpose comments on new or changed types; the description names the skills used (`/csharp`, `/dotnet-tunit`, `/humanizer`, `/docs`); no new build warnings under `.editorconfig`.

### 6.4 Project file

Binding from 1.0: one commented `PropertyGroup` per concern; `<EnableNETAnalyzers>true</EnableNETAnalyzers>`; `None` items for packed MSBuild files; `<InternalsVisibleTo Include="AnglePlates.Tests" />`; Roslyn (`Microsoft.CodeAnalysis.CSharp`) 4.14.0 and `netstandard2.0`. 1.1 changes nothing in the csproj. The CSharp package stays because `TemplateGenerator`, `GeneratorSettings`, and `SyntaxFactory.Literal` use it (S29); `DirectiveReader` no longer does ([O17](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)).

### 6.5 Incremental pipeline

Binding from 1.0 and unchanged: `GeneratorSettings` record; `TemplateContext` as the only carrier of the root namespace; per-template input `(TemplateFile, TemplateContext)`; SLAP005 reported in `AddTemplateSources`; hint-name numbering in the `Collect` step with the case-insensitive comparer. `ParsedTemplate` isn't cached between steps, so the parser rewrite doesn't touch caching. `IncrementalTests` must still pass.

### 6.6 Small correctness fixes from 1.0

| Fix | 1.1 status |
| --- | --- |
| Fix the two invalid `cref`s; doc-mode compile test | Done; `DocumentationModeTests` must stay warning-free after the emitter change |
| Directive scanning treats CR, LF, and CRLF as line ends (S16) | Done; the rewritten parser keeps it ([7.2](#72-the-algorithm)) |
| `#access` case-insensitive (S17) | Done |
| A C# variable ending in `//` gets a line break before the closing `)` | **No longer in scope (superseded by 1.1).** Deleted with `EndsWithLineComment` (S31). |
| C# block indentation counts brace tokens from the lexer (`CountBraces`) | **No longer in scope (superseded by 1.1).** Deleted; no brace-based indentation (S30). |
| Cancellation checked per block in the parser and between targets | Done; the rewritten parser keeps a per-block check |
| SLAP008's message covers empty, unreadable, and directive-less files | Done |
| Remove the unreachable `i > 0` guard | Done |
| Route enum `default` arms through `UnexpectedValue.Exception` | Done; binding, and the only accepted generator coverage gap |

### 6.7 Simplification targets

The owner will look for a measurable reduction. Measure with `git ls-files -- 'src/AnglePlates/*.cs' | xargs wc -l` (or an equivalent PowerShell sum over the same file list) before Phase B1 and after Phase B4, and put both in the pull request description as a table with one row per changed file plus the totals for `Parsing/`, `Directives/`, `Emit/TemplateClassWriter.cs`, and `src/AnglePlates`. The `Culture` work ([4.11](#411-culture)) adds lines to `Emit/SupportTypes.cs` (an estimated 15 to 20), and removing the presets takes about 20 out again ([O16](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)); both count toward the `src/AnglePlates` total, and the table reports `SupportTypes.cs` on its own row so the reader can see the reductions and the feature growth separately.
