## 7. The bracket-level parser

### 7.1 Where it lives

`Parsing/TemplateParser.cs`, one static class:

```csharp
// Splits template text into directives and body tokens; a block ends at the first exact match of its closer
internal static class TemplateParser
{
    public static ParsedTemplate Parse(string text, CancellationToken cancellationToken);
}
```

Private members: `_directivePrefixes` and `CreateDirectivePrefixes` (unchanged), `TryParseDirective` (index-based), `TryFindOpener`, `TryParseBlock`, `AddLiteral`, and the existing `Trim(ReadOnlySpan<char>, int, int)` bounds helper. Delete `_blockStarts`, `TryParseText`, `GetOpenerLength`, `CountBackslashes`, every escape or backslash rule, and the "Escaped openers can split literal text" comment. [Appendix A](#appendix-a-parser-sketch) has a sketch; adapt it through `/csharp` and run its comments through `/humanizer`.

### 7.2 The algorithm

**Directives.** Semantics unchanged. At `position`, try each `#name` prefix with `text.AsSpan(position).StartsWith(prefix.AsSpan(), StringComparison.Ordinal)`; the name must be followed by `TemplateSyntax.Whitespace` or the end of the text. The value is the trimmed span from the end of the name to `TemplateSyntax.LineEnd(text, nameEnd)`; a blank value is the empty span at the end of the name. The next directive starts at `TemplateSyntax.NextLineStart(text, lineEnd)`, which skips one CR, LF, or CRLF. Directives stop at the first line that isn't a known directive. Keep today's tests for CR, LF, and CRLF directive lines passing unchanged.

**Body.** `literalStart = position`. Loop, checking cancellation once per block:

1. `runStart = text.IndexOf('[', position)`. If it's -1, add the literal `[literalStart, text.Length)` if it isn't empty, and stop.
2. `runEnd` is the first index after `runStart` that isn't `[`. `k = runEnd - runStart`, `N = min(k, 4)`, `openerStart = runEnd - N`. The `k - N` brackets before `openerStart` stay inside the current literal.
3. `marker = runEnd < text.Length ? text[runEnd] : '\0'`. `%` gives `Code` and `contentStart = runEnd + 1`. `*` gives `Comment` and `contentStart = runEnd + 1`. Otherwise, when `N >= 2`, `Variable` and `contentStart = runEnd`. Otherwise (a single `[`, not followed by `%` or `*`), it's literal: set `position = runEnd` and go back to step 1.
4. Add the literal `[literalStart, openerStart)` if it isn't empty.
5. `closer = TemplateSyntax.GetCloser(type, N)`. `trimPrevious = contentStart < text.Length && text[contentStart] == '-'`; `start = trimPrevious ? contentStart + 1 : contentStart`. `closerStart = text.IndexOf(closer, start, StringComparison.Ordinal)`.
6. If `closerStart` is -1, add `new TemplateError(DiagnosticDescriptors.UnclosedBlock, TextSpan.FromBounds(openerStart, contentStart), closer)` and stop. The rest of the text is consumed and produces no tokens, as in 1.0, and the error makes the generator skip the template.
7. `trimNext = closerStart > start && text[closerStart - 1] == '-'`. The token is `new Token(type, Trim(start, trimNext ? closerStart - 1 : closerStart), trimPrevious, trimNext)`, where `Trim` drops `TemplateSyntax.Whitespace` from both ends of the content bounds (1.0 behavior). Set `position = literalStart = closerStart + closer.Length`.

Semantics this keeps on purpose (pin each with a test):

- `[[-]]` is an empty C# variable with `TrimPrevious` only (the search starts after the marker, and `closerStart > start` is false).
- `[%]` is unclosed: the search for `%]` starts after the `%`.
- `[[` at the end of the text is an unclosed C# variable with `{0} = ]]` and a two-character span.
- `[` at the end of the text is literal.

### 7.3 Invariants

- Every token span is a slice of the text, and spans never overlap.
- The parser never emits two adjacent `Literal` tokens: each gap between blocks becomes at most one literal, and literal brackets from a single `[` or an over-limit run stay inside that gap's literal. This is what lets the class writer drop `JoinLiterals` ([8.5](#85-literal-runs)). A test asserts it over every row of `Parse_Text_ReturnsTokens`.
- Parsing is linear: one `IndexOf` for each `[` run and one for each closer. No input makes the parser revisit text. A test with 100,000 `[` and a test with thousands of blocks containing broken C# assert tokens, not timing.

### 7.4 `TemplateSyntax`

Remove `VariableOpen`, `VariableClose`, `CodeOpen`, `CodeClose`, `CommentOpen`, `CommentClose`, and `LineBreaks` (its only user was `TemplateReader.AdvancePastLine`). Keep `TrimMarker`, `DirectivePrefix`, `Whitespace`, `IsLineBreak`, and `LineEnd`, which `DirectiveReader`, `DirectiveSpellCheck`, `TemplateGenerator.ProcessTemplate`, and the class writer's trimming use. Add:

```csharp
// The fixed spelling of AnglePlates templates. An opener repeats '[' one to four times, and its closer repeats ']' as often.
internal static class TemplateSyntax
{
    public const char OpenBracket = '[';
    public const char CloseBracket = ']';
    public const char CodeMarker = '%';
    public const char CommentMarker = '*';

    // Four is plenty, and a fixed limit keeps the parser from handling runs of any length
    public const int MaxBracketLevel = 4;

    // One '[' followed by anything but a marker is ordinary text, so C# variables start at two
    public const int MinVariableLevel = 2;

    public const char TrimMarker = '-';
    public const char DirectivePrefix = '#';
    public const string Whitespace = " \t\r\n";

    // The closer for a block of this kind and level, such as "]]]" or "%]]"
    public static string GetCloser(TokenType type, int level);

    // CR, LF, and CRLF all end a line, in directives and everywhere else
    public static bool IsLineBreak(char value) => value is '\r' or '\n';

    public static int LineEnd(string text, int start);        // existing

    // Where the next line starts after a line that ends at lineEnd; skips one CR, LF, or CRLF
    public static int NextLineStart(string text, int lineEnd);
}
```

`GetCloser` is a `switch` on `type` (`Variable` gives `new string(']', level)`, `Code` gives `"%" + new string(']', level)`, `Comment` gives `"*" + new string(']', level)`, and the default arm throws `UnexpectedValue.Exception(type, nameof(type))`), or a precomputed `string[]` per kind indexed by level; pick whichever reads simpler. If you add a `switch` default arm, it joins the accepted coverage gaps in `docs/testing.md#coverage`; the precomputed table avoids that. Signatures above are a sketch; keep whatever `LineEnd` already looks like.

### 7.5 Superseded 1.0 scanner design

All of 1.0's section 7 is **No longer in scope (superseded by 1.1).** For the record, it specified:

- `Parsing/CSharpBlockScanner.FindCloser(string text, int contentStart, TokenType blockType)`, called by `TemplateParser.TryParseBlock` for C# variables and C# blocks, returning -1 for SLAP004.
- Lexing with `SyntaxFactory.ParseTokens` under `LanguageVersion.Preview` and `DocumentationMode.None`: a variable ended at a depth-0 `]` token followed by `]`; a block at a `%` token followed by `]`; whitespace, line breaks, and `/* */` comments never held a closer; line-shaped trivia ended at its first closer; lexing restarted after directive lines; broken C# (`ContainsDiagnostics`) ended at its first closer.
- Verified lexer facts for Roslyn 4.14 (token shapes of `]]`, `%]`, `-]]`, string kinds, unterminated forms, linear lexing).
- No fallback: -1 for no closer, an unmatched `[`, closers only inside literals or comments, or more than 15 hidden closers. A doubling window (1, 2, 4, 8, 16 closers) avoided quadratic lexing and an interpolation-hole stack overflow. The 16-closer example and the "move the strings out or write `"%" + "]"`" fix.
- A parity promise (anything that compiled before scans the same) and a CHANGELOG note about the limit.
- Host-compiler authority: the consumer SDK's lexer decided, proven by the `ScannerParity` consumer on the SDK floor and the newest SDK.

Replacements: [7.2](#72-the-algorithm) (closer search), [4.1](#41-template-syntax) (consumer rules), [S28](#26-spec-decisions-for-11) (SLAP004), [S32](#26-spec-decisions-for-11) (`DelimiterLevels` replaces `ScannerParity`). The CI `latest-sdk` and `sdk-floor` legs stay, because they still prove the package on the oldest and newest SDKs.

## 8. Emitting C# without reading it

### 8.1 The rule

The class writer never reads the C# inside a token. For each C# variable and C# block it writes one `#line` mapping, the token's text with every line at the generated type's indent (column 0, [S39](#26-spec-decisions-for-11)), and `#line default`. It doesn't count braces, look for comments, or split on anything but line breaks (and it doesn't need to split at all; see [8.3](#83-the-layout-rule)). `_writer.Indent` stays constant from the first statement of a render method to its last, apart from the temporary `Indent = 0` around each C# token's own lines.

### 8.2 What `#line` does on continuation lines

Measured with Roslyn 4.14.0 on the .NET 10.0.402 SDK, `net10.0`, LangVersion 11 and latest, in a scratch app outside the repository:

- With `#line (1, 6) - (3, 19) 8 "X.tpl"` over a three-line block with errors expected at template (1,15), (2,12), (3,15):
  - Indenting every line by 8 reported (1,15), (2,20), (3,23): columns on lines 2 and 3 off by 8.
  - Indenting only the first line (today's layout) reported (1,15), (2,12), (3,15): exact.
  - One `#line` per line with every line indented reported exact columns, but breaks on multi-line strings, interpolation holes, and comments (CS1010, CS1002, CS8999, CS1056, CS1073, CS1003, and a wrong line after a multi-line comment).
- The same holds for a multi-line C# variable (`Write(` plus the first line, then the rest, then `, writer);`, offset 14): indented continuation lines reported columns 8 too far right; verbatim continuation lines were exact.
- Effect on output: prefixing every line made `@"one\n  two"` render `"one\n          two"` (8 extra spaces), the same for `$@"..."`; raw `"""` strings were unchanged because the closing delimiter got the same prefix. Today's layout left every string unchanged. Neither layout produced compiler warnings.

So the line-and-column rule is: a `#line` span directive maps the first generated line with its character offset and every later line by its generated column unchanged. That's why layout C0 ([S39](#26-spec-decisions-for-11)) holds exact columns: continuation lines are copied at column 0 plus their own template whitespace, which is exactly their template column, and the first line's offset accounts for the call prefix.

### 8.3 The layout rule

Layout C0 ([E1 and S39](#24-emitter-layout-e1-resolved-by-o9)):

```csharp
// Copies the C# at the generated type's indent (column 0). Nothing reads it; the template's own line breaks and columns
// are kept, so #line maps every line exactly.
private void WriteCodeBlock(Token token)
{
    BeginStatement();
    int indent = _writer.Indent;
    _writer.Indent = 0;
    WriteLineMapping(token.Span, 0);
    _writer.WriteLine(Slice(token.Span).ToString());
    WriteDefaultLineMapping();
    _writer.Indent = indent;
}
```

`WriteLineMapping` computes its offset as `_writer.Indent * _indentString.Length + prefixLength`, so setting `Indent = 0` before the call gives offset 0 for a block. `IndentedTextWriter.WriteLine(string)` indents only before the first character (none at indent 0) and writes embedded line breaks as they are, so the block's own CR, LF, or CRLF breaks are kept. That's the same bytes as splitting on line breaks and writing each line at indent 0 with its original break, which is what O9 describes, so no split is needed. The generator's own lines keep using the writer's newline. The comment above must say why no split is needed. Any U+0085, U+2028, or U+2029 inside a block is copied as is; the compiler counts them as line breaks in both the template and the generated file, so line numbers stay aligned. If a shared helper (for example `WriteCSharp(TextSpan span, string prefix, string suffix)`) reads simpler than the save-and-restore in both methods, use it.

The old 1.0 comment "Copies a C# block as it is. Indenting around braces only affects readability…" and the `CountBraces` call and indent arithmetic are deleted.

### 8.4 C# variables

```csharp
// Writes one C# variable as one call. The base class overloads pick the conversion, and an expression that isn't one
// argument, such as "1, 2", fails to compile at the template location.
private void WriteVariable(Token token, bool checkFlush)
{
    var (call, end) = checkFlush ? (_flushingWrite, _flushingWriteEnd) : (_write, _writeEnd);
    BeginStatement();
    int indent = _writer.Indent;
    _writer.Indent = 0;
    WriteLineMapping(token.Span, call.Length);
    _writer.WriteLine($"{call}{Slice(token.Span).ToString()}{end}");
    WriteDefaultLineMapping();
    _writer.Indent = indent;
}
```

The call prefix goes before the first line and the call's end after the last line, and the call starts at column 0, so the `#line` offset is the call-prefix length (`Write(` is 6; `_flushingWrite` is longer). The `EndsWithLineComment` branch is deleted (S31): `[[ x // note ]]` now comments out `, writer);` and fails to compile. Which errors appear depends on what follows the call: if the next generated token is `}`, CS1026 and CS1002 map to the template; if another statement follows (a literal, another write, or `await FlushAsync` in `RenderAsync`), CS1003 maps to the template and CS1026 lands in the generated file. `LineMappingTests` pins the exact diagnostics per target.

### 8.5 Literal runs

The parser no longer produces adjacent literal tokens ([7.3](#73-invariants)), so:

- Rewrite the `FindLiteralRunEnd` comment: a run merges literals across comments and empty blocks, and a trim marker on one of those tokens ends the run because it trims the literal next to it. Remove "Escaped openers split literal text into adjacent tokens".
- Simplify `WriteLiteralRun`: the head is `tokens[start]` and the tail is `tokens[end - 1]`. With one token, `Trim(Slice(span), trimStart, trimEnd)`. With several, `Trim(head, trimStart, false) + ConcatenateLiterals(start + 1, end - 1) + Trim(tail, false, trimEnd)`. Delete `JoinLiterals` and the head-end and tail-start walking loops, which become unreachable and would cost branch coverage.
- Keep the rules that a trim marker ends a run, that trimming reaches only the literal next to the marker, and that the first write after a C# block in `RenderAsync` is the flushing write, including the `#line` mapping on a flushing literal.

### 8.6 Render method cleanup

In `WriteRenderMethod`, delete "Unbalanced braces in C# blocks can leave the indent anywhere, so finish the method where it started" and the `_writer.Indent = indent + 1` reset; the indent doesn't move inside the token loop anymore. Keep the closing `_writer.Indent--` (or the existing `indent` local) before `}`. The `default` arm that throws `UnexpectedValue.Exception` stays and stays an accepted coverage gap. Keep `_flushingWrite`'s comment citing `[% if (x) %]a[% else %]b`; it explains why the flush check is one statement, which still holds.

### 8.7 Generated code and consumer analyzers

[O14](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11) adds a requirement to the approved layout: generated files must not cause analyzer warnings in a consumer's build because the generator's own lines sit at the method-body indent while template C# sits at column 0 with whatever whitespace, braces, and `var` style the author used. The mechanism is Roslyn's generated-code detection, which the generator already triggers; 1.1 pins it and proves it ([S54](#26-spec-decisions-for-11)).

**How it works.** Roslyn treats a file as generated code when its first comment contains `<auto-generated` or when its file name ends in `.g.cs`, and analyzers that call `context.ConfigureGeneratedCodeAnalysis(GeneratedCodeAnalysisFlags.None)` skip such files, as the IDE, CA, and StyleCop analyzers do. An analyzer that never calls it analyzes generated code by default: Roslyn's default for an unconfigured analyzer is `Analyze | ReportDiagnostics`, which is why the analyzer-authoring rule RS1025 exists. Before relying on this in the docs, confirm it in a scratch build outside the repository with a tiny analyzer that never calls `ConfigureGeneratedCodeAnalysis` and reports on every syntax tree; record the result in the checklist note. Every generated file starts with the `// <auto-generated>` header ([4.10](#410-generated-header)), and every hint name ends in `.g.cs` ([4.9](#49-generated-file-names)), so both signals are already there. Source-generator output isn't treated as generated code on its own; the signals are what count. Compiler diagnostics (`CS*`) aren't analyzers, so generated-code detection doesn't touch them, and they keep surfacing at the template location through `#line`.

**Evidence** (scratch builds on 2026-10-14 with the .NET 10 SDK and Roslyn 4.14.0, outside the repository): a consumer with `EnforceCodeStyleInBuild=true`, `AnalysisLevel=latest-all`, `TreatWarningsAsErrors=true`, `GenerateDocumentationFile=true`, and an `.editorconfig` that raises IDE0055 and the whole Style category to warning (plus a block-scoped namespace preference, `csharp_prefer_braces = true:warning`, and `var` styles set to `false:warning`) built a generated file with mixed indentation, odd braces, tabs, and `var` in multi-line column-0 C#:

| Setup | Diagnostics in the generated file or at template locations |
| --- | --- |
| Header and `.g.cs` hint name | None |
| Header, plus StyleCop.Analyzers 1.1.118 or 1.2.0-beta.556 (active on the consumer's own files) | None |
| The real repository generator over the HelloWorld templates, plus StyleCop 1.1.118 | None (only the consumer's own files reported) |
| No header, `.g.cs` hint name | None |
| No header, hint name without `.g.cs` (control) | IDE0055 on the generator's 8-space lines and about 25 mapped to the template; IDE0160, IDE0240, IDE0008, CA1822, CA1304, CA1311; StyleCop SA1000, SA1002, SA1003, SA1008, SA1009, SA1012, SA1025, SA1027, SA1137, SA1500, SA1633 |
| Header, but the consumer sets `[*.g.cs] generated_code = false` | The IDE and CA set from the control returns |
| Header, template C# with `int unused;`, `string s = null;`, and a nullable assignment | CS0168, CS0219, and CS8600 reported at the template path, line, and column |

**Rules for the implementer:**

- Keep the header as the first line of every generated file (a UTF-8 BOM before it is fine) and keep `.g.cs` on every hint name, including sanitized and numbered ones ([S50](#26-spec-decisions-for-11)). A generator test asserts both for a template class and for each support type (an existing snapshot or `HintNameTests` row may already show it; add an explicit assertion).
- Don't emit `#pragma warning disable` (blanket or per-ID) and don't add `[GeneratedCode]` or `[ExcludeFromCodeCoverage]` attributes for this purpose. A pragma would hide real compiler warnings from template C#, which must keep surfacing at the template location.
- Prove it through the packed package with the new `StrictAnalyzers` consumer ([11.4](#114-package-validation)), which has to build with zero warnings.

**Limits, for the docs** (`docs/rendering.md` "Viewing the generated code" gets two sentences, [15.4](#154-documentation-that-changes-with-11)):

- The suppression covers more than indentation: IDE and CA rules about the template's own C# (for example CA1304 on `ToUpper()`) don't run on it either. Template C# gets compiler warnings, not code-style or code-quality analysis.
- A consumer who overrides detection (`generated_code = false` for `*.g.cs`) gets the analyzers back, including IDE0055 on the generator's own lines; that's unsupported and not guaranteed warning-free.
- An analyzer that opts into generated code (`GeneratedCodeAnalysisFlags.Analyze` or `ReportDiagnostics`), or never configures generated-code analysis at all, still runs and can report on generated files. That includes third-party or in-house analyzers a consumer enables, which [O14](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11) names, so this is a residual gap, which the owner accepted as documented ([O20](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)). Only the built-in analyzers and StyleCop were tested, so the docs promise only "analyzers that exclude generated code, as the built-in IDE and CA analyzers and StyleCop do".
- A narrower option is on record for Q1 and isn't built unless the owner picks it: emit `#pragma warning disable` outside the `#line` regions and `#pragma warning restore` around each mapped template C# span, so the generator's own lines are silenced for every analyzer while compiler and analyzer warnings in template C# still surface. It costs a pragma pair per C# block and variable in every generated file.
- The fix doesn't depend on the column-0 layout: without the header and the `.g.cs` name, the generator's own indented lines trip IDE0055 too.

## 9. Performance

The owner's rule: performance work never comes at the expense of simple code.

### 9.1 Adopted changes (history)

1.0 replaced the hand-rolled encode loop with `encoder.Encode`, emitted one-line `Write(expr, writer)` calls through the overload set, and added `WriteLiteral` and merged literal runs. Measured under the JIT in 1.0 Phase 4:

| Benchmark | Before | After |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 220-character value that needs encoding, text | 577 ns | 686 ns (about 19% slower, accepted by the owner) |
| Plain value, text | 34 ns | 17 ns |
| 3,000 C# variables, text | 209 us | 70 us |
| Table, 1,000 rows | 351 us | 258 us |

The nullable overload is meant to remove the `int?` boxing; the UTF-8 target still allocates the encoded string for a value that needs escaping. None of the old encode loop comes back.

The 1.0 "after" run predates the restored nullable overload (see the PR #1 description), so the `int?` allocation fix has never been measured. Phase B0's baseline run is the first with it; report the `TableBenchmarks` allocation in the 1.1 pull request and don't compare B0 allocations with this table.

### 9.2 Benchmark protocol

1. Before a change to emitted code, run `ValueBenchmarks`, `ManyVariablesBenchmarks`, `TableBenchmarks`, and `LiteralPageBenchmarks` under the JIT (`dotnet run -c Release --project benchmarks/AnglePlates.Benchmarks -- --filter *`), and record the Native AOT executable size that `validate-package.ps1` prints for the `NativeAot` consumer.
2. Run them again after the change.
3. Put both tables in the pull request description. They aren't committed.
4. Any regression beyond noise needs the owner's written acceptance in the pull request.

**For 1.1** the render statements don't change (only whitespace and C# layout). The support types change in one place on the hot path: the conversion helper reads `Options.Culture` instead of the static `CultureInfo.InvariantCulture` ([4.11](#411-culture)), one property read per non-string value. No runtime change beyond noise is expected, and `ValueBenchmarks` and `TableBenchmarks` are the ones that would show it; a regression beyond noise needs the owner's written acceptance like any other (step 4). The benchmarks run with the default options, so they measure the invariant path (the benchmarks project also sets `InvariantGlobalization`). Run the protocol anyway in Phase B0 (before, on `main`) and Phase B5 (after), and don't claim a performance change the numbers don't show. Parser speed isn't benchmarked (there's no parser benchmark, and adding one would be agent-only tooling); the simplification evidence is the line counts.

The benchmarks project stays in the solution and builds in CI, but CI doesn't run it. `docs/development.md` keeps its "Benchmarks" section. 1.1 changes no benchmark template or code; check that none uses `\[[`, `[[*`, or a closer inside C# (none did at `6d59e69`).

### 9.3 Considered and skipped

- **Span formatting** (`ISpanFormattable`/`IUtf8SpanFormattable` with `stackalloc`): about 207 B saved per table row, at the cost of a span-encoding loop in both renderers. If wanted later, it fits inside the `Write<T>` overloads. Deferred ([Deferred past 1.1](#deferred-past-11); no issue is filed, [O19](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)).
- **A configurable flush threshold**: no evidence it's needed.
- **Lexer fast paths**: **No longer in scope (superseded by 1.1)**; there's no lexer.
- (Sharing one pattern-variable name across C# variables was moot in 1.0 and is dropped.)

## 10. Native AOT and trimming

The promise stays: compiled templates are plain C# with no reflection and no dynamic code. Binding from 1.0:

- The `NativeAot` consumer keeps `PublishAot`, `IsAotCompatible`, `TreatWarningsAsErrors`, and `GenerateDocumentationFile`.
- `validate-package.ps1` asserts `EnableTrimAnalyzer`, `EnableAotAnalyzer`, and `IlcTreatWarningsAsErrors` evaluate to `true`.
- The AOT canary (`-p:AotCanary=true`, a `[[ (dynamic)model.Title ]]` template) must fail to publish with IL2026 or IL3050.
- The consumer renders several encoders through options (1.0: presets; 1.1: `new TemplateOptions()`, `new TemplateOptions { Encoder = null }`, and `new TemplateOptions { Encoder = JavaScriptEncoder.Default }` in `CheckOptions`, [O16](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)), a custom `NullVariable`, a custom `Culture` (new in 1.1, below), non-ASCII text, a `#using`, a value over 32K characters, `DateTimeOffset`, `Guid`, `int?`, enums, a `[Flags]` enum, a child template, and `RenderToString`, comparing exact text and bytes in the JIT and native runs.
- CI publishes natively on Windows and Linux. No `IsAotCompatible` or `IsTrimmable` on the analyzer-only package. The README's "Two things are up to you" bullets stay.

**1.1 addition**: put one level-3 C# variable and one level-2 C# block into `tests/AnglePlates.PackageConsumers/NativeAot/Templates/report.html` (not `canary.html`, `line.html`, or `options.html`, whose outputs are checked differently), so the native run covers bracket levels. Change its first body line from `<h1>[[ model.Title ]]</h1>` to `<h1>[[[ model.Title ]]][[% var level = 2; %]] [[[ level ]]]</h1>`, and change the first line of `_expectedReport` in `NativeAot/Program.cs` from `"<h1>Q3 &lt;Report&gt;</h1>\n"` to `"<h1>Q3 &lt;Report&gt; 2</h1>\n"`; check it against the actual render before relying on it. `_expectedReport` is compared for text, `RenderToString`, UTF-8, and `RenderAsync`, so that one edit covers every path. The templates use none of the removed constructs today. `NativeAot` sets `TreatWarningsAsErrors`, so the new local is read by `[[[ level ]]]`; see the warning-free rule in [11.3](#113-runtime-tests).

**1.1 culture row** ([4.11](#411-culture)). Checked at `6d59e69`: `NativeAot.csproj` doesn't set `InvariantGlobalization` (only `samples/HelloWorld` and `benchmarks/AnglePlates.Benchmarks` do), so the native executable loads ICU and a named culture works there. Add `Templates/number.html` (`#type Number`, `#model double`, `#namespace NativeAot.Templates`, then `[[ model ]]`, LF, no final newline) and a check in `Program.cs` in the style of `CheckOptions`: render `1.5` through `NumberTextTemplate` and `NumberUtf8Template` with `new TemplateOptions { Culture = CultureInfo.GetCultureInfo("de-DE") }` and expect `1,5`, and with `new TemplateOptions()` and expect `1.5`, comparing text and bytes in the JIT and native runs. This proves the culture reaches the conversion after trimming.

**1.1 preset removal** ([O16](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)): in `NativeAot/Program.cs`, the `CheckOptions` calls with `TemplateOptions.Html`, `TemplateOptions.Unencoded`, and `TemplateOptions.JavaScript` become `new TemplateOptions()`, `new TemplateOptions { Encoder = null }`, and `new TemplateOptions { Encoder = JavaScriptEncoder.Default }`, with the same expected strings; the comment "Each preset" and `NativeAot/OptionsModel.cs`'s comment ("each options preset") are reworded to "each encoder". If `InvariantGlobalization` is ever turned on for this consumer, drop the de-DE check instead of weakening it: with invariant globalization, `GetCultureInfo("de-DE")` throws `CultureNotFoundException` (predefined cultures only), so the row would test nothing useful.

## 11. Testing

### 11.1 Three layers

| Layer | Project | Proves | Coverage module |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Generator | `tests/AnglePlates.Tests` | Parsing (bracket levels, first-exact-match closers, trims), directives, diagnostics and their locations, `#line` mappings, hint names, MSBuild options, accessibility, language versions, incremental caching. In-memory compilations. | `SlusserLabs.AnglePlates` |
| Emitted code | `tests/AnglePlates.Runtime.Tests` | Support types and template classes, both targets, `Render`, `RenderAsync`, `RenderToString`, encoding, nulls, conversion, culture, cancellation, flushing, child templates, README examples, and exact bytes of bracket-level and multi-line C# templates. Strongly typed, no reflection. | `AnglePlates.Runtime.Tests`, minus test classes |
| Package | `eng/validate-package.ps1` with `tests/AnglePlates.PackageTests` and `tests/AnglePlates.PackageConsumers/*` | The packed nupkg: layout, metadata, consumer assets, analyzer loading, MSBuild discovery, real build errors, chains, Native AOT, incremental rebuilds, the syntax through the packed analyzer, and a warning-free build under strict consumer analyzers. | Not measured |

`docs/testing.md`'s layer table changes "Parsing, the C# block scanner, directives, …" to "Parsing (bracket levels and trim markers), directives, …".

No helper becomes dead in 1.1.

Rewrite its comment ("Braces inside strings don't change the generated indentation, which is cosmetic anyway") to say that the writer doesn't look at braces, so a brace inside a C# string changes nothing. Drop the three `[[ model // note ]]` rows (they move to `LineMappingTests`).

Keep the rest.

- `MultiLineCSharp_InBlocks_KeepsBytesAndCompiles` (new, `[Arguments]` rows): multi-line C# kept byte for byte. A string holding a line break goes through `WriteRaw`, because the default HTML encoder writes CR and LF as `&#xD;` and `&#xA;`: `[% var s = @"a\r\n  b"; WriteRaw(s, writer); %]` gives `a\r\n  b`; the LF variant gives `a\n  b`; `[% var s = @"x\ry"; WriteRaw(s, writer); %]` gives `x\ry`; `[% var s = """\r\n    a\r\n    """; %][[ s ]]` gives `a`; `[[% var s = $"""\r\n    {model.Length} %]\r\n    """; %]][[ s ]]` gives `7 %]`; `[% #if NEVER\r\n var x = 1;\r\n#endif %]ok` gives `ok`. Brace-less and unbalanced blocks across tokens (the existing if/else and foreach rows) still compile.

**`DiagnosticTests`**:

Rows: `[Arguments("prefix [[[[[ model", 8, 4, "]]]]")]` and `[Arguments("prefix [[[ model ]]", 7, 3, "]]]")]` (a closer of another level doesn't count). It asserts the message with `closer`, `TextSpan(TemplateText.Header.Length + column, length)`, and `LineSpan` (3,`column`) to (3,`column + length`), matching the line and column style of the existing test.
- One test per registry row stays, with the help-anchor check.

**`LineMappingTests`**:

- `InvalidCSharp_InVariableOrBlock_ReportsCompilerErrorAtTemplate`: rewrite the six "closers that the scanner skips" rows to higher levels, keeping exact columns: `[[[ "]]" + missing ]]]`; `[[[ "]]" +\r\n  missing ]]]`; `[[[ model[\r\n  missing[0]] ]]]`; `[[% var s = "%]"; s += missing; %]]`; `[[% var s = "%]";\r\n  s += missing; %]]`; and `[[% /* %] */ var s = $"{\r\n  missing}%]"; %]]` with CS0103 at (6,2) length 7. Compute each expected template location from the row text and pin it (the scanner-era rows show the format, for example `<p>[% var count = 1;\r\n  count += missing; %]</p>` at (6,11) length 7). Replace the comment above them ("Closers that the scanner skips inside C# don't move the template lines or columns after them") with one that says a higher bracket level keeps a closer inside C# from ending the block, and the template lines and columns after it still map exactly. Delete the three `//` rows (`[[ missing // note ]]`, `[[ model +\r\n  missing // note\r\n]]`, `[[ 1, 2 // note ]]`) and their comment ("A C# variable that ends in a // comment closes its call on the next generated line") from this method; `LineCommentVariable_*` below covers that case.
- Add multi-line rows for both targets and `RenderAsync`: LF `[% var a = 1;\n  var b = missing; %]`; lone CR `[% var a = 1;\r  var b = missing; %]`; a third-line error `[% var a = 1;\r\n\r\n    a += missing; %]`; level-2 code with the error on line 2; a level-4 multi-line variable `[[[[ model +\r\n   missing ]]]]`; and a block whose first line follows HTML with the error on line 3. Under C0 every reported column equals the template column.
- New `LineCommentVariable_ReportsSyntaxErrors`: rows `[[ model // note ]]`, `[[ model // note ]]x` (trailing literal text, so another generated statement follows the call in every render method), and `[[ model +\r\n  model // note\r\n]]`. Use `model`, not a missing name, so only the syntax errors appear. Generate one target at a time (like `InvalidCSharp_*`) and pin the full diagnostic list (written by hand, see below) for Text (`Render`) and for UTF-8 (`Render` plus `RenderAsync`) separately. Which errors appear depends on what follows the call (scratch builds with the .NET 10 SDK): if the next generated token is `}` (the variable is the last statement of `Render`), CS1026 ("`)` expected") and CS1002 map to the template; if another statement follows (a literal, another write, or `await FlushAsync` in `RenderAsync`), CS1003 ("Syntax error, ',' expected") maps to the template and CS1026 lands in the generated file. Pin mapped errors with `ExpectedCompilerDiagnostic.Mapped(...)` and their exact template spans, and generated-file errors with `ExpectedCompilerDiagnostic.Generated("CS1026", ") expected", "Consumer.Example{target}Template.g.cs")`. Expected IDs per row, from that rule: `[[ model // note ]]` and the multi-line row (whose trimmed content also ends in `// note`, so `, writer);` lands after the comment on the same line) give CS1026 and CS1002 mapped in each `Render` (Text and UTF-8), and CS1003 mapped plus CS1026 generated in `RenderAsync`; `[[ model // note ]]x` gives CS1003 mapped plus CS1026 generated in every render method. These generated-file CS1026 errors, and the one `ClosersInsideCSharp_*` pins, are the deliberate exceptions to "every compiler error maps to the template" (S31).
- New `ClosersInsideCSharp_CloseAtFirstCloser_ReportCompilerErrorsAtTemplate`: `[[ model[0..1]]]`, `[[ grid[row[0]] ]]` (with setup), `[% var s = "%]"; %]`, `[[ "]]" + model ]]`. Pin each case's full diagnostic list per target (generate one target at a time, like `InvalidCSharp_*`), following the rule below. None is SLAP004. Every error maps to the template except a CS1026 that lands in the generated file when the variable's C# leaves the call open, such as an unterminated string (`[[ "]]" + model ]]` generates `Write(", writer);`, and a scratch build reported CS1026 at the end of the next generated line); pin that one with `ExpectedCompilerDiagnostic.Generated`.
- **How to write the expected values for `LineCommentVariable_*` and `ClosersInsideCSharp_*`.** `docs/testing.md` says compiler-error expectations are written by hand and never copied from actual diagnostics, and that rule holds here; it isn't a spec-versus-repository conflict and isn't a STOP. Derive each mapped span by hand from the row text: the template line and 0-based column of the token the compiler flags (for a missing-token error such as CS1026, CS1002, or CS1003, the position where the missing token was expected), with the `#line` offset rules in [8](#8-emitting-c-without-reading-it). Take the IDs and their mapped or generated placement from the rules above and [S31](#26-spec-decisions-for-11). Then run the test to confirm. If the compiler disagrees, find out why (read the generated source in the failure output and reason about where Roslyn places that error) and fix whichever side is wrong; never paste actual output into the expectation. Say in the pull request which expectations needed a second look and why.
- Keep `BrokenCSharp_UnterminatedString_EndsAtFirstCloserAndReportsAtTemplate` (CS1010 at (6,10), CS1002 at (6,14)) and rewrite its comment: the block ends at the first exact closer, and nothing reads the C# inside it. Leave the words "lexer" and "scanner" out of it. Its line-2 assertions guard continuation-line columns. Keep `RefStructVariable_*`, `AsyncRestriction_*`, `FlushingLiteral_*` (its `#line (6, 24) - (6, 24)` stays), and `TemplatePath_*`.

**`TemplateSnapshotTests`** (review every received and verified diff by hand; never auto-accept):

- Add `Snapshot_BracketLevels_MatchesReviewedSources` (Text target). The C# string literal passed to `RenderingTestHelper.Template` is exactly `"#generate Text\r\n[[[ \"]]\" ]]][[% var b = \"%]\"; %]][[[* c *]]][[[[[ model ]]]]]\\[[ model ]]"` (the `\\` is one backslash in the template). ### 11.3 Runtime tests

Binding from 1.0: TUnit on `net10.0`; the generator loaded like the sample (props import, analyzer `ProjectReference`, targets import); real templates in `Templates/`; `[ExcludeFromCodeCoverage]` on each test class, not the assembly (explained in `Properties/AssemblyInfo.cs`); `ThrowingTextWriter`, `ThrowingBufferWriter`, `CountingPipeWriter`, and `ProbeEncoder` (with `AllowUnsafeBlocks`); README examples copied verbatim under `Templates/Readme` with `ReadmeExampleTests` comparing `<!-- sample: … -->` blocks in `README.md` and `docs/templates.md` against the files; every conversion and behavior test from 1.0.

Every existing fixture under `Templates/` (Children, Conversion, Models, Output, Readme, RenderAsync) uses none of the removed bracket constructs. They need no content change and must render byte-identically; `block-end-crlf`, `block-end-lf`, and the README greeting and order fixtures guard multi-line blocks. The exception is `Templates/Encoding/` (revision 2 said it needed no change): its four `encode-{html,none,javascript,url}.html` fixtures exist only to test `#encode` and are deleted ([O16](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)).

**Removing `#encode` and the presets** (Phase B2, in the same commits as the generator change, because CI builds the whole solution with `-warnaserror` and Runtime.Tests references the presets):

- `TemplateOptionsTests` (129 lines; about 70 after): drop `Presets_EachEncoder_HasExpectedEncoderAndNoNullVariable` and replace it with a test that `new TemplateOptions()` has `HtmlEncoder.Default`, a null `NullVariable`, and `CultureInfo.InvariantCulture` as `Culture`. Drop `Encode_Directive_FallsBackToMatchingPreset`, `Encode_Directive_DefaultSharesPreset`, `Encode_ExplicitOptions_OverridePreset`, `Options_BuiltFromPresetEncoder_LeavesPresetUnchanged`, and `CreateEncodeTemplates`. Replace them with options-based rows for no encoder (`Encoder = null`), `JavaScriptEncoder.Default`, `UrlEncoder.Default`, and the HTML default, with the same expected strings as the deleted `#encode` rows, through both targets, using the existing `Templates/Output/variable.html` (`VariableText/Utf8Template`, `#model string?`) and `TestOptions.Create(bool, string?)` or `TestOptions.Create(TextEncoder)`. Update the class comment.
- `TemplateDefaultsTests`: delete the eight `Encode*` rows and the preset switch; rename `Template_WithoutOptions_UsesEncodePreset` (for example to `Template_WithoutOptions_UsesDefaultOptions`); assert that `Default.Options` is the same reference on repeated reads and that options from `new X()` have the default `Encoder`, `NullVariable`, and `Culture`. Drop the fourth tuple element (`preset`) from `Templates()` and from the test's parameters on every row (about 100 rows at `6d59e69`, not only the eight `Encode*` ones). The data source returns `Func<object>` because `TemplateOptions` is internal, so the test casts `(TemplateOptions)createOptions()` and asserts `Encoder` is `HtmlEncoder.Default`, `NullVariable` is null, and `Culture` is `CultureInfo.InvariantCulture`, and asserts `defaultOptions()` returns the same reference on two calls. Update the class comment.
- `OutputTests.Constructor_WithoutOptions_UsesSharedHtmlPreset`: rewrite and rename; a fresh `new X()` gets default-valued options, not a shared preset.
- `SurfaceTests.Constructor_NullOptionsToBase_FallsBackInsteadOfThrowing`: assert default-valued options instead of `IsSameReferenceAs(TemplateOptions.Html)`.
- `TemplateOptions.Unencoded` becomes `new TemplateOptions { Encoder = null }` in `ChildTemplateTests`, `ReadmeExampleTests`, and `RenderToStringTests`.
- `Infrastructure/TestOptions.cs` needs no change.
- `samples/HelloWorld`: remove `#encode none` from `Templates/welcome-email.txt`, render it with `new WelcomeEmailTextTemplate(new TemplateOptions { Encoder = null }).RenderToString(model)` in `Program.cs` (rewrite its comment), and fix `samples/HelloWorld/README.md` ("uses `#encode none`"). The sample's output must stay byte-identical with the B0 capture.

1.1 additions:

- `Templates/Syntax/levels.html`: every level of every kind, extra brackets, former escapes, literal openers through variables, first-exact-match closers raised to the right level, `[[*ptr*]]` as a comment, and trims at levels 1 to 4.
- `Templates/Syntax/multiline-lf.html` (LF bytes) and `Templates/Syntax/multiline-crlf.html` (CRLF bytes): a verbatim string spanning lines, a raw string, a multi-line C# variable, and `#if NEVER` / `#endif` lines inside a block. The fixtures write the verbatim string with `[% WriteRaw(s, writer); %]`, because the default HTML encoder writes CR and LF as `&#xD;` and `&#xA;`; then assert the verbatim string's line terminators come out exactly as the file has them. `*.html` is `-text` in `.gitattributes`, so the bytes stay as committed.
- **Warning-free fixtures.** CI builds `AnglePlates.slnx` with `-warnaserror` (which compiles every Runtime.Tests fixture), and the `NativeAot` consumer sets `TreatWarningsAsErrors`. Every C# local declared in a Runtime.Tests fixture, a `NativeAot` template, or any package-consumer template must be read later (for example `[[[% var x = 1; %]]][[ x ]]`), and fixtures must compile warning-free. Generator-test rows such as `\[% var x = 1; %]` compile in memory and ignore warnings, so don't copy them into fixtures unchanged; write `\[% var x = 1; %][[ x ]]` instead.
- `SyntaxTests` (`[ExcludeFromCodeCoverage]`), using `TemplateOutput.AssertRendersAsync` (Text, UTF-8, and pipe `RenderAsync`).
- `SyntaxTests.LiteralOpener_UnderOtherEncoders_WritesEncodedOpener` (moved from `BlockSyntaxTests`, S33, [O16](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)): a fixture `Templates/Syntax/openers.html` (`#type SyntaxOpeners`, `#model string`, the explicit `#namespace`, then `[[ "[[" ]][[[ "]]" ]]][[ "%]" ]]`, LF, no final newline; the middle variable is level 3 because its C# contains `]]`), rendered through both targets with `TestOptions.Create(JavaScriptEncoder.Default)` and `TestOptions.Create(UrlEncoder.Default)` (the existing `Create(TextEncoder)` overload): JavaScript gives `[[]]%]` unchanged, and URL gives `%5B%5B%5D%5D%25%5D` (measured on .NET 10). Register `SyntaxOpeners` in `TemplateDefaultsTests.Templates()`.
- Register each new template class in `TemplateDefaultsTests.Templates()` so constructors and `Default` stay covered. A UTF-8-only fixture also goes into `RenderAsyncTests`' cases per `docs/testing.md`.
- Add the marked `docs/templates.md` sample for bracket levels as `Templates/Readme/levels.html` with a render test in `ReadmeExampleTests` ([15.4](#154-documentation-that-changes-with-11)). `ReadmeExampleTests` compares the markers it finds in each document with its hard-coded `_samples` dictionary, so insert `"Templates/Readme/levels.html"` as the first element of `_samples["docs/templates.md"]`, because its marker (in "How blocks end") comes before the child-template markers.
- **Fixture headers.** Follow the convention every non-Readme fixture uses: explicit `#type <Folder><Name>` and `#namespace SlusserLabs.AnglePlates.Runtime.Tests.Templates`. So `Templates/Syntax/levels.html` starts `#type SyntaxLevels`, `#model string`, `#namespace SlusserLabs.AnglePlates.Runtime.Tests.Templates`; `multiline-lf.html` uses `#type SyntaxMultilineLf` and `multiline-crlf.html` uses `#type SyntaxMultilineCrlf`, with the same `#model` and `#namespace` lines. None has `#generate`, because `AssertRendersAsync` needs both the Text and Utf8 classes. `Templates/Readme/levels.html` follows the README greeting instead: `#model string` and nothing else in its header, so its names are derived (`LevelsTextTemplate` and `LevelsUtf8Template` in `SlusserLabs.AnglePlates.Runtime.Tests.Templates.Readme`, which `TemplateDefaultsTests` already imports). The names don't collide, and `TemplateDefaultsTests` needs no new `using`.
- `Templates/Readme/levels.html` is the fenced block from `docs/templates.md` verbatim, with LF line breaks and exactly one final LF (the drift test enforces it).
- **Culture** ([4.11](#411-culture), S43 to S45), in a new `CultureTests` class (`[ExcludeFromCodeCoverage]`) unless a row fits an existing class better. `AnglePlates.Runtime.Tests` doesn't set `InvariantGlobalization` (checked at `6d59e69`), so named cultures work there. Use `de-DE` as the non-invariant culture (its decimal separator is a comma, so a wrong culture shows up), write every expectation by hand, and assert Text, UTF-8, and pipe `RenderAsync` wherever a template has both targets. `TemplateOutput.AssertRendersAsync(text, utf8, model, expected)` takes template instances, so build them with the existing `Conversion*TextTemplate(options)` and `Conversion*Utf8Template(options)` constructors and `new TemplateOptions { Culture = de }`. `ConversionTests.AssertConvertsAsync` builds its own options with `TestOptions.Create(encoded, nullVariable)` and doesn't take a culture; don't widen it unless a culture parameter reads simpler:
  - Both targets with `new TemplateOptions { Culture = de }`, through the existing `Templates/Conversion` fixtures: `double` `1.5` gives `1,5`; `decimal` `1234.5m` gives `1234,5`; `DateTime` (2026-10-07 13:05:00) gives `07.10.2026 13:05:00`; `DateTimeOffset` gives the de-DE text the test author derives by hand; `(dynamic)` holding `1.5` gives `1,5`. Avoid AM/PM cultures in expectations: newer ICU versions put U+202F before `PM`, which makes hand-written strings fragile. Never paste actual output into an expectation; if a derived string disagrees with the run, find out why.
  - The nullable overload: add `Templates/Culture/nullable-double.html` (`#type CultureNullableDouble`, `#model double?`, `#namespace SlusserLabs.AnglePlates.Runtime.Tests.Templates`, `[[ model ]]`); `1.5` gives `1,5`, and `null` with `NullVariable = "-"` gives `-` (the culture doesn't touch `NullVariable`).
  - `RenderAsync` on its own: the pipe output with a de-DE culture equals the `Render` bytes (the shared helper covers it; this pins it).
  - `RenderToString`: a text template with a de-DE culture whose C# block writes straight to the writer, `Templates/Culture/writer.html` (`#type CultureWriter`, `#model double`, `#generate Text`, the explicit `#namespace`, then `[% writer.Write(model); %]|[[ model ]]`); `RenderToString(1.5)` gives `1,5|1,5`, proving the `StringWriter` uses `Options.Culture`. With `new TemplateOptions()` it gives `1.5|1.5`.
  - Child templates inherit the culture: add `Templates/Culture/invoice.html` (`#type CultureInvoice`) and `Templates/Culture/invoice-line.html` (`#type CultureInvoiceLine`), both with `#generate Text` and the explicit `#namespace`, like `Templates/Children`; a hand-written partial half `Partials/CultureInvoiceTextTemplate.cs` with the `_line ??= new(Options)` pattern, like `Partials/OrderTextTemplate.cs`; and models under `Models` with a `decimal` price. Rendering the parent with a de-DE culture writes the child's price with a comma; rendering through `Default` writes a period.
  - `CultureInfo.CurrentCulture` doesn't leak in: with `CurrentCulture` set to `de-DE` (restored in `finally`, like the generator test), the default options still write the invariant text (`1.5`; `10/07/2026 13:05:00` for the `DateTime`), and options with `Culture = CultureInfo.GetCultureInfo("fr-FR")` write the fr-FR `DateTime` text (`07/10/2026 13:05:00`), which differs from both the invariant and the de-DE (`07.10.2026 13:05:00`) text, so the test can tell all three apart. Tests that change `CurrentCulture` must not run in parallel with others that read it; mark them `[NotInParallel]` (`/dotnet-tunit`).
  - Culture and encoder are independent ([O16](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)): with a `Templates/Culture/plain.html` fixture (`#type CulturePlain`, `#model double`, `#generate Text`, the explicit `#namespace`, then `<b>[[ model ]]</b>[[ "&" ]]`), `new TemplateOptions { Encoder = null, Culture = de }` renders `1.5` as `<b>1,5</b>&` and `new TemplateOptions { Culture = de }` renders `<b>1,5</b>&amp;`. Setting `Culture` never changes the encoder. (Revision 2's row, which pinned explicit options replacing the `#encode` preset under S47, is **No longer in scope (superseded by 1.1).**)
  - `null` throws: `new TemplateOptions { Culture = null! }` throws `ArgumentNullException` with `ParamName` `Culture` (S44).
  - The default is invariant: `new TemplateOptions().Culture` and the `Options.Culture` of a template created without options are `CultureInfo.InvariantCulture` (in the `TemplateOptionsTests` default-values test above), and `Options_Properties_AreInitOnlyAndClassIsSealed` covers `Culture` as `init`-only (its expected array becomes `{ "Culture", "Encoder", "NullVariable" }` in Phase B2, in the commit that adds the property). Revision 2's "presets are invariant" row is gone with the presets.
  - The output-parity matrix in `ConversionTests` still passes unchanged with the default options.
  - Register `CultureNullableDouble`, `CultureWriter`, `CulturePlain`, and the invoice classes in `TemplateDefaultsTests.Templates()`. The new fixtures read every local they declare ("Warning-free fixtures" above).
- `ConversionTests.cs` and `OutputTests.cs`: the wording changes in [S37](#26-spec-decisions-for-11) ("captured from the prototype's conversion", "pin the overload set", "as the prototype's Convert.ToString did", "Captured from the prototype's code"), and rename `Render_ImplicitStringConversion_UsesToStringLikePre10` to `Render_ImplicitStringConversion_UsesToStringLikePrototype`.

### 11.4 Package validation

Binding from 1.0: consumers live outside the solution, each with its own exit code, run by `eng/validate-package.ps1` after a fresh pack; `#Requires -Version 7.4`; RID from `RuntimeInformation`; the repository's `global.json` copied (or `-SdkVersion` pinned); `-KeepOutput` with binlogs; `-Source` for published previews; `-ExpectedCommit`; a named error when the native executable is missing; one generated `NuGet.config`. The validator requires its package arguments, uses `build/` paths, checks the icon (PNG, 128 to 512 px square, at most 100 KB), README links and images (`https://` or `#anchor`, trusted hosts, no to-do lines), non-empty description and tags, and the consumer's `project.assets.json` (no compile or runtime assets). Most consumers use the one-line `PrivateAssets="all"` reference; `AspNetCore` keeps the CLI's multi-line form; `NoPrivateAssets` is the exception. `tests/AnglePlates.PackageTests/README.md` holds the one consumer table, which is the checklist for every stable release. The deliberate fixtures (`PublicAPI.Shipped.txt`, `Templates/README.md`, `Templates/empty.txt`, `CustomViews/*`, `Explicit/*`) stay unedited.
