| Consumer | Change |
| --- | --- |
| `ScannerParity` | **Delete** the folder (`Program.cs`, `ScannerParity.csproj`, `Templates/parity.html`). 1.0's "ScannerParity proves scanner parity on the consumer's compiler" is **No longer in scope (superseded by 1.1)**. |
| `DelimiterLevels` (new) | `tests/AnglePlates.PackageConsumers/DelimiterLevels`: `DelimiterLevels.csproj` copied from `ScannerParity.csproj`, `Program.cs`, and `Templates/levels.html` (LF bytes, contents in [Appendix B](#appendix-b-delimiterlevels-consumer-template)). It renders Text, UTF-8, and `RenderAsync` to a `Pipe`, compares each with the exact expected string, prints `DelimiterLevels passed.`, and exits 1 on any mismatch. |
| `eng/validate-package.ps1` | Replace the `ScannerParity` entry in the consumer list with `@{ Name = 'DelimiterLevels'; Source = 'tests/AnglePlates.PackageConsumers/DelimiterLevels'; Project = 'DelimiterLevels.csproj'; Arguments = @() }`, and add `@{ Name = 'StrictAnalyzers'; Source = 'tests/AnglePlates.PackageConsumers/StrictAnalyzers'; Project = 'StrictAnalyzers.csproj'; Arguments = @() }`. CI already runs every registered consumer on all legs. Also replace the consumer copy (`Copy-Item -Path (Join-Path $repositoryRoot "$($consumer.Source)/*") -Destination $consumerRoot -Recurse`, line 259 at `6d59e69`) with `Get-ChildItem -LiteralPath (Join-Path $repositoryRoot $consumer.Source) -Force \| Copy-Item -Destination $consumerRoot -Recurse -Force`: PowerShell treats dotfiles as hidden on Linux and macOS, and a wildcard copy without `-Force` likely skips them, so `StrictAnalyzers/.editorconfig` (the first dotfile in any consumer) would never reach the ubuntu, latest-sdk, and sdk-floor legs. Confirm on the ubuntu leg that `.editorconfig` lands in the copied `StrictAnalyzers` and `BuildErrors/StrictAnalyzersControl` folders (for example from the `StrictAnalyzersControl` case failing with IDE0055) and note the run in the checklist. |
| `BuildErrors/Slap004` | Keep `Templates/unclosed.html` and its line. Add `Templates/unclosed-level.html` (`#type UnclosedLevel`, `#model string`, `#namespace Cases`, `Hello [[[ model ]]`) and the expected line `Templates/unclosed-level.html(4,7): error SLAP004: Template block is missing its closing delimiter ']]]' (https://github.com/SlusserLabs/AnglePlates/blob/main/docs/diagnostics.md#slap004)`. Verify the column against the actual build output. |
| `BuildErrors/Cs0103` | Keep `[% Missing(); %]` at (5,4). Add a multi-line block on lines 6-7 (`[% var ok = 1;` on line 6 and `   Missing2(ok); %]` on line 7, with an LF added after the existing line 5, so the block reads its local and stays warning-free per [11.3](#113-runtime-tests)) and the expected line `Templates/bad.html(7,4): error CS0103: The name 'Missing2' does not exist in the current context`. This proves exact line-2 mapping on the floor and latest SDK compilers. |
| `BuildErrors/Cs1026` (new, required) | Named after the diagnostic it pins, like every other `BuildErrors` case. `validate-package.ps1` builds each case as `<case>/<case>.csproj`, so the folder `Cs1026` holds `Cs1026.csproj` and `Program.cs` (copied from `Slap004` with the name changed), `Templates/comment.html` (`#type LineComment`, `#model string`, `#namespace Cases`, then `[[ model // note ]]`), and `ExpectedErrors.txt` with one message-only line, `error CS1026: ) expected`. `Assert-BuildFails` matches a line that starts with `error` as a message at any location. That's right here because CS1026's location depends on what follows the call: in a `Render` method where `}` follows, it maps to the template; in `RenderAsync`, where `await FlushAsync` follows, it lands in the generated file. The message-only line matches either way. A template-mapped CS1003 line (`Templates/comment.html(4,…): error CS1003: Syntax error, ',' expected`) is optional; add it only after checking the exact location against the build output. |
| `NativeAot` | Edit `Templates/report.html`'s `<h1>` line and the first line of `_expectedReport` in `Program.cs` as [10](#10-native-aot-and-trimming) gives them. Add `Templates/number.html` and the culture check from [10](#10-native-aot-and-trimming). Replace the three presets in `CheckOptions` with options and reword the "preset" comments ([10](#10-native-aot-and-trimming), [O16](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)). |
| `BuildErrors/Slap001`, `BuildErrors/Slap002` | **Delete** both folders ([O17](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)). `validate-package.ps1` discovers `BuildErrors` subfolders (`BuildErrors = $true`), so no script edit is needed. Don't add a replacement case for a bad derived name; the generator tests cover it. |
| `DefaultNames` | Remove `#encode none` (line 2) from `Templates/Emails/Welcome/welcome_back.txt`; if it stayed, it would end the directive section and turn `#generate Text` into body text. `Program.cs` renders it with `new WelcomeBackTextTemplate(new TemplateOptions { Encoder = null }).RenderToString("<Ada>")` and drops the `#encode` comment; the expected output doesn't change ([O16](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)). |
| `StrictAnalyzers` (new, required) | Proves [O14](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11) through the packed analyzer ([8.7](#87-generated-code-and-consumer-analyzers)). `tests/AnglePlates.PackageConsumers/StrictAnalyzers` holds `StrictAnalyzers.csproj`, `.editorconfig`, `Program.cs`, and `Templates/strict.html` (LF bytes). The csproj: `TargetFramework` `net10.0`, `LangVersion` `11.0`, `Nullable` `enable`, `EnforceCodeStyleInBuild` `true`, `AnalysisLevel` `latest-all`, `TreatWarningsAsErrors` `true`, `GenerateDocumentationFile` `true`, the one-line `PrivateAssets="all"` package reference like the other consumers, and `StyleCop.Analyzers` `1.1.118` (latest stable; don't take a newer beta) with `PrivateAssets="all"`. The `.editorconfig` (`root = true`, `[*.cs]`): `dotnet_diagnostic.IDE0055.severity = warning`, `dotnet_analyzer_diagnostic.category-Style.severity = warning`, `csharp_style_namespace_declarations = block_scoped:warning` (deliberately the opposite of the generated file-scoped namespace), `csharp_prefer_braces = true:warning`, and `csharp_style_var_for_built_in_types`, `csharp_style_var_when_type_is_apparent`, and `csharp_style_var_elsewhere` set to `false:warning`. Keep the consumer's own `Program.cs` clean under these rules: write it as top-level statements with no instance members, and expect it to meet more than the StyleCop rules (for example CA1303 on `Console.WriteLine("StrictAnalyzers passed.")`, CA2007, IDE0210, IDE0008, SA1633, SA1200, and the SA1101/IDE0003 conflict). Silence rules only for the consumer's own files, in a `[Program.cs]` section of the `.editorconfig`, never in `[*.cs]` and never for `*.g.cs`, so generated files face every configured rule; list every silenced rule in a comment there. Don't relax `AnalysisLevel`, `EnforceCodeStyleInBuild`, `TreatWarningsAsErrors`, or the `[*.cs]` severities to get a green build; that would weaken the O14 proof, so STOP and report instead. The template has a multi-line column-0 C# block and a multi-line C# variable with tabs and spaces mixed, odd brace placement (`for(...){`, `}` on its own line at another indent), and `var`, and reads every local it declares. Pass condition: the build exits 0 with zero warnings, `Program.cs` renders the template through Text, UTF-8, and `RenderAsync`, compares each with the exact expected string (checked against the actual render), prints `StrictAnalyzers passed.`, and returns 1 on any mismatch. Register it in `validate-package.ps1` like `DelimiterLevels`. The generated `NuGet.config` already maps every package other than AnglePlates and the consumer-feed packages to nuget.org, so `StyleCop.Analyzers` restores without a script change. **Positive control:** add `BuildErrors/StrictAnalyzersControl` (no script registration needed, because `BuildErrors` subfolders are discovered) with the same csproj settings and `.editorconfig` as `StrictAnalyzers` (renamed project), the same `Program.cs`, and one extra user file, `Misindented.cs`, with one deliberately misindented line. Its `ExpectedErrors.txt` holds one message-only `error IDE0055: ...` line, with the message text verified against the actual build output. It proves the analyzers and the `.editorconfig` actually ran in the copied folder, so `StrictAnalyzers`' zero warnings can't come from analyzers that never ran. |
| All others (`AspNetCore`, other `BuildErrors` cases, `CentralPackageManagement`, `DirectoryBuildPropsRemove`, `DirectoryBuildTargetsRemove`, `LibraryChain`, `NoPrivateAssets`, `PackagedLibrary`, `RemoveDefault`, `SharedTemplates`, `UnsupportedFramework`, `PackageTests`) | No change. They must pass on a fresh `1.1.0-local.<yyyyMMdd>.<n>` pack. `PackagedLibrary`'s two `1.0.0` values (`App/App.csproj`'s `Version="1.0.0"` and `Library/Library.csproj`'s `<Version>1.0.0</Version>`) are the fixture library's own version; leave them. |

`tests/AnglePlates.PackageTests/README.md`: replace the `ScannerParity` row with a `DelimiterLevels` row ("Bracket levels 1 to 4 for C# variables, C# blocks, and comments, extra brackets written as text, blocks ending at the first closer of their level, a backslash before a C# variable, and multi-line C# kept byte for byte, through the packed analyzer. Text, UTF-8, and `RenderAsync` output must match exactly."), and add the level-3 SLAP004 case, the (7,4) line-2 case, the `Cs1026` case, and the `StrictAnalyzersControl` case (IDE0055 on a misindented user file, proving the strict settings run) to the `BuildErrors` row and remove SLAP001 and SLAP002 from it; add a custom `Culture` to the `NativeAot` row and replace its `TemplateOptions.Html`, `Unencoded`, and `JavaScript` with "options with the HTML default, no encoder, and `JavaScriptEncoder.Default`"; reword the `DefaultNames` row, which describes an `#encode none` template, to "a text template rendered with `Encoder = null`"; and add a `StrictAnalyzers` row ("Builds templates with mixed-indentation, multi-line C# under `EnforceCodeStyleInBuild`, `AnalysisLevel=latest-all`, IDE0055 and the Style category at warning, StyleCop, and `TreatWarningsAsErrors`, and must produce zero warnings, because generated files are marked as generated code."). `.github/workflows/ci.yml`'s comment on the validation step renames `ScannerParity` to `DelimiterLevels`.

### 11.5 Coverage policy

| Check | Where | Threshold |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Generator line and branch | CI, every PR and release run, `windows` and `ubuntu` | Fails below 90% |
| Emitted-code line and branch | Same | Fails below 90% |
| Both modules | Manual, before every stable release | 100% line and branch with only the accepted gaps in `docs/testing.md#coverage`, measured in Visual Studio 2026 or with `--coverage`; the result goes in the release-prep PR |
| Package | Every stable release | Every packaged behavior has a consumer row |

CI uses the Microsoft collector TUnit brings along (`--coverage --coverage-output-format cobertura`), each test project's `code-coverage.settings.xml`, and the short `pwsh` gate step in `ci.yml`. No other coverage scripts, configuration, packages, or gates (AGENTS.md). If a TUnit update ever drops the collector, stop and ask the owner.

1.1 effects: the scanner's many branches disappear. The new parser's branches all need rows: `k == 1` followed by `%`, `*`, or anything else; `k` 2 to 4; `k > 4`; each kind; a trim marker at the start and at the end; closer found or not for each kind. Delete unreachable writer code ([8.5](#85-literal-runs)) so branch coverage doesn't drop. The directive side loses most of its branches with the Roslyn checks; what remains needs rows: the shared blank check per directive, the empty derived `#type`, `#generate` and `#access` values, and the hint-name sanitizer with and without characters to replace, and a `.` kept in the namespace part and replaced in the type part. In emitted code, the `Culture` `init` accessor's null and non-null branches both need a test ([11.3](#113-runtime-tests)), and the conversion helper's `IConvertible`, `IFormattable`, and plain `ToString()` branches stay covered with a non-invariant culture as well as the default. The accepted gaps stay: the `UnexpectedValue.Exception` default arms (in `DirectiveKindExtensions.GetName`, `DirectiveReader`, `TemplateClassWriter.WriteRenderMethod`, plus `TemplateSyntax.GetCloser` if it uses a `switch`) and the compiler's dynamic-await branch in emitted code. In Phase B5, measure both modules locally and report the numbers in the pull request, separately from the 100% target. The recorded pre-release check belongs to the 1.1.0 release-prep pull request.

## 12. Packaging and MSBuild

All of this is binding from 1.0 and unchanged by 1.1.

### 12.1 Package contract

- `developmentDependency` is true.
- The analyzer and its XML doc ship under `analyzers/dotnet/cs/netstandard2.0`; the `.snupkg` holds the PDB there.
- The MSBuild files ship under `build/` (not `buildTransitive/`, which leaked discovery into referencing projects).
- No `lib/`, `ref/`, or `runtimes/` folders, no dependencies, and no compile or runtime assets in a consumer's `project.assets.json`.
- The package root holds `README.md`, `LICENSE`, and `icon.png`.

### 12.2 `build` and the props marker

`build/` (S1) plus the compiler-visible `AnglePlatePropsImported` marker (S26): without it the generator emits nothing, which stops support types leaking into projects that reference a library without `PrivateAssets`. `PrivateAssets="all"` is still needed for libraries that get packed, which the README says in one sentence.

### 12.3 `EnableDefaultAnglePlateItems`

Defaults to `true` and gates `<AnglePlate Include="Templates\**\*">` with `_AnglePlateSource` as a child element (an attribute starting with `_` fails with MSB4066). It works from `Directory.Build.props`, where a `Remove` doesn't. Every props and targets element is commented. `docs/configuration.md` notes the folder name is case-sensitive on Linux and macOS and that a project whose `Templates` folder belongs to another tool should set it to `false`.

### 12.4 Symbols and XML docs

Keep the `.snupkg` and the packed XML doc (S11). Push only `*.nupkg`.

### 12.5 Metadata

`Description` in the owner's voice; `PackageTags` `templates;template-engine;text-templates;source-generator;roslyn;codegen;html;utf8;aot;trimming` (no `t4`); the approved `Copyright` in the csproj and LICENSE; `Title` `AnglePlates`; `PackageReleaseNotes` pointing at `CHANGELOG.md`; `RepositoryUrl` and `PackageProjectUrl` unchanged; `PackageReadmeFile` `README.md`; `PackageIcon` `icon.png`.

### 12.6 Version properties

`Directory.Build.props` holds `<VersionPrefix>` and `<VersionSuffix>dev</VersionSuffix>` with a comment pointing at the version table in `docs/releasing.md`, and keeps `Deterministic`, `ContinuousIntegrationBuild`, `EmbedUntrackedSources`, `PublishRepositoryUrl`, the repository properties (`RepositoryType`, `RepositoryUrl`), and `DebugType`, each with a one-line comment. Builds and packs pass `-p:Version`, never `-p:PackageVersion`. The informational version is the version plus `+<commit>`, which the validator compares with `-ExpectedCommit`.

**1.1 change (Phase B4):** `<VersionPrefix>1.1.0</VersionPrefix>` and the comment "1.1.0 is the next release". The 1.0 value ("VersionPrefix 1.0.0 as the next release") is **No longer in scope (superseded by 1.1)**. The release-prep pull request clears `VersionSuffix`.

## 13. CI, versioning, and publishing

### 13.1 Versions

| Kind | Format | Where it goes | Who makes it |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Local | `1.1.0-local.<yyyyMMdd>.<n>` | `artifacts/package` only, never pushed | Anyone. Clear older pairs first (AGENTS.md). |
| CI | `0.0.0-ci.<run>.<attempt>` | CI artifacts only | `ci.yml` |
| Preview | `1.1.0-preview.<run number>` | **int.nugettest.org only** | `release.yml` from a manual `workflow_dispatch` (S23). Run numbers never repeat; gaps are fine. |
| Stable | `1.1.0` | **nuget.org only** | `release.yml` from a published GitHub Release for tag `v1.1.0` on `main` that equals `VersionPrefix`, **after the owner approves the `nuget-org` deployment** (S24). |

The 1.0 values (`1.0.0-local…`, `1.0.0-preview…`, `1.0.0`, `v1.0.0`) are **No longer in scope (superseded by 1.1)**. `local` sorts below `preview`. Nothing is pushed until full validation passes, and pushes never use `--skip-duplicate`. Nothing in the code validates the version number: `release.yml` reads `VersionPrefix` and compares the tag with `v$VersionPrefix`, so changing `VersionPrefix` is enough.

### 13.2 CI workflow

Binding from 1.0 (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`): runs on every pull request, on pushes to `main`, and through `workflow_call`. Legs: `windows` and `ubuntu` (`global.json` SDK; everything, with the coverage gate), `latest-sdk` (newest `10.0.x`; package validation), `sdk-floor` (`global.json` plus exactly `10.0.100`; package validation with `-SdkVersion 10.0.100`, splatted as a hashtable per `6e69bb7`). Each full leg restores, builds Release with `-warnaserror`, tests both projects with coverage and the 90% gate, runs the sample, packs the CI version, installs Linux AOT prerequisites, runs `validate-package.ps1 -KeepOutput $RUNNER_TEMP/validation`, and uploads `*.received.*`, test results, and binlogs on failure. Actions are pinned by SHA with Dependabot; the CI artifact is `ci-package`; CI cancels superseded runs and the release workflow never does.

The 1.1 pull request must be green on all four legs. The only `ci.yml` edit is the `ScannerParity` comment ([11.4](#114-package-validation)).

### 13.3 Release workflow

Binding from 1.0 (`.github/workflows/release.yml`; the file name is fixed because the trusted-publishing policies name it). Triggers: `workflow_dispatch` (a preview, or the nuget.org login check with `check-nuget-org: true`) and `release: published`. `cancel-in-progress: false`.

| Job | Runs when | Permissions | What it does |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `validate` | unless `check-nuget-org` | `contents: read` | Calls `ci.yml`. |
| `pack` | after `validate` | `contents: read` | A dispatch packs `$(VersionPrefix)-preview.<run number>`. A release checks out the tag (`fetch-depth: 0`), requires `^v\d+\.\d+\.\d+$`, equality with `v$(VersionPrefix)`, and ancestry on `origin/main`, then packs the tag's version. Validates with `-ExpectedCommit $GITHUB_SHA` and uploads `release-package`. |
| `publish-int` | dispatch, not the login check | `contents: read`, `id-token: write` | No environment. First step fails with "owner inputs pending; see Phase 8" when the user secret is empty. `NuGet/login` with `token-service-url: https://int.nugettest.org/api/v2/token` and `audience: https://int.nugettest.org`, then pushes `*.nupkg` to `https://apiint.nugettest.org/v3/index.json`. |
| `publish-nuget` | `release: published` and `vars.NUGET_ORG_PUBLISH_ENABLED == 'true'` | `contents: write`, `id-token: write` | Environment `nuget-org` (the owner approves every run). `NuGet/login` with defaults, pushes `*.nupkg` to nuget.org, uploads `.nupkg`, `.snupkg`, and `SHA256SUMS` to the Release. |
| `verify-nuget-login` | dispatch with `check-nuget-org: true` | `id-token: write` | Environment `nuget-org`. `NuGet/login` only; pushes nothing. |

The package is built once and published as is. Only the publish jobs get `id-token: write`, and they run no build code. `NuGet/login` runs right before the push, because the key lasts one hour. `NUGET_USER` (and `NUGET_INT_USER` if the int profile differs) are repository secrets provided in Phase 8.

**Trusted-publishing policies** (owner-only): the int policy names the repository and `release.yml` with no environment; the nuget.org policy names the repository, `release.yml`, and environment `nuget-org`, and is created only in Phase 9. On a private repository a new policy stays active for only 7 days until a successful publish, so the owner creates the int policy right before the first rehearsal and re-activates it if more than 7 days pass.

`release.yml`'s "see Phase 8" message and `docs/releasing.md`'s quote of it stay valid, because Phase 8 keeps its meaning (S34). The release-prep pull request changes both together ([13.7](#137-stable-release-to-nugetorg)).

### 13.4 Agent guardrails

AGENTS.md's Publishing section is authoritative. It's also in `docs/releasing.md` ("What agents may do"), and it binds the implementing agent. Quoted verbatim from AGENTS.md at `6d59e69`:

- Agents may commit and push to the remote repository as they work. Once CI exists (Phase 1), a phase's work goes up as a branch and pull request so CI runs on it. Once the `main` ruleset exists (Phase 9), every change to `main` goes through a pull request.
- **Only the owner** changes the repository's visibility. Agents never make the repo public (or private), and never change other repository settings that need Administration.
- Agents may publish previews to int.nugettest.org at any time by dispatching `release.yml`, after the preview preflight in [docs/releasing.md](../docs/releasing.md#preview-preflight) passes.
- Agents never create stable tags or GitHub Releases, never approve a `nuget-org` deployment, never change `NUGET_ORG_PUBLISH_ENABLED`, never create or edit trusted-publishing policies, never dispatch the nuget.org login check unless the owner asks, and never call nuget.org's push API directly.
- From Phase 8 on, agents use their own GitHub credential (a machine user's fine-grained PAT or a GitHub App) that is **not** a required reviewer on `nuget-org`. If an agent runs under the owner's own login, it's technically a reviewer and could approve through the API, which would make the gate only as strong as the agent's restraint. Until the owner provides that credential in Phase 8, agents commit and push with the git credentials already on the machine; nothing before Phase 9 has a nuget.org gate an agent could approve.
- Agent credential permissions: Contents read/write, Actions read/write, Pull requests read/write, Workflows read/write, Environments read, Variables read, Secrets read (names only), Metadata read, and **no Administration and no Variables write**. Without Variables write, an agent can't flip `NUGET_ORG_PUBLISH_ENABLED`.

(AGENTS.md's link in the third bullet is `docs/releasing.md#preview-preflight`, relative to the repository root; only the target here is adjusted for the `plans/` folder.)

**1.1 change ([O18](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11), [O19](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)), made in Phase B0.** No agent credential is specified at this time and no Issues permission is given, so the last two bullets describe something that won't exist. Replace them in AGENTS.md with this one bullet (by hand, no phase numbers):

- "Agents use the git and `gh` credentials already on the machine; no separate agent credential exists. Those credentials may be the owner's own login, which can technically approve a `nuget-org` deployment through the API, so the rules above are what keep agents out of stable releases: agents never approve a `nuget-org` deployment, never change `NUGET_ORG_PUBLISH_ENABLED` or any other variable, environment, ruleset, or repository setting, and never dispatch the nuget.org login check unless the owner asks. The owner's own gates (creating the GitHub Release, flipping the variable, and approving the deployment) stay in front of every nuget.org publish."

`docs/releasing.md`'s two matching bullets in "What agents may do" change the same way, in the owner's voice through `/docs` and `/humanizer`, in the same commit. The first four bullets don't change; the first one's "(Phase 1)" and "(Phase 9)" go at release prep ([13.7](#137-stable-release-to-nugetorg)). Agents don't file or manage GitHub issues for this work; tracking is the [implementation checklist](#implementation-checklist).

Without a separate credential, the agent's restraint is the only thing between `gh` and an approval it could technically give ([3](#3-where-the-project-stands)). That's why the never-approve rules are explicit and why the owner's other gates stay: no nuget.org policy before Phase 9, the owner-created GitHub Release, the owner-only kill switch, and the owner's environment approval. If the owner later provides a credential, its permission list (the 1.0 bullet above, without Issues) comes back as an owner decision.

The four gates in front of nuget.org: (1) no nuget.org policy until Phase 9; (2) the owner creates the GitHub Release; (3) the owner-only `NUGET_ORG_PUBLISH_ENABLED` kill switch; (4) the owner's `nuget-org` environment approval on every run.

Visibility (S22) and plans: environment reviewers and rulesets work on public repositories on every plan, but on a private one they need a paid plan (rulesets) or Enterprise (reviewers). In Phase 9, after the owner makes the repository public, the owner creates: `nuget-org` (the owner as the only required reviewer, "prevent self-review" off, deployment refs `v*` tags and `main`); a `main` ruleset (pull request required with 0 approvals; required checks `windows`, `ubuntu`, `latest-sdk`, `sdk-floor`; block force pushes and deletion); and a tag ruleset blocking create, update, and delete of `v*` with an admin-only bypass.

### 13.5 Publishing access preflight

The checklist lives in `docs/releasing.md`, written for people and agents, not as a committed script. The agent reports each line as pass or fail.

**Preview preflight** (first in Phase 8; then before a preview whenever access might have changed):

1. `gh --version` (install with `winget install GitHub.cli` if it's missing), then `gh auth status` shows a signed-in account. With no separate agent credential ([O19](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)) that may be the owner's login; report which account it is, and the never-approve rules in [13.4](#134-agent-guardrails) apply either way. (1.0's "shows the agent account, not the owner" is **No longer in scope (superseded by 1.1)** until the owner provides a credential.) Access is proven by the next lines, not by a scope list.
2. `gh api repos/SlusserLabs/AnglePlates --jq '.permissions'` shows `push: true`. Report the `admin` value. When it's `true` (the owner's login, [O19](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)), the never-change-settings rules in [13.4](#134-agent-guardrails) apply. (1.0's `admin: false` requirement is **No longer in scope (superseded by 1.1)** until the owner provides a credential.)
3. `gh workflow list` shows `CI` and `Release` active.
4. `gh secret list` shows `NUGET_USER` (and `NUGET_INT_USER` if used).
5. The owner has confirmed that an int.nugettest.org account exists, that its profile name is the one in `NUGET_USER` (or `NUGET_INT_USER`), and that the int trusted-publishing policy for `SlusserLabs/AnglePlates` and `release.yml` (no environment) is active, within its 7-day window while the repo is private.
6. Functional proof: `gh workflow run release.yml --ref main`, `gh run watch`, then `./eng/validate-package.ps1 -Version 1.1.0-preview.<run> -Source https://apiint.nugettest.org/v3/index.json` once the version appears in `https://apiint.nugettest.org/v3-flatcontainer/slusserlabs.angleplates/index.json`.

**Stable preflight** (Phase 9, after the repository is public):

7. `gh api repos/SlusserLabs/AnglePlates --jq .visibility` shows `public`.
8. `gh api repos/SlusserLabs/AnglePlates/environments --jq '.environments[] | {name, protection_rules}'` shows `nuget-org` with a `required_reviewers` rule that names only the owner.
9. `gh api repos/SlusserLabs/AnglePlates/environments/nuget-org/deployment-branch-policies` shows `main` and the `v*` tag policy.
10. `gh api repos/SlusserLabs/AnglePlates/rulesets` shows the `main` ruleset with all four checks and the tag ruleset.
11. `gh variable list` shows `NUGET_ORG_PUBLISH_ENABLED`, still `false`.
12. `curl -s https://api.nuget.org/v3-flatcontainer/slusserlabs.angleplates/index.json` returns 404 or lists only the owner's versions.
13. The owner has confirmed the `SlusserLabs.*` prefix reservation, the package-owner account, and an active nuget.org policy for `SlusserLabs/AnglePlates`, `release.yml`, environment `nuget-org`.
14. **Only when the owner asks:** dispatch `release.yml` from `main` with `check-nuget-org: true`; it waits for approval, then `verify-nuget-login` gets a key and pushes nothing. Waiting isn't a failure.

nuget.org has no public API for listing policies, so lines 5 and 13 are the owner's confirmation; lines 6 and 14 are the functional proof.

### 13.6 Preview rehearsal

Before any nuget.org release: publish at least one `1.1.0-preview.N` to int.nugettest.org through `release.yml`; run `validate-package.ps1 -Source` against the int feed; install the preview into a fresh consumer (`dotnet package add SlusserLabs.AnglePlates --prerelease --source https://apiint.nugettest.org/v3/index.json`), build it, and run it. int.nugettest.org may wipe data and has no prefix reservation, so it's rehearsal only. Previews aren't listed in the CHANGELOG; `Unreleased` is what 1.1.0 ships.

### 13.7 Stable release to nuget.org

Entry criteria, in order: every phase up to Phase 8 accepted (including B0 to B6); the repository public; `nuget-org`, `NUGET_ORG_PUBLISH_ENABLED=false`, and the rulesets exist; the prefix reserved and the nuget.org policy created; the stable preflight lines 7 to 13 pass (and 14 if asked); Q1 to Q8 answered (Q5 is the owner's choice about the deferred items, since `plans/` is about to be deleted).

Then:

1. **The release-prep pull request** (agent):
   - Moves the rules from `AnalyzerReleases.Unshipped.md` into `AnalyzerReleases.Shipped.md` under `## Release 1.1.0`.
   - Turns the CHANGELOG's `Unreleased` section into `## 1.1.0`.
   - Clears `VersionSuffix`.
   - Updates the README: the stable install line, NuGet and CI badges, and the icon through an absolute `raw.githubusercontent.com` URL on `main`.
   - Deletes `plans/` (this spec; `plans/icon-draft/` is already gone, [O13](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)).
   - Removes every committed citation of this plan's phases, which would otherwise point at a deleted file: `AGENTS.md` (the Publishing bullet drops "(Phase 1)" and "(Phase 9)" to read "Work goes up as a branch and pull request so CI runs on it. Once the `main` ruleset exists, every change to `main` goes through a pull request."; the Commits bullet and the credential bullet already lost their plan references in Phase B0, [O18](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11) and [O19](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)), `.github/workflows/release.yml` (drop "; see Phase 8" from the owner-inputs error), and `docs/releasing.md` (its quote of that message, and step 1's "For 1.1.0 only, deletes the `plans` folder"). Then `git grep -nE "Phase [0-9]|release spec|plans/"` outside `CHANGELOG.md` finds nothing. (Revision 2's STOP Q, which waited on the owner's commit wording for S41, is gone: O18 settled it.)
   - Records the manual 100% coverage check.
   - The 1.0 version of this list ("`## Release 1.0.0`", "`## 1.0.0`") is **No longer in scope (superseded by 1.1)**.
2. **The owner sets `NUGET_ORG_PUBLISH_ENABLED` to `true`** first, because `publish-nuget` reads it during the run the Release starts.
3. **The owner tags `v1.1.0`** on the merged commit, publishes a GitHub Release with the CHANGELOG section as notes, and **approves the `nuget-org` deployment.**
4. After publishing: set the variable back to `false`; verify help links resolve, Source Link opens the right commit, the README renders on nuget.org with its icon and links, `dotnet package add SlusserLabs.AnglePlates` works, and the Release has the `.nupkg`, `.snupkg`, and `SHA256SUMS`.

### 13.8 Lessons from AssemblyInfo

Binding from 1.0:

| What happened in AssemblyInfo | What this plan does |
| --- | --- |
| Four public betas on nuget.org, each fixing a packaging defect found after publishing. | Previews go only to int.nugettest.org; nothing is pushed before full validation. |
| A bad build burned a version number. | Run-numbered previews; no `--skip-duplicate`. |
| Pushing the `.snupkg` explicitly failed as a duplicate. | Push only `*.nupkg`. |
| `-p:PackageVersion` left assembly versions behind. | Always `-p:Version`. |
| Assets in the wrong folders, missing PDB and XML doc. | Exact-entry, symbol, Source Link, and `project.assets.json` checks. |
| `unzip -l … \| grep -q` under `pipefail` failed on SIGPIPE. | Validate in C#; capture output before grepping in workflow shell steps. |
| The nuspec regex required a `branch` that tag builds lack. | Compare the commit only. |
| Int trusted publishing worked with the int token URL and audience and no environment. | Same settings. |
| Stable from a published Release behind a variable and an environment, explicit tag checkout, tag validation, no cancel-in-progress, assets and `SHA256SUMS` on the Release. | All kept, plus tag equals `VersionPrefix` on `main` and the owner as reviewer. |
| The owner removed a name-collision diagnostic. | No duplicate-class diagnostic (S21); the compiler reports C# problems, the same philosophy as 1.1's parser. |
| Release docs in the owner's voice with exact commands. | `docs/releasing.md` keeps a trigger table, numbered steps, and copy-paste commands. |

## 14. Icon

**Done and approved** (2026-10-14). The mark is two square brackets drawn as solid steel angle plates: blue left, silver right, bolt holes, a soft blue glow, frontal-oblique view (30 degrees, 0.62 foreshortening), `viewBox="0 0 256 256"`, palette in the SVG's `<style>` (blue `#0678DC`/`#3EDCFF`/`#10A0F0`, silver `#68768A`/`#E6EAF0`/`#AAB4C2`, glow `#1E9BFF` at 0.7 with blur 8), one shared `ap-bore` `clipPath`. `icon.svg` (source, never packed) and the 256x256 `icon.png` sit at the repo root. `docs/development.md` "Updating the icon" has the `uv run --with resvg-py==0.5.0 …` render command and the 128 and 32 px check, works from the root `icon.svg`, and must not point at `plans/`; no script is committed. The root `icon.svg` and `icon.png` are the only icon source.

The draft and review renders in `plans/icon-draft/` (`icon.svg`, `icon-512.png`, `preview-sizes.png`, `preview-nuget.png`, `rejected-isometric.png`) are deleted in the commit that adds this spec, together with `plans/release-1.0-spec.md` ([O13](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)). Don't use, restore, or recreate them. The 1.0 instruction "The draft and its review renders stay in `plans/icon-draft` until the release-prep pull request deletes `plans/`" is **No longer in scope (superseded by 1.1).** The 1.0 disposition row "`icon.svg` | Add | 5 | From plans/icon-draft" and 1.0 Appendix A are history. Checked at `6d59e69` with `rg --hidden "icon-draft|plans/"` outside `plans/`: no committed file references `plans/icon-draft` (and `docs/development.md` "Updating the icon" doesn't mention `plans/`), so Phase B4 has nothing to fix; it reruns the check ([Phase B4](#phase-b4-docs-versions-and-prose)) and fixes any hit it finds.

Open for Phase 9: the README shows the icon through an absolute `raw.githubusercontent.com` URL once public (release-prep, agent), and the owner uploads the 1280x640 social-preview image in repository settings (not committed).

## 15. Documentation

### 15.1 Voice and process

Binding:

- README files and every consumer or contributor doc follow AGENTS.md's Author Voice: conversational, technically specific, contractions, concrete examples, direct opinions, first person where it fits, a little dry humor.
- README prose goes through `/humanizer`. Files under `docs/` go through `/docs`, with `/humanizer` for the prose. Code comments, emitted XML docs, and props/targets comments go through `/humanizer`. Never run `/humanizer` on AGENTS.md, plans, or the README's AI Disclosure.
- Plain American English. Write for a junior engineer: say it once, show an example, stop.
- No dated measurements, no "now" or "this pass" language, no regression inventories, no em dashes (use semicolons or periods), no section-sign cross-references (link to the heading).
- Language keywords and type names go in backticks.
- Docs describe what's implemented. The old escape and scanner approach must not appear as "used to" text anywhere except CHANGELOG entries measured against the prototype. Planned work stays out of the docs; for 1.1 it's listed in [Deferred past 1.1](#deferred-past-11), and agents don't file issues for it ([O19](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)).
- Docs change in the same phase as the behavior.
- **The owner signs off on the voice** of `README.md`, `docs/templates.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, and `SECURITY.md` (Q2), in their 1.1 versions.
- **A default names its mechanism** ([O12](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11), [S46](#26-spec-decisions-for-11)): when prose describes a default, it names the exact directive, option, property, parameter, or MSBuild setting that changes it. Not "unless you say otherwise", "unless you set it", or a bare "by default". This binds every new sentence in [15.4](#154-documentation-that-changes-with-11) and the fixes in [15.7](#157-defaults-name-their-mechanism).

### 15.2 Doc set

The 1.0 doc set (S13) is in place: `README.md`, `docs/templates.md`, `docs/rendering.md`, `docs/configuration.md`, `docs/diagnostics.md`, `docs/development.md`, `docs/testing.md`, `docs/releasing.md`, `docs/lexicon.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `SECURITY.md`, `samples/HelloWorld/README.md`, `tests/AnglePlates.PackageTests/README.md`, the issue forms, and the PR template. `tests/AnglePlates.PackageTests/Templates/README.md` is a fixture (don't edit). `tests/AnglePlates.Tests/Snapshots/README.md` stays. Their 1.0 outlines stand, with the 1.1 changes in [15.4](#154-documentation-that-changes-with-11). 1.0 outline items that are **No longer in scope (superseded by 1.1)**:

- `development.md`: "the scanner and the authority of the host lexer". Replaced by a short description of bracket-level parsing and the emitter's hard line.
- `templates.md`: "how blocks end in C#" and "Escaping, including the Markdown `[*` case". Replaced by "How blocks end" (bracket levels, first exact match) and "Writing openers as text".
- README: "Keep the passages that already sound like the owner: the escaping gotcha…". The escaping gotcha goes; "I'd rather fail the build than guess" stays.
- 1.0 section 14.4 "Docs that must change with the scanner" as a whole. Replaced by [15.4](#154-documentation-that-changes-with-11).

### 15.3 README

Binding from 1.0: one README packed as the package readme (S3); every link and image an absolute `https://` URL (the validator enforces it); about 200 lines; previews carry the same README and show the stable install command. The outline stays: title (icon and badges added at release prep), pitch with the T4 analogy and "There are three delimiters and a handful of directives. Everything else is C# you already know." (still true: three kinds of block), quick start, what gets generated, syntax at a glance, rendering, encoding and options, where templates live, Native AOT, trusted code, what it doesn't do, requirements (.NET 10 and C# 11 or later), diagnostics, contributing, AI disclosure, license.

The `## AI Disclosure` section stays word for word as a plain paragraph, never edited or humanized:

> This project was built with the help of Codex 5.6 Sol for code generation. As with any tool I use for coding, I am fully responsible for the end result and only put forward this project with full confidence that I have reviewed every line. Whether a line of code in this project was fully AI generated, AI assisted, or entirely from my own Muppet fingers on the keyboard, it is held to the same expectations.

### 15.4 Documentation that changes with 1.1

Every item lands in Phase B4 unless noted (the AGENTS.md Commits and credential bullets and the matching `docs/releasing.md` guardrail edits land in Phase B0; the AGENTS.md directive-values bullet and `AnalyzerReleases.Unshipped.md` in Phase B1; the AGENTS.md hard-line, generated-code, culture-default, and sample bullets and `samples/HelloWorld/README.md` in Phase B2; the `tests/AnglePlates.PackageTests/README.md` and `ci.yml` edits in Phase B3). Route `docs/` through `/docs` and `/humanizer`; README, CHANGELOG, SECURITY, and comments through `/humanizer`; AGENTS.md by hand.

**`README.md`** (absolute links only):

- "Syntax at a glance": replace the `Escaped opener` row (`\[[1, 2]]`) with `| More brackets | [[[ grid[row[0]] ]]] | Add up to four [ to an opener, and the same number of ] to its closer, when your C# contains the shorter closer. |` (with code spans). Keep the other rows.
- Delete the backslash paragraph ("Watch out for backslashes in front of a C# variable…"). Replace it with an owner-voice tip of two or three sentences: a block ends at the first closer with the same number of brackets, so `[[ a[b[0]] ]]` ends after the `0`; write `[[[ a[b[0]] ]]]`, and `[[% var s = "%]"; %]]` for a C# block. One sentence: a literal `[[` in your text goes through a C# variable, `[[ "[[" ]]`.
- The link sentence: "covers the directives, how blocks end, bracket levels, trimming, how values turn into text, and child templates."
- "What AnglePlates doesn't do": add a fifth bullet in the owner's voice for the new non-goal, for example: "No reading your C#. AnglePlates doesn't parse the C# inside a block. A block ends at the first closer of its bracket level, so raise the level when your C# contains that closer."
- If `README.md` gains a template example under a `<!-- sample: … -->` marker, add its copy under `tests/AnglePlates.Runtime.Tests/Templates/Readme` and to `ReadmeExampleTests._samples["README.md"]`.
- Culture ([4.11](#411-culture)), "Syntax at a glance": the C# variable row's "Converts any C# expression to text with the invariant culture, encodes it, and writes it." becomes, for example, "Converts any C# expression to text with the invariant culture (set `TemplateOptions.Culture` to change it), encodes it, and writes it."
- Culture, [O12](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11), and [O16](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11), "Encoding and options" (lines 152 to 161 at `6d59e69`): rewrite the first paragraph so each default names what changes it and nothing mentions `#encode` or presets, for example: "Literal text is written unchanged. C# variables go through `HtmlEncoder.Default` until you set `TemplateOptions.Encoder` in the options you create the template with; `null` turns encoding off. Numbers and dates convert with the invariant culture until you set `TemplateOptions.Culture`. A null C# variable writes `TemplateOptions.NullVariable`, which is empty until you set it." Delete the `#encode` paragraph, the `TemplateOptions.Unencoded` example, and "The presets are…". Show the options in one example: `new TemplateOptions { Encoder = null }` for a plain-text email, and `Culture = CultureInfo.GetCultureInfo("de-DE")` in the `custom` example or a third line.
- [O12](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11) and O16, the pitch paragraph (line 7 at `6d59e69`): "C# variables are HTML-encoded by default" names its switch, for example "C# variables are HTML-encoded until you set `TemplateOptions.Encoder`". The [15.7](#157-defaults-name-their-mechanism) checklist covers the rest of the file.
- The directives part of "Syntax at a glance" or "Quick start", wherever the README lists directives: no `#encode`, and `#using` isn't described as "plain namespace names only".

**`docs/templates.md`**:

- Intro: "directives, syntax, bracket levels, trimming, …" instead of "escaping".
- Directives ([O16](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11), [O17](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11), [4.2](#42-directives)): delete the `#encode` row from the directive table (line 18 at `6d59e69`) and the whole `### Picking an encoder with #encode` section (lines 39 to 41); point at the renamed Options section (`rendering.md#options`) once, where the table says how output is encoded. In the table, `#type` drops "Must be a C# identifier", `#namespace` drops "A dotted namespace name", and `#using`'s "Plain namespace names only; no static, aliases, or global::" becomes "Copied after `using`, so `static`, aliases, and `global::` work". Replace the sentence about SLAP001 and SLAP002 (line 37) with: a name that isn't valid C#, given or derived (for example `2fa-code.html`), is a compiler error in the generated file, so add `#type` or `#namespace`. Add one sentence each: directive values are copied into the generated code as written, so a mistake shows up as a compiler error on the generated `using`, `namespace`, or class line rather than at the directive; comments don't belong on directive lines; and directive values are trusted like the rest of the template's C#. A blank value still reports `SLAP007` (link it to `diagnostics.md#slap007`).
- Child templates (line 202 at `6d59e69`): "rendering the parent with `TemplateOptions.Unencoded`" becomes "rendering the parent with `new TemplateOptions { Encoder = null }`".
- `## Syntax`: keep the five rows; add one or two sentences: an opener can repeat its `[` up to four times, and its closer repeats `]` as often; `[* c *]` and `[[[* c *]]]` are the same, and so are `[[ x ]]` and `[[[[ x ]]]]`; C# variables use two to four brackets, C# blocks and comments one to four.
- `### How blocks end`: rewrite entirely. A block ends at the first closer with the same number of brackets as its opener (`]]`, `%]`, `*]`, then `]]]`, `%]]`, `*]]`, and so on). AnglePlates doesn't read your C#, so strings and comments don't hide a closer. Examples: `[[ a[b[0]] ]]` closes early, write `[[[ a[b[0]] ]]]`; `[% var s = "%]"; %]` closes inside the string, write `[[% var s = "%]"; %]]`; `[[ model[0..1]]]` leaves a stray `]`, write `[[[ model[0..1] ]]]`. More than four `[` in a row: the last four open the block and the rest are text, so `[[[[[ x ]]]]]` writes `[`, the value, and `]`. A single `[` not followed by `%` or `*` is text. `[[*ptr ]]` opens a comment, so write `[[ *ptr ]]`. `[% // note %]` and `[% #if DEBUG %]` work, because each C# block lands on its own lines. A C# variable that ends in `//` doesn't compile; use `/* */` or leave the comment out. A block with no closer at its level reports `SLAP004` (linked to `diagnostics.md#slap004`) at the opener. Add one `<!-- sample: Templates/Readme/levels.html -->` block with a short example (its first line is `#model string`, with no other directive, per [11.3](#113-runtime-tests)) and its output, copied verbatim to `tests/AnglePlates.Runtime.Tests/Templates/Readme/levels.html` with a test. Delete every mention of the lexer, windows, 15 or 16 closers, and "Fifteen is plenty".
- `## Escaping`: delete the whole section (backslash table, Handlebars note, `C:\out\` gotcha, the `\[*Important*]` tip, "Escapes only apply to literal text"). Add `## Writing openers as text` with the content of [Writing openers as text](#writing-openers-as-text): closers and single `[` are already text; write openers through a C# variable or `WriteRaw`; the JavaScript `[[1, 2], [3, 4]]`, Bash `[[ ]]`, TOML `[[table]]`, and Markdown `[*Important*](…)` examples; the encoder note (HTML and JavaScript encoding leave brackets alone; URL encoding writes `[` as `%5B` and `]` as `%5D`, so use `WriteRaw` when `TemplateOptions.Encoder` is `UrlEncoder.Default`); and that `\[[` writes a backslash and then the C# variable.
- `## Trimming`: keep it; add one example at another level (`[[[- model.Title -]]]`, `[[%- … -%]]`) and say the marker sits right after the full opener and right before the full closer.
- `## Compiler errors`: add that a block cut short by a closer inside its C# shows up as a compiler error at the template location (raise the bracket level), and that a C# variable ending in `//` fails to compile, and one of its errors may point into the generated file (use `/* */`). Errors on any line of a multi-line block map to the exact template line and column.
- `## How values become text` (culture, [4.11](#411-culture)): "Everything else converts the way `Convert.ToString(value, CultureInfo.InvariantCulture)` would" becomes "the way `Convert.ToString(value, culture)` would, where `culture` is the template's `TemplateOptions.Culture`"; the `IConvertible` and `IFormattable` bullets say "with `TemplateOptions.Culture`" instead of "with the invariant culture" and `ToString(null, culture)` instead of `ToString(null, CultureInfo.InvariantCulture)`. "So `[[ 1234.5m ]]` writes `1234.5` on every machine, whatever culture the server runs in" stays true for the default, so say: `Culture` is `CultureInfo.InvariantCulture` until you set it, so `[[ 1234.5m ]]` writes `1234.5` on every machine; create the template with `new TemplateOptions { Culture = CultureInfo.GetCultureInfo("de-DE") }` and it writes `1234,5`. Replace the "format the value yourself" advice and its `[[ model.Total.ToString("C", model.Culture) ]]` example: for a culture, set `Culture`; for a format string such as currency, format in the template with the template's own culture, `[[ model.Total.ToString("C", Options.Culture) ]]`, which with `de-DE` and `1234.5m` writes `1.234,50 &#x20AC;` (keep the euro-sign sentence). Add one sentence: C# that calls `ToString()` or writes to `writer` itself doesn't go through `Culture`, so pass `Options.Culture` there too. Link the Options section (`rendering.md#options`) for the per-request pattern (the heading is renamed from "Options and presets", below).
- `## How values become text`, null paragraph ([O12](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)): "writes the `NullVariable` option, which is nothing unless you set it" becomes "writes `TemplateOptions.NullVariable`, which is empty until you set it in the options you pass to the template".
- Child templates: one sentence that a child created with `new(Options)` also inherits the parent's `Culture`.

**`docs/diagnostics.md`**:

- `## SLAP004`: rewrite. A C# variable, C# block, or comment has no closer with the same number of brackets after it. The message names the closer it expected, such as `]]]` for `[[[`. Keep the three-line `<p>Hello, [[ model.Name </p>` example and "The squiggle covers the opener." Add a level-mismatch example (`[[[ model.Name ]]` expects `]]]`). Delete the rest: the lexer bullets, the 16-closer case, the `CodeLists` example, the `"%" + "]"` workaround, and "the scanner never reads `.cs` files".
- `## Mistakes without a diagnostic`: add bullets: a closer inside your C# ends the block early, which shows up as a compiler error at the template (add brackets); `[[*` opens a comment; more than four `[` writes the extras as text; `\[[` writes a backslash and then the C# variable; a literal `[` right before an opener raises its level; a directive value that isn't valid C# (including a derived `#type` or `#namespace`) is a compiler error in the generated file; a leftover `#encode` line ends the directive section.
- `## SLAP001` and `## SLAP002` ([S51](#26-spec-decisions-for-11)): keep each heading and its bold title, and replace the body (lines 9 to 35 at `6d59e69`) with "**Retired in 1.1.**" and one sentence: AnglePlates no longer checks derived names, so a name that isn't valid C# shows up as a compiler error in the generated file; add `#type` or `#namespace`.
- `## SLAP007` (line 123 at `6d59e69`): it fires for a blank value of any directive, an empty derived `#type`, `#generate` other than `Text` or `Utf8`, and `#access` other than `public` or `internal`. Drop the encoder values and every C# name or type check from the section.

**`docs/development.md`**:

- "Where the generator code lives" Parsing row: list `TemplateParser`, `TemplateSyntax`, and the token types; remove `TemplateReader` and `CSharpBlockScanner`.
- `### Parsing`: rewrite to the algorithm in [7.2](#72-the-algorithm) in prose (find `[`, count the run, cap at four with extras as text, the next character picks the kind, build the closer from the level, one ordinal `IndexOf`, trim markers, SLAP004 when there's no closer, cancellation per block). Update which classes use `TemplateSyntax` (drop `TemplateReader` and `CSharpBlockScanner`). Add the hard line in one or two sentences: the parser and the class writer never read the C# inside a block; the template author picks the bracket level. Delete the four paragraphs on `FindCloser`, windows, -1 cases, `CountBraces`, `EndsWithLineComment`, the host lexer, and `ScannerParity`.
- `### Emitting C#`: drop "escaped openers" from the literal-run sentence. Replace "C# blocks are copied into the render method as they are" with the C0 layout: the C# of every C# block and C# variable is copied at the generated type's indent (column 0), line breaks unchanged, so `#line` maps every line exactly; no brace-based indentation and no `//` handling. Recheck the `#line` sentences in "Two render methods from one walk", and rewrite its excerpt in the C0 layout: template C# lines (`foreach (var row in model.Rows) {`, `Write(row, writer);`, `}`) at column 0, the generator's own statements (`await WriteLiteralAsync(...)`, `WriteLiteral(...)`, `await FlushAsync(...)`) at one constant indent with no extra indent inside the braces, and the comment lines adjusted to match. Copy the layout from the regenerated `RenderAsync` snapshot.
- "How MSBuild hands over templates": "Before 1.0 they shipped in `buildTransitive`" becomes "Before the first release they shipped in `buildTransitive`".
- Directives ([O16](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11), [O17](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)): the pipeline step "Read and validate the directives" (line 58 at `6d59e69`) becomes "Read the directives"; the Directives paragraph (line 89) drops `TemplateEncoding`, the presets, `#encode none`, and the validation and derivation errors, and says instead that `DirectiveReader` copies values as written, derives `#type` and `#namespace` with plain string work, and reports only blank values, `#generate`, `#access`, a duplicate, and a missing `#model`; line 93's "falls back to the preset that `#encode` picked" becomes "falls back to `new TemplateOptions()`"; line 136's "four presets" goes. Add one sentence to "Where the generator code lives" or the hint-name passage: hint names are sanitized with plain string work because directive values aren't validated ([S50](#26-spec-decisions-for-11)), and every generated file keeps its `// <auto-generated>` header and `.g.cs` name so consumer analyzers skip it ([S54](#26-spec-decisions-for-11)).
- `### Emitting C#`, culture ([4.11](#411-culture)): "converts the value the way `Convert.ToString(value, CultureInfo.InvariantCulture)` does" becomes "converts the value the way `Convert.ToString(value, Options.Culture)` does", and add that `RenderToString` creates its `StringWriter` with `Options.Culture`. The `TableBenchmarks` baseline row ("formats each value … with the invariant culture") stays, because the benchmarks use the default options.
- "Updating the icon": keep it pointing at the root `icon.svg` and `icon.png`; it must not mention `plans/` ([O13](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11); it doesn't at `6d59e69`).
- "Validating the package": `1.1.0-local.<yyyyMMdd>.<n>` and `1.1.0-local.20261014.1` in the text, the pack command, the validate command, and the docker command; "derived names, the scanner, ASP.NET Core streaming" becomes "derived names, bracket levels, ASP.NET Core streaming".

**`docs/lexicon.md`**:

- Opener: "One to four `[` followed by `%` (C# block) or `*` (comment), or two to four `[` (C# variable), as in `[%`, `[[[*`, or `[[`. A `[` beyond four is literal text."
- Closing delimiter: "The `]`, `%]`, or `*]` sequence that ends a block, with as many `]` as its opener has `[`, as in `]]`, `%]]`, or `*]]]]`. Call it the closer for short. The first one in the text ends the block." Delete the scanner sentence.
- Add "Bracket level": the number of `[` in an opener, matched by the same number of `]` in its closer; one to four for C# blocks and comments, two to four for C# variables; write "level 3" or "a level-3 C# variable"; don't call it depth, nesting, or escaping.
- Delete "Escaped opener".
- Trim marker: keep; optionally add a level-3 example (`[[[-`).
- Literal run: remove "escaped openers".
- Preset (line 50 at `6d59e69`): delete the row; there are no presets ([O16](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)). (Revision 2's rewording under O12 is **No longer in scope (superseded by 1.1).**) If another lexicon row mentions `#encode` or a preset, reword it to `TemplateOptions.Encoder`.
- Add "Culture" ([4.11](#411-culture)): "The `CultureInfo` in `TemplateOptions.Culture` that C# variables convert with, such as `1,5` for `1.5` under `de-DE`. It's `CultureInfo.InvariantCulture` until the options set `Culture`. Say 'culture', not 'locale' or 'format provider'."

Keep the "owner inputs pending; see Phase 8" quote. The tag-pattern text stays generic, and "Bump `VersionPrefix` to the next planned version" stays. Look for `"push": true` and `"admin": false`.") drops the `"admin": false` requirement, in the owner's voice, for example "The account can push. Look for `"push": true`. **`docs/diagnostics.md`** ([O12](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)), in addition to the SLAP004 edits above: "Every rule … is enabled by default" names how to change a rule's severity, for example "Every rule is on until you change its severity with `dotnet_diagnostic.SLAPnnn.severity` in `.editorconfig`". Confirm that `.editorconfig` severity actually applies to these generator diagnostics before writing it (a scratch build outside the repository is enough; don't commit it); if it doesn't, name whatever does, or say plainly that the severity is fixed.

**`docs/rendering.md`** (culture, [4.11](#411-culture), and [O16](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11); required in 1.1):

- "Default or new" (line 34 at `6d59e69`): drop "which `#encode` picks" and "the defaults are the shared presets"; a template created without options uses `new TemplateOptions()`, and `Default` is one shared instance per template class.
- Rename the heading "Options and presets" to "Options" (anchor `#options`) and fix every inbound link (`templates.md`'s link from the deleted "Picking an encoder" section goes with it; the new culture link in `templates.md` uses `#options`). Delete the preset table (lines 47 to 54). "`TemplateOptions` has two properties" becomes three; add a row `| Culture | CultureInfo.InvariantCulture | Converts C# variables that aren't strings, such as numbers and dates. null throws ArgumentNullException. |` (with code spans), between `Encoder` and `NullVariable`, and say `Encoder` and `Culture` are independent. Show the common options in one example: `new TemplateOptions { Encoder = null }` (unencoded), `new TemplateOptions { Encoder = JavaScriptEncoder.Default }`, and `new TemplateOptions { Culture = CultureInfo.GetCultureInfo("de-DE") }`.
- Add the per-request localization pattern from [4.11](#411-culture) (one options object and template per culture, cached by culture name and built with `CultureInfo.GetCultureInfo(name)`; options and templates are immutable and thread safe, so sharing is fine), and say why there's no culture argument on `Render`: options travel to child templates on their own. There's no preset trap to warn about anymore ([S47](#26-spec-decisions-for-11) withdrawn): `new TemplateOptions { Culture = culture }` encodes HTML like every template's default, and a plain-text template sets `Encoder = null` in the same initializer.
- Child template options (line 132 at `6d59e69`) and the lone-surrogate table (lines 140 and 141): the `Html` and `Unencoded` rows become "An encoder (the default)" and "`Encoder = null`".
- Extend "Options only affect C# variables and `Write` calls" to name the culture too, and say C# that calls `ToString()` or `writer.Write(...)` itself doesn't use `Culture` unless it passes `Options.Culture`; `RenderToString`'s `StringWriter` uses `Options.Culture`.
- "Child template options": a child created with `new(Options)` inherits the parent's `Culture` along with its encoder; one called through `Default` keeps the invariant culture.
- "Viewing the generated code" ([O14](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11), [8.7](#87-generated-code-and-consumer-analyzers)): one sentence saying that template C# shows up at column 0 with its own line breaks, which is why it looks unindented, and two sentences on analyzers: generated files start with `// <auto-generated>` and end in `.g.cs`, so analyzers that exclude generated code, as the built-in IDE and CA analyzers and StyleCop do, skip them (which also means template C# gets compiler warnings but no IDE or CA analysis), and turning that off with `generated_code = false` isn't supported.

**`CHANGELOG.md`** (`/humanizer`, owner voice; entries are measured against the 0.1 prototype, which ended blocks at the first plain closer and had backslash escapes; keeping the prototype-relative Removed and Changed entries is [S42](#26-spec-decisions-for-11), which the owner can drop under Q2):

- `## Unreleased` intro: "This is the road to 1.1.0, the first release on nuget.org. Nothing before it was published, so the list below is measured against the 0.1 prototype builds some of you tried from source." Whether to add a parenthetical about why there's no 1.0.0 is the owner's voice call (Q2); default: no parenthetical. Leave the `keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0` link alone.
- `### Added`: "Openers and closers can repeat their brackets up to four times, as in `[[[ grid[row[0]] ]]]` or `[[% var s = "%]"; %]]`, so C# that contains a closer still fits in a block."
- `### Added` (culture, [4.11](#411-culture)): "`TemplateOptions.Culture` picks the culture C# variables convert with, so `[[ model.Total ]]` can write `1234,5` for a German page. It's `CultureInfo.InvariantCulture` until you set it, so output doesn't depend on the server's culture. Child templates created with `new(Options)` pick it up too." If an existing CHANGELOG entry says C# variables always use the invariant culture, reword it to match (none did at `6d59e69`).
- `### Changed`, the `TextEncoder` entry ([O12](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11), [15.7](#157-defaults-name-their-mechanism), [O16](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)): "HTML encoding is still the default." names the property, for example "HTML encoding is still the default; set `TemplateOptions.Encoder` to change it."
- [O16](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11): delete the `### Added` entries for `#encode` (line 12 at `6d59e69`) and for the presets (line 13), and the `### Changed` entry "A template created without options uses the preset its `#encode` picks…" (line 25). The prototype had neither, so no Removed entry is needed.
- [O17](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11): the entry that lists "SLAP001 through SLAP011" (line 36 at `6d59e69`) becomes "SLAP003 through SLAP011" (or names the shipped IDs), because SLAP001 and SLAP002 are retired before release. No "validation removed" entry: the prototype at `8410d49` didn't validate directive values with Roslyn. If an Added entry describes optional `#type` and `#namespace`, add that a name that isn't valid C# is a compiler error in the generated file.
- `### Changed`: delete the two lexer entries ("C# variables and C# blocks end where the C# lexer says…" and "A C# variable or C# block with no closer the lexer accepts reports `SLAP004`…"). Add: `[[%` and `[[*` now open level-2 C# blocks and comments; they used to open a C# variable whose C# started with `%` or `*`, so `[[*ptr ]]` (a pointer dereference) must now be written `[[ *ptr ]]`; a `[` right before an opener raises its level; runs of more than four `[` keep the extras as text; generated C# blocks are no longer re-indented around braces; template C# now sits at column 0 of the generated file and keeps the template's own line breaks.
- `### Removed`: add "Backslash escapes (`\[[`, `\[%`, `\[*`). Write an opener as text through a C# variable such as `[[ "[[" ]]`. A backslash in front of a C# variable is plain text now."
- `### Fixed`: delete "A C# variable that ends in a `//` comment compiles." and "Braces inside C# strings no longer throw off the indentation of generated code." (Neither shipped; a `//`-ending variable didn't compile in the prototype either.)

**`AGENTS.md`** (Phase B0 for the Commits and credential bullets, Phase B1 for directive values, Phase B2 for the hard-line bullet, the culture default, the sample bullet, and generated-code detection, Phase B4 for Scope and the Author Voice bullet; by hand, concise; don't cite spec decisions or phases in new text, because they would dangle after release prep):

- Commits (Phase B0, [O18](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)): "Agents may commit and push to the remote repository while working through the release spec. Only the owner makes the repository public or approves a nuget.org publish." becomes "Agents may commit and push to the remote repository as they work. Only the owner makes the repository public or approves a nuget.org publish."
- Publishing (Phase B0, [O19](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)): replace the "From Phase 8 on, agents use their own GitHub credential…" and "Agent credential permissions…" bullets with the one bullet in [13.4](#134-agent-guardrails). The other Publishing bullets stay.
- Add to "Generated Code and Rendering" (Phase B2): "The parser and class writer never interpret the C# inside a C# variable or C# block: no lexing and no bracket, brace, string, comment, or directive detection. A block ends at the first closer of its bracket level, and the writer copies the C# under one `#line` mapping at the generated type's indent (column 0), line breaks unchanged."
- Add to "Generated Code and Rendering" (Phase B1, [O17](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)): "Directive values are copied into the generated code without C# validation; the compiler reports invalid names and types. The generator checks only blank values (including an empty derived `#type`) and the values AnglePlates defines (`#generate`, `#access`). Hint names are sanitized with plain string work."
- Add to "Generated Code and Rendering" (Phase B2, [O14](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)): "Every generated file starts with the `// <auto-generated>` header and has a `.g.cs` hint name, so analyzers that exclude generated code skip it; never emit `#pragma warning disable`, which would hide compiler warnings from template C#."
- Current Repository (Phase B2, [O16](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)): "`Templates/welcome-email.txt` shows `#encode none` with `RenderToString`" becomes "`Templates/welcome-email.txt` shows unencoded output through `new TemplateOptions { Encoder = null }` with `RenderToString`".
- Extend the Scope bullet (Phase B4) so it names every permanent non-goal from [1](#non-goals): "T4 syntax and `.tt` files, runtime template compilation, a runtime package, IntelliSense inside template files, and C#-aware parsing or emission of embedded C# are out of scope. Don't add them."
- Leave the `#line` bullet as it is; C0 keeps mappings exact.
- In "Generated Code and Rendering" (Phase B2, with the culture code): "Templates contain trusted C#; preserve default C# variable encoding and explicit raw-output behavior" becomes "Templates contain trusted C#; preserve default C# variable encoding, the invariant default of `TemplateOptions.Culture`, and explicit raw-output behavior".
- Add to "Author Voice" (Phase B4, [S46](#26-spec-decisions-for-11)), as a new bullet at the end of the section, after the paragraph that starts "Keep the writing conversational": "- When prose describes a default, name the exact directive, option, property, parameter, or MSBuild setting that changes it; don't write 'unless you say otherwise', 'unless you set it', or a bare 'by default'." `.editorconfig` can't express a writing rule, so nothing is added there.
- Nothing else: the Publishing bullet's phase citations stay until release prep (S34); the rest of the Author Voice stays.

### 15.5 Link and drift checks

Binding: `DocumentationLinkTests` checks that every relative link and anchor under `docs/` and in `CHANGELOG.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, and `SECURITY.md` resolves, that `README.md` has no relative links, and that every diagnostic help anchor exists. `ReadmeExampleTests` checks marked samples. Renaming `## Escaping` removes `templates.md#escaping`; fix every inbound link (none existed at `6d59e69`; `diagnostics.md`'s link to `#how-blocks-end` is deleted with the SLAP004 rewrite, and a new one may be added). Renaming "Options and presets" removes `rendering.md#options-and-presets`; fix every inbound link to `rendering.md#options` ([O16](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)). Deleting `### Picking an encoder with #encode` removes its anchor; check nothing links to it.

### 15.6 Terminology

Keep "C# variable" and `NullVariable` (S19); `docs/templates.md` keeps its one sentence saying a C# variable can be any expression. Add "bracket level" ([15.4](#154-documentation-that-changes-with-11)). Say "closer" for short, "level-3 C# variable" for a block's level, and never "escape" for anything in the 1.1 syntax. Add "Culture" ([4.11](#411-culture)); say "culture", not "locale" or "format provider". Remove "Preset" ([O16](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)); say "options" or name the property.

### 15.7 Defaults name their mechanism

[O12](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11) and [S46](#26-spec-decisions-for-11). When human-facing prose (README files, `docs/`, CHANGELOG, CONTRIBUTING, SECURITY, the package-tests README, and emitted XML docs) describes a default, it names the exact directive, option, property, parameter, or MSBuild setting that changes it. Phrases like "unless you say otherwise", "unless you set it", "unless you give it another one", or a bare "by default" fail the rule. A phrase is fine when the same sentence names the mechanism ("`internal` until you set `AnglePlateBaseAccessModifier` to `public`").

**Current offenders**, verified with `rg -n -i "unless you|says otherwise|by default"` over `README.md`, `docs`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, `SECURITY.md`, `samples`, `tests/AnglePlates.PackageTests/README.md`, `src/AnglePlates/build`, and `src/AnglePlates/Emit/SupportTypes.cs` (the emitted XML docs) at `6d59e69`; Phase B5 greps the same paths. Line numbers drift; find each by its text. The B4 docs pass fixes every one (the suggested wording is in [15.4](#154-documentation-that-changes-with-11)):

| File | Line at `6d59e69` | Text | Fix names |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `README.md` | 7 | "C# variables are HTML-encoded by default" | `TemplateOptions.Encoder` ([O16](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11); revision 2 named `#encode`) |
| `README.md` | 152 | "C# variables go through `HtmlEncoder.Default` unless you say otherwise" | `TemplateOptions.Encoder` (no `#encode`, no presets) |
| `README.md` | 152 | "a null C# variable writes nothing unless you set `NullVariable`" | `TemplateOptions.NullVariable` |
| `docs/lexicon.md` | 50 | "`Html` unless the template says otherwise" | None: the Preset row is deleted (O16) |
| `docs/templates.md` | 123 | "which is nothing unless you set it" | `TemplateOptions.NullVariable` |
| `docs/testing.md` | 39 | "unless you give it another one" | `RenderingTestHelper.Render`'s `model` parameter |
| `docs/diagnostics.md` | 5 | "is enabled by default" | `dotnet_diagnostic.SLAPnnn.severity` in `.editorconfig` (verify first) |
| `docs/configuration.md` | 71 | "They're `internal` by default, just like your compiled templates" | `AnglePlateBaseAccessModifier` (support types; named only in the next paragraph today) and `#access public` (compiled templates) |
| `CHANGELOG.md` | 30 | "HTML encoding is still the default." (found by the wider read below, not the phrase search) | `TemplateOptions.Encoder` (for example "HTML encoding is still the default; set `TemplateOptions.Encoder` to change it") |

**Hits judged fine** at `6d59e69` (they aren't defaults, or the same sentence names the mechanism); leave them:

- `README.md` 193: "A .NET 10 project gets a newer C# version by default, so this only matters if you set `LangVersion`" (names `LangVersion`).
- `SECURITY.md` 7: "unless you'd rather I didn't" (not a default).
- `docs/testing.md` 63: "The provider sets it by default. Use `WithoutProps()` or `WithPropsImported(value)`" (names both methods).
- `docs/testing.md` 163: "the collector leaves the test assembly out by default" (the same sentence names `IncludeTestAssembly`).

**Wider search.** The phrase list can't catch every way of hiding a default, so B4 also reads each "default" mention in the touched docs (`rg -n -i "default" README.md docs CHANGELOG.md`) and fixes any that describe a default without naming what changes it. A first pass at `6d59e69` found `CHANGELOG.md` 30 (listed above) and judged two hits fine, because each sentence names its setting: `docs/diagnostics.md` 173 ("Set `LangVersion` to 11 or later, or remove it so the .NET 10 SDK picks its default") and `docs/lexicon.md` 38 (the "Generator setting" row, about MSBuild properties such as `AnglePlateBaseAccessModifier`). New 1.1 prose follows the rule from the start, including the culture text ("the invariant culture; set `TemplateOptions.Culture` to change it").

**AGENTS.md** gets the Author Voice bullet in [15.4](#154-documentation-that-changes-with-11). `.editorconfig` can't express a writing rule, so the CLAUDE.md habit of mirroring formatting rules there doesn't apply.

**Icon references** ([O13](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)): the same B4 pass reruns `rg -n --hidden "icon-draft|plans/" --glob "!plans/**" --glob "!.git/**"`. It found nothing at `6d59e69`; fix any hit that points a reader at `plans/icon-draft`. (A `plans/` mention that isn't about the icon, if one appears, belongs to the release-prep cleanup in [13.7](#137-stable-release-to-nugetorg) and stays until then.)

Phase B5 checks the result with a grep ([Phase B5](#phase-b5-self-audit-measurements-and-the-pull-request)).

## 16. Repository hygiene

### 16.1 `.editorconfig`

Binding from 1.0: fixture sections `[{tests,benchmarks,samples}/**/{Templates,Emails}/**]` and `[tests/AnglePlates.PackageTests/{CustomViews,Explicit}/**]` with `end_of_line = unset`, `insert_final_newline = unset`, `trim_trailing_whitespace = false`; `[*.ps1]` with `indent_size = 4` and `end_of_line = lf`; `svg` in the two-space group; new rules at warning severity. 1.1 adds nothing.

### 16.2 `.gitattributes`

Binding from 1.0: `*.md text eol=crlf`, `*.ps1 text eol=lf`, `*.svg text eol=crlf`, `*.dic text eol=crlf`, `*.png binary`, `**/Templates/**/*.txt -text`, `**/Templates/**/*.md -text`, `*.verified.cs`/`*.verified.txt` LF, and `*.html -text`. New 1.1 fixtures (`.html`) keep their committed bytes; write `multiline-lf.html` with LF and `multiline-crlf.html` with CRLF on purpose. `plans/release-1.1-spec.md` is CRLF in the working tree; the copy that lands there is converted to CRLF (or renormalized) before it's committed.

### 16.3 `exclusion.dic`

Cleaned in 1.0 (29 words). For 1.1 see [15.4](#154-documentation-that-changes-with-11).

### 16.4 Solution, ignore rules, and local state

`AnglePlates.slnx` doesn't list `plans/` or any deleted file; check after deleting files that nothing in it points at them. `.gitignore` needs no change (`TestResults/`, `artifacts/`, `*.user`, `BenchmarkDotNet.Artifacts`, `.vs/` are covered). Before packing, clear older package pairs from `artifacts/package` so it holds only the version under validation.

### 16.5 AGENTS.md changes

AGENTS.md changes in the same phase as the work it describes; otherwise the next agent "fixes" the change back. For 1.1: the Commits and credential bullets ([O18](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11), [O19](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)) land in Phase B0, the directive-values bullet ([O17](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)) in Phase B1, and the generated-code bullet ([O14](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)) and the Current Repository sample wording ([O16](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)) in Phase B2; the hard-line bullet ([15.4](#154-documentation-that-changes-with-11)) and the culture-default wording land with the emitter and support-type change in Phase B2, and the extended Scope bullet and the Author Voice "name the mechanism" bullet ([S46](#26-spec-decisions-for-11)) land in Phase B4 with the docs they govern. All exact wording is in [15.4](#154-documentation-that-changes-with-11). 1.0's AGENTS.md rows (scope, commits, publishing, local version format, "compile-time template engine", diagnostics registry, one support-type snapshot, coverage gate, `build/`, doc routing, `testing.md` wording, release tracking, sample wording, absolute README links, ".NET 10 includes `System.IO.Pipelines`") are all in place and stay, apart from the 1.1 edits listed above and in [15.4](#154-documentation-that-changes-with-11).

### 16.6 `plans` folder

`plans/release-1.1-spec.md` replaces `plans/release-1.0-spec.md`. The 1.1 pull request links to it. `plans/icon-draft/` is deleted in the same commit ([O13](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)), so the spec is the only file left in `plans/`. The 1.1.0 release-prep pull request deletes `plans/` and the phase citations ([13.7](#137-stable-release-to-nugetorg)).

### 16.7 OSS files

In place: `.github/dependabot.yml` (NuGet weekly, TUnit and Verify grouped, BenchmarkDotNet included, `Microsoft.CodeAnalysis*` ignored because the 4.14 host floor is deliberate; GitHub Actions weekly), the issue forms, the PR template, and the yearly Roslyn-floor check in `docs/releasing.md` (the floor still matters for the generator host, no longer for lexer parity). Still open: `.github/CODEOWNERS` in Phase 8 with the owner's handle; private vulnerability reporting when the repository goes public (owner).

### 16.8 Version wording

Every committed "1.0.0" that means the next release becomes "1.1.0" in Phase B4: `Directory.Build.props`, `docs/releasing.md`, `docs/development.md`, `docs/configuration.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `SECURITY.md`, `.github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/bug.yml`. Leave: `PackagedLibrary`'s two fixture versions (`App/App.csproj`'s `Version="1.0.0"` and `Library/Library.csproj`'s `<Version>1.0.0</Version>`), the `## Release 1.0` fixture strings in `DiagnosticTests` and `DirectiveSpellCheckTests`, the Keep a Changelog `1.1.0` link, and numeric literals like `1.0 / 3`. The "pre-1.0" and "1.0" comment wording in `SupportTypes.cs` (Phase B2, with its four snapshots), `ConversionTests.cs`, and `OutputTests.cs` (Phase B3), and the `Pre10` method rename, follow [S37](#26-spec-decisions-for-11); Phase B5 checks them.
