## 18. Execution plan

Rules for every phase: work goes up on the 1.1 branch and one pull request to `main` with CI on all four legs. The pull request is opened as a draft right after the first push in Phase B0, so CI runs on every phase push (`ci.yml` runs only on pull requests and pushes to `main`); AGENTS.md requires that a phase's work goes up as a branch and pull request so CI runs on it. 1.0's Phases 0 to 7 skipped phase pull requests only because `gh` wasn't available; it is now. 1.0's rule "Each phase is one or more PRs" is **No longer in scope (superseded by 1.1)** for Phases B0 to B5, which share one draft pull request with CI on every phase push ([S35](#26-spec-decisions-for-11)). Phases 8 and 9 keep it. Agents may commit and push ([O18](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)); only the owner makes the repository public or approves a nuget.org publish. A phase can take as many commits as it needs, as long as every commit builds; each commit that finishes a checklist item ticks it in this file ([O19](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11), [How to use this spec](#how-to-use-this-spec)). Every phase's acceptance includes the code-quality items in [6.3](#63-comments-style-and-skills) and its AGENTS.md changes. The order protects the snapshot review: the generated code changes once, in one batch, with one review.

### Phases 0 to 7 (history)

Done in 1.0; see [Implementation status](#implementation-status). Their rules still bind: Phase 0 (fixture bytes never change on renormalize; AGENTS.md scope and publishing rules), Phase 1 (four green legs, failure binlog upload, `sdk-floor` on 10.0.100), Phase 2 (only reviewed snapshot diffs; emitted bytes identical otherwise), Phase 4 (output-parity matrix, Runtime.Tests in CI, 90% coverage, NativeAot with doc generation, docs free of removed behavior), Phase 5 (validation on four legs with every consumer; icon, README, assets, and commit checks), Phase 6 (link, anchor, and drift tests; `git grep -niE "runtime version|prototype|roadmap\.md|performance\.md" -- README.md docs samples` and `git grep -nE "# TODO|TODO:" -- README.md docs` find nothing; every `buildTransitive` mention explains the move; the AI disclosure verbatim), Phase 7 (`release.yml` stops at `publish-int` with "owner inputs pending").

Phase 3 ("C#-aware scanner": section 7 in full, the `//` fix, lexer brace counting, the scanner docs, and its acceptance including 100% branch coverage of `CSharpBlockScanner`) is **No longer in scope (superseded by 1.1).** Phases B1 to B4 replace it.

### Phase B0: baselines and housekeeping

1. Check out the 1.1 branch ([How to use this spec](#how-to-use-this-spec)). `git status` is clean. If the only uncommitted changes are adding `plans/release-1.1-spec.md`, deleting `plans/release-1.0-spec.md`, and deleting `plans/icon-draft/` ([O13](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)), convert the spec to CRLF and commit all three as `Spec: replace the 1.0 spec with the 1.1 spec` first. If `plans/icon-draft/` is still there and everything else matches, delete it in that same commit. Any other uncommitted change: STOP.
2. Record baselines for the pull request (not committed): the line counts in [6.7](#67-simplification-targets); `dotnet run --project samples/HelloWorld -c Release --no-launch-profile` output saved to a scratch file outside the repository; the full benchmark run ([9.2](#92-benchmark-protocol)) on this unchanged tree (run it from a separate `git worktree` of `origin/main` outside the repository folder if you want B1 to proceed meanwhile; the measured tree must not change during the run); and the Native AOT size from a local `validate-package.ps1` run on a fresh `1.1.0-local.<yyyyMMdd>.<n>` pack (`-p:Version` overrides `VersionPrefix`, so the baseline tree packs as 1.1.0 too), or the sizes [3](#3-where-the-project-stands) records from CI run 38621469699 (or run 38627910273's log, which built the same tree) if the local toolchain is missing.
3. Run `gh auth status` (STOP G1 if it fails). Push the branch (it already holds the spec commit) and open the draft pull request: `gh pr create --draft --base main --head release-1.1 --title "AnglePlates 1.1: bracket levels and a C#-blind parser" --body "<link to plans/release-1.1-spec.md; the full description comes in Phase B5>"`. From here on, every phase push runs CI on all four legs. The `release.yml` dispatch that closes Phase 7's open item isn't done here; it runs in Phase B6 from the merged `main`, so it packs `1.1.0-preview.<run>`.
4. Update the agent rules the owner settled ([O18](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11), [O19](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)): AGENTS.md's Commits bullet and credential bullets, and the matching `docs/releasing.md` passages (guardrails, preflight lines 1, 2, and 8, owner-inputs checklist), with the exact wording in [13.4](#134-agent-guardrails) and [15.4](#154-documentation-that-changes-with-11). Commit them as one commit, and tick the checklist.
5. Don't file GitHub issues for the deferred items. Revision 2's step (S36) is withdrawn; the items stay in [Deferred past 1.1](#deferred-past-11) ([O19](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)).

**Accept when** baselines are recorded (summarized in the checklist), the draft pull request exists and CI ran on it, and the AGENTS.md and `docs/releasing.md` agent-rule edits are committed.

### Phase B1: the parser and the directives

Phase B1 does both C#-blind changes that edit no snapshot file: the parser, and the directive simplification of [O17](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11). The `#encode` removal ([O16](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)) waits for Phase B2, because it changes the constructor line in every template snapshot.

1. Rewrite `TemplateSyntax` ([7.4](#74-templatesyntax)) and `TemplateParser` ([7](#7-the-bracket-level-parser), [Appendix A](#appendix-a-parser-sketch)) with `/csharp`.
2. Delete `CSharpBlockScanner.cs`, `TemplateReader.cs`, and `CSharpBlockScannerTests.cs`. The class writer still calls `CSharpBlockScanner.CountBraces` and `EndsWithLineComment`, so the B1 commit also makes the minimal edits that remove those two calls, and nothing more: in `WriteCodeBlock`, delete the `CountBraces` call with its `_writer.Indent -= closed;` and `_writer.Indent += opened;` lines; in `WriteVariable`, delete the `EndsWithLineComment` branch and its comment and always write `call + variable + end` on one line. Leave the C# lines at the method-body indent; the C0 layout (`Indent = 0`, [8.3](#83-the-layout-rule) and [8.4](#84-c-variables)) comes in Phase B2. Removing the brace-count deltas means generator lines after a brace-opening block no longer move: in `RenderAsync#ExampleUtf8Template.verified.cs` the brace-indented generator lines drop from 12 to 8 spaces, `Write(c, writer);` moves from offset 18 to 14, and the flushing literal's offset moves from 95 to 91. So `TemplateSnapshotTests`' `RenderAsync` case is expected to fail until B2. Don't accept, edit, or regenerate any `.verified` file in B1; its new snapshot is accepted only in the B2 batch. Every commit must build.
3. Rewrite `TemplateParserTests` and update `DiagnosticTests` ([11.2](#112-generator-tests)) with `/dotnet-tunit`.
4. Run the focused tests: `dotnet test --project tests/AnglePlates.Tests --treenode-filter "/*/*/TemplateParserTests/*"` and `dotnet test --project tests/AnglePlates.Tests --treenode-filter "/*/*/DiagnosticTests/*"` (`global.json` selects the Microsoft.Testing.Platform runner, so the filter goes straight after the project, with no `--`, as `docs/testing.md` shows).
5. Measure parser coverage with one run of the whole generator suite using the `ci.yml` coverage arguments: `dotnet build AnglePlates.slnx -c Release`, then `dotnet test --project tests/AnglePlates.Tests -c Release --no-build --results-directory TestResults --coverage --coverage-output-format cobertura --coverage-output generator.cobertura.xml --coverage-settings tests/AnglePlates.Tests/code-coverage.settings.xml`. Failures outside `TemplateParserTests` and `DiagnosticTests` are expected in B1 and don't matter here. Read the line and branch rates of the `TemplateParser` and `TemplateSyntax` class entries in `TestResults/generator.cobertura.xml`.
6. Simplify `DirectiveReader` per [6.2](#62-class-responsibilities) and [4.2](#42-directives) with `/csharp`: remove every Roslyn check of directive values and derived names, collapse the `Read*` methods for `#type`, `#model`, `#namespace`, and `#using` into assignments after one blank check, simplify `DeriveType` and `DeriveNamespace`, and add the empty-derived-`#type` SLAP007 case ([S49](#26-spec-decisions-for-11)). Leave `#encode` for Phase B2.
7. Delete the SLAP001 and SLAP002 descriptors and their `AnalyzerReleases.Unshipped.md` rows ([S51](#26-spec-decisions-for-11)), and sanitize hint names in `TemplateGenerator.CreateHintPrefix` ([S50](#26-spec-decisions-for-11)).
8. Update `DirectiveReaderTests` (the B1 rows), `HintNameTests`, and `DiagnosticTests` ([11.2](#112-generator-tests)); add the AGENTS.md directive-values bullet ([15.4](#154-documentation-that-changes-with-11)). Run `dotnet test --project tests/AnglePlates.Tests --treenode-filter "/*/*/DirectiveReaderTests/*"`, the same for `HintNameTests`, and a `-warnaserror` build to confirm no RS20xx release-tracking warning.

**Accept when** the solution builds with `-warnaserror`, `TemplateParserTests`, `DiagnosticTests`, `DirectiveReaderTests`, and `HintNameTests` pass, no file in `src` references the deleted types, every row in [11.2](#112-generator-tests) for these classes exists, `TemplateParser` and `TemplateSyntax` reach 100% line and branch coverage (measured as in step 5), apart from a `GetCloser` switch default arm if one exists, the "Roslyn validation of directive values" grep in [6.7](#67-simplification-targets) finds only `TemplateEncoding` hits (which go in B2), `DirectiveReader.cs` is at most 200 lines (or the checklist notes how far off it is before the B2 `#encode` removal), no `.verified` snapshot file was edited or accepted (`TemplateSnapshotTests`' `RenderAsync` case is expected to fail in B1, only on the brace-indent lines and offsets listed in B2 allowed change (b) and the C# offsets, because removing `CountBraces` moves the brace-indented generator lines; its new snapshot is accepted only in the B2 batch), and CI on the draft pull request ran for this push. CI may fail in `BlockSyntaxTests`, `LineMappingTests`, the snapshots, and package validation (`ScannerParity`, `BuildErrors/Slap001`, `BuildErrors/Slap002`) until Phases B2 and B3 land; note it next to the checklist item (a pull request comment is optional).

### Phase B2: the class writer, `#encode`, and the support types

1. Edit `TemplateClassWriter` per [8](#8-emitting-c-without-reading-it) (layout C0, [S39](#26-spec-decisions-for-11)): `WriteCodeBlock`, `WriteVariable`, `FindLiteralRunEnd`'s comment, `WriteLiteralRun` (delete `JoinLiterals`), and `WriteRenderMethod`'s indent reset.
2. Remove `#encode` and the presets ([O16](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)) in the same snapshot batch: delete `TemplateEncoding.cs`; remove `Encode` from `DirectiveKind` and `DirectiveKindExtensions`; remove the encoding from `TemplateDirectives` and `DirectiveReader`; change the constructor fallback to `new global::SlusserLabs.AnglePlates.TemplateOptions()` ([S48](#26-spec-decisions-for-11)); delete the four presets and their XML docs from `SupportTypes.TemplateOptions`. In the same commits, so the solution keeps building with `-warnaserror`: the B2 rows of `DirectiveReaderTests`, `DirectiveSpellCheckTests`, and `RenderingTestHelper`'s comment ([11.2](#112-generator-tests)); the Runtime.Tests preset and `#encode` changes and the `Templates/Encoding` deletion ([11.3](#113-runtime-tests)); and `samples/HelloWorld` (template, `Program.cs`, `README.md`) plus the AGENTS.md Current Repository wording. Run the sample and diff its output against the B0 capture (byte-identical).
3. Add a generator test that every generated file's first line is the `// <auto-generated>` header and every hint name ends in `.g.cs` ([S54](#26-spec-decisions-for-11)), and the AGENTS.md generated-code bullet ([15.4](#154-documentation-that-changes-with-11)). Don't add any `#pragma`.
4. The eight support-type snapshots change only as [11.2](#112-generator-tests) lists (presets removed, culture, the `TemplateOptions` class summary, and the S37 comment line), the [6.7](#67-simplification-targets) directive-validation grep finds nothing (no `TemplateEncoding` left), the sample output is byte-identical with the B0 capture, `DocumentationModeTests` stays warning-free with the new `Culture` docs, the brace-in-string `BlockSyntaxTests` row still passes, every compiler error in `LineMappingTests` maps to the template, except the generated-file CS1026 errors that `LineCommentVariable_*` and `ClosersInsideCSharp_*` pin explicitly (S31), and CI on the draft pull request is green for this push, or the failure is explained and fixed in the next phase. **Accept when** all greps pass, every [6.7](#67-simplification-targets) hard requirement holds, CI is green on all four legs, the pull request description has every item above, and every B0 to B5 item in the [implementation checklist](#implementation-checklist) is ticked (or carries a note explaining why not) in the pushed spec.

**STOP P1.** The owner reviews the snapshot batch (including the preset removal and the culture changes in the support types and the new constructor fallback in every template snapshot) and the voice of `README.md`, `docs/templates.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `CONTRIBUTING.md`, and `SECURITY.md`, records the review in the pull request, and merges. Agents don't merge it.

### Phase B6: after the merge

When the owner has merged (this may be a later agent session): run `gh secret list`. **STOP R1** if it lists anything. If it's empty, run `gh workflow run release.yml --ref main` (no inputs; never pass `check-nuget-org`), `gh run watch`, and confirm `validate` and `pack` pass with `1.1.0-preview.<run>` and `publish-int` fails at its first step with "owner inputs pending". Record the run ID by ticking the B6 checklist item with the run ID in a small commit to this spec, pushed on a branch with a pull request per AGENTS.md (the spec is on `main` by then), or in the next spec revision if one is already under way. Don't use an issue for it.

Why the preview preflight doesn't apply to this dispatch: AGENTS.md lets agents dispatch `release.yml` to publish previews only after the preview preflight passes, and the preflight can't pass yet (lines 4 and 5 need the owner's secrets and int policy). This dispatch publishes nothing: STOP R1 guarantees no secrets exist, so `publish-int` fails at its first step. It's the Phase 7 acceptance check the 1.0 spec planned, not a preview publish. If the owner prefers, skip it and leave it for Phase 8.

Then answer nothing on the owner's behalf; Q1 to Q8 are the owner's.

**Accept when** that run is recorded.

### Phase 8: owner inputs and int rehearsal

The owner provides the Phase 8 inputs in [20](#20-owner-only-tasks-and-remaining-inputs) in one sitting. Then the agent adds `.github/CODEOWNERS` with the owner's handle, runs the preview preflight (lines 1 to 6) with the credentials already on the machine (revision 2's "switches to the agent credential" is **No longer in scope (superseded by 1.1)**, [O19](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)), publishes at least one `1.1.0-preview.N` to int.nugettest.org, validates it with `-Source`, and installs it into a fresh consumer.

**Accept when** a preview installs from int into a fresh consumer and runs, and preflight lines 1 to 6 pass. (1.0's "at least one `1.0.0-preview.N`" is **No longer in scope (superseded by 1.1)**.)

### Phase 9: stable 1.1.0 (owner-gated)

- **The owner makes the repository public** when ready. Agents don't.
- The owner sets up `nuget-org`, `NUGET_ORG_PUBLISH_ENABLED` (`false`), the rulesets, private vulnerability reporting, and the social preview, and creates the nuget.org policy.
- The agent runs the stable preflight (lines 7 to 13, and 14 only if asked).
- The agent opens the release-prep pull request ([13.7](#137-stable-release-to-nugetorg)), including the phase-citation cleanup and the recorded 100% coverage check.
- **The owner flips the variable, tags `v1.1.0`, publishes the GitHub Release, and approves the deployment.**

**Accept when** the package is live on nuget.org, help links resolve, Source Link opens, the nuget.org page renders with its icon, the Release has its assets, and the variable is back to `false`. (1.0's "Phase 9: stable 1.0.0" and "tag `v1.0.0`" are **No longer in scope (superseded by 1.1)**.)

### Next steps

As of 2026-10-14, in order:

1. Phases B0 to B5, ending at STOP P1.
2. The owner reviews (snapshots and voice) and merges the 1.1 pull request.
3. Phase B6.
4. The owner answers Q2 to Q4 and Q6 to Q8 (Q1 and Q5 were answered on 2026-10-14, [O20](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)).
5. Phase 8.
6. Phase 9.

1.0's next steps are settled: the GitHub CLI and four green legs (done), merging pull request #1 (done; its review moved to the 1.1 pull request), and the `release.yml` dispatch (Phase B6).

## 19. Risks

| Risk | Mitigation |
| --- | --- |
| First-exact-match closes a block early when its C# contains the closer for its level (`[[ a[b[0]] ]]`, `"%]"` in a string, `]]` in a C# variable), which usually shows up as a compiler error at the template. | `docs/templates.md` leads with the bracket-level rule and examples; `#line` maps the error to the template; `LineMappingTests` and `ClosersInsideCSharp_*` pin it. |
| Templates written for the prototype change meaning: `[[*ptr ]]` becomes a comment, `\[[` no longer escapes, a `[` before an opener raises its level, over-limit runs keep literal brackets. | Nothing shipped. The CHANGELOG lists each as a breaking change; tests pin each case. |
| `[[ x // note ]]` stops compiling where the 1.0 branch made it work. Which errors appear depends on what follows the call: if the next generated token is `}`, CS1026 and CS1002 map to the template; if another statement follows (a literal, another write, or `await FlushAsync` in `RenderAsync`), CS1003 maps to the template and CS1026 lands in the generated file. | It never compiled in the prototype. Docs say it fails to compile, that an error may point into the generated file, and to use `/* */`; `LineCommentVariable_*` and `BuildErrors/Cs1026` pin the errors. The owner confirmed no `//` handling ([O15](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)). |
| The column-0 C# next to the generator's indented lines trips a consumer's formatting or style analyzers (IDE0055, StyleCop), and `TreatWarningsAsErrors` fails their build. | The `// <auto-generated>` header and `.g.cs` hint names make Roslyn skip analyzers that exclude generated code, as IDE, CA, and StyleCop do ([8.7](#87-generated-code-and-consumer-analyzers), measured); [S54](#26-spec-decisions-for-11) pins both signals, a generator test asserts them, and the `StrictAnalyzers` consumer proves a zero-warning build through the packed package on every CI leg. |
| An analyzer that opts into generated code or never configures generated-code analysis (including a custom analyzer a consumer enables), or a consumer that sets `generated_code = false`, reports on generated files. | Documented as unsupported and not guaranteed warning-free ([8.7](#87-generated-code-and-consumer-analyzers)); no blanket `#pragma`, because it would hide compiler warnings from template C#; the owner accepted the residual gap and declined the narrower per-span pragma option ([O20](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)). |
| Generated-code detection also hides IDE and CA analysis of the template's own C#. | Documented in `docs/rendering.md`; compiler warnings still map to the template. Accepted as the cost of O14's mechanism. |
| An encoder rewrites brackets, so `[[ "[[" ]]` doesn't write `[[` when `TemplateOptions.Encoder` is `UrlEncoder.Default` (measured: `%5B%5B`). | A Runtime.Tests row pins each encoder's output; docs point to `WriteRaw` for URL encoding (S33). |
| Errors in directive values now land in the generated file (on the `using`, `namespace`, or class line), not at the directive ([O17](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11), [S52](#26-spec-decisions-for-11)). | The error text names the bad value, `docs/templates.md` and `docs/diagnostics.md` say where to look and that the fix is in the directive, and `DirectiveReaderTests` pins a few cases. No mapping machinery ([S52](#26-spec-decisions-for-11), following the owner's simplification goal in O17). |
| An unvalidated directive value produces a hint name `AddSource` rejects, and the generator fails for every template (CS8785). | Hint names are sanitized before numbering ([S50](#26-spec-decisions-for-11)); `HintNameTests` rows with `<`, `>`, `:`, `"`, and `|` prove no CS8785. An empty derived `#type` reports SLAP007 instead of colliding with a support type's hint name ([S49](#26-spec-decisions-for-11)), and a `.` in a given `#type` is sanitized so `#namespace SlusserLabs` with `#type AnglePlates.` can't collide either (a `HintNameTests` row). |
| A template written for 1.0 still has an `#encode` line, which now ends the directive section and turns later directives into body text. | Nothing shipped; the CHANGELOG doesn't list `#encode`, and a missing `#model` after it reports SLAP003. A test pins the behavior ([S53](#26-spec-decisions-for-11)). |
| `#using` and `#model` values can inject code into the generated file. | Templates are trusted C# (AGENTS.md); the docs say directive values are part of that trust. |
| A fixture declares an unused local, which `-warnaserror` in CI or `TreatWarningsAsErrors` in `NativeAot` turns into a build failure. | The warning-free fixture rule in [11.3](#113-runtime-tests). |
| The snapshot review is large (1.1 changes plus the never-recorded 1.0 batch). | One consolidated review with a checklist in the pull request that covers every file in `tests/AnglePlates.Tests/Snapshots` (added, changed, deleted, or unchanged, including the eight support-type files, `UnclosedBlock.verified.txt`, and `DirectiveErrors.verified.txt`), each with a one-line rationale; support-type snapshots change only in the culture members and one comment line ([11.2](#112-generator-tests)). |
| Per-request culture needs one options object per culture. | The owner chose the property ([O16](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)); [4.11](#411-culture) shows the cached-per-culture pattern, and a render-argument overload can still be added later without breaking anything. |
| A non-invariant `Culture` leaks into output where it shouldn't, or `CultureInfo.CurrentCulture` leaks in through `Console.Out` or a `TextWriter`. | The culture comes only from `Options.Culture`, never from the writer (`TextWriter.FormatProvider` is a rejected alternative); `CSharpVariable_UnderNonInvariantCulture_FormatsWithInvariantCulture` and the `CultureTests` current-culture row pin it; `new TemplateOptions()` is invariant. |
| A forgotten `new(Options)` in a child template drops the parent's culture. | The documented child pattern passes `Options`; `docs/rendering.md` calls out `Default` as the trap, as it already does for the encoder; a Runtime.Tests case pins inheritance. |
| The `Options.Culture` read slows the conversion hot path. | One property read per non-string value; the [9.2](#92-benchmark-protocol) protocol measures it in B0 and B5, and a regression beyond noise needs the owner's acceptance. |
| ICU is missing where the `NativeAot` consumer runs, so the de-DE check fails. | `NativeAot` doesn't use invariant globalization, so it already needs ICU to start; CI's Windows and `ubuntu` runners have it. If a leg ever lacks it, fix the runner setup rather than dropping the check ([10](#10-native-aot-and-trimming)). |
| A consumer who wanted unencoded output forgets `Encoder = null` when creating options for a culture. | There's one place to set it, and every template's default is the same HTML encoding ([O16](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)); `docs/rendering.md` shows `Encoder = null` and `Culture` in one initializer, and a `CultureTests` row pins that the two are independent. (Revision 2's `#encode` preset trap is gone, S47 withdrawn.) |
| Docs drift back to "unless you say otherwise" phrasing. | The AGENTS.md Author Voice bullet (S46) and the Phase B5 voice grep with judged exceptions. |
| A packaging defect reaches a feed and burns a version. | Full validation before every push, the consumer-assets check, run-numbered previews, no `--skip-duplicate`. |
| int.nugettest.org's trusted publishing changes. | The rehearsal catches it. An int-only API key is a fallback only with the owner's approval. |
| The int policy expires before the first publish. | The 7-day note ([13.3](#133-release-workflow)) and preflight line 5. |
| The repository stays private longer than planned. | Previews aren't blocked; the stable path waits; no nuget.org policy exists meanwhile. |
| An agent approves or triggers its own stable release, especially while `gh` runs as the owner. With no separate agent credential ([O19](#23-owner-decisions-2026-10-14-11)), nothing technical stops an approval through the API. | The four gates, three of which only the owner can open (the GitHub Release, the kill switch, and the environment approval), and the nuget.org policy doesn't exist before Phase 9; the explicit never-approve and never-change-variables rules in AGENTS.md and [13.4](#134-agent-guardrails); this spec's ban on environment, variable, ruleset, and settings changes ([3](#3-where-the-project-stands)). The owner can add a credential later. |
| Progress is lost between sessions now that nothing is tracked on GitHub. | The [implementation checklist](#implementation-checklist) is ticked and committed with the work, with notes, so the next session resumes from the first unticked item. |
| Optional `#type` changes which typos are caught. | SLAP011. |
| AGENTS.md drifts and a later agent reintroduces C#-aware scanning or brace indentation. | The hard-line bullet lands in AGENTS.md with the emitter change; AGENTS.md changes are part of each phase's acceptance. |
| Phase citations point at a deleted plan after release. | The release-prep pull request removes them ([13.7](#137-stable-release-to-nugetorg)). |
| The output-parity matrix regresses, for example through the culture change. | `ConversionTests` stays as the guard and must pass unchanged with the default options. |

1.0 risks that are **No longer in scope (superseded by 1.1)**: "The scanner behaves differently on a newer SDK's Roslyn" (no lexer dependency), and "With no fallback, C# hiding more than 15 closers reports SLAP004" (no window). The 1.0 risk "the overload set changes output" was mitigated in 1.0 and survives as the parity-matrix row above.

## Appendix A. Parser sketch

A sketch, not final code. Adapt names, run it through `/csharp`, and run the comments through `/humanizer`. `TryParseDirective` keeps its 1.0 behavior in index form ([7.2](#72-the-algorithm)), and `Trim` is the existing bounds helper.

```csharp
public static ParsedTemplate Parse(string text, CancellationToken cancellationToken)
{
    var directives = new List<DirectiveToken>();
    var tokens = new List<Token>();
    var errors = new List<TemplateError>();
    int position = 0;
    while (TryParseDirective(text, ref position, out var directive))
    {
        directives.Add(directive);
    }

    int bodyStart = position;
    int literalStart = position;
    while (true)
    {
        cancellationToken.ThrowIfCancellationRequested();
        if (!TryFindOpener(text, ref position, out int openerStart, out var type, out int level))
        {
            AddLiteral(tokens, literalStart, text.Length);
            break;
        }

        AddLiteral(tokens, literalStart, openerStart);
        if (!TryParseBlock(text, openerStart, type, level, ref position, tokens, errors))
        {
            break;
        }

        literalStart = position;
    }

    return new ParsedTemplate(text, directives, tokens, errors, bodyStart);
}

// A run of '[' opens a block with its last MaxBracketLevel brackets, and the brackets before those stay literal text.
// On success, position is where the block's content starts.
private static bool TryFindOpener(string text, ref int position, out int openerStart, out TokenType type, out int level)
{
    while (true)
    {
        int runStart = text.IndexOf(TemplateSyntax.OpenBracket, position);
        if (runStart < 0)
        {
            (openerStart, type, level) = (text.Length, TokenType.Literal, 0);
            return false;
        }

        int runEnd = runStart + 1;
        while (runEnd < text.Length && text[runEnd] == TemplateSyntax.OpenBracket)
        {
            runEnd++;
        }

        level = Math.Min(runEnd - runStart, TemplateSyntax.MaxBracketLevel);
        openerStart = runEnd - level;
        char marker = runEnd < text.Length ? text[runEnd] : '\0';
        type = marker switch
        {
            TemplateSyntax.CodeMarker => TokenType.Code,
            TemplateSyntax.CommentMarker => TokenType.Comment,
            _ => TokenType.Variable,
        };

        position = type == TokenType.Variable ? runEnd : runEnd + 1;
        if (type != TokenType.Variable || level >= TemplateSyntax.MinVariableLevel)
        {
            return true;
        }
    }
}

// A block ends at the first exact match of its closer. Nothing inside it is read.
private static bool TryParseBlock(
    string text, int openerStart, TokenType type, int level, ref int position, List<Token> tokens, List<TemplateError> errors)
{
    int contentStart = position;
    string closer = TemplateSyntax.GetCloser(type, level);
    bool trimPrevious = contentStart < text.Length && text[contentStart] == TemplateSyntax.TrimMarker;
    int start = trimPrevious ? contentStart + 1 : contentStart;
    int closerStart = text.IndexOf(closer, start, StringComparison.Ordinal);
    if (closerStart < 0)
    {
        errors.Add(new TemplateError(DiagnosticDescriptors.UnclosedBlock, TextSpan.FromBounds(openerStart, contentStart), closer));
        position = text.Length;
        return false;
    }

    bool trimNext = closerStart > start && text[closerStart - 1] == TemplateSyntax.TrimMarker;
    tokens.Add(new Token(type, Trim(text.AsSpan(), start, trimNext ? closerStart - 1 : closerStart), trimPrevious, trimNext));
    position = closerStart + closer.Length;
    return true;
}

// Adds the literal text between two blocks, if there is any
private static void AddLiteral(List<Token> tokens, int start, int end)
{
    if (end > start)
    {
        tokens.Add(new Token(TokenType.Literal, TextSpan.FromBounds(start, end), trimPrevious: false, trimNext: false));
    }
}
```

Notes: `TokenType.Literal` in the "not found" tuple is only a placeholder for the `out` parameter. If `Token`'s constructor or `Trim`'s signature differs, keep the existing ones. The `marker switch` default arm is reachable (any other character), so it isn't a coverage gap.

## Appendix B. DelimiterLevels consumer template

`tests/AnglePlates.PackageConsumers/DelimiterLevels/Templates/levels.html`, written with LF line breaks and no final newline. The model is the string `<value>`; output is HTML-encoded.

```text
#type Levels
#model string
#namespace Consumers
[% var grid = new[] { 7 }; var row = new[] { 0 }; %]1:[[[ grid[row[0]] ]]]
2:[[[ model[0..1] ]]]
3:[[% var s = "%]"; %]][[ s ]]
4:[[[ "]]" ]]][[ ']' ]][% var c = '%'; %][[ c ]]
5:[[[[ "]]]" ]]]][[[* hidden [[ x ]] and *]] *]]]
6:[[[% var raw = """%]]"""; %]]][[ raw ]]
7:[% // note %]tail
8:[% #if LEVELS_NEVER %]never[% #endif %]done
9:[[[[[ model ]]]]]
10:\[[ model ]]
11:[[ "[[" ]]x[[[ "]]" ]]]
[[% var lines = @"a
  b"; %]]12:[% WriteRaw(lines, writer); %]
13:[[*ptr*]]ok
```

Expected output for Text, UTF-8 (as bytes), and `RenderAsync` to a `Pipe`, with `\n` meaning LF:

```text
1:7\n2:&lt;\n3:%]\n4:]]]%\n5:]]]\n6:%]]\n7:tail\n8:done\n9:[&lt;value&gt;]\n10:\&lt;value&gt;\n11:[[x]]\n12:a\n  b\n13:ok
```

Row 12 writes the multi-line string with `WriteRaw`, because the default HTML encoder would write the LF as `&#xA;`; with `WriteRaw` the template's own LF comes through byte for byte, which is what proves the C0 layout leaves verbatim strings unchanged. Every local the template declares (`grid`, `row`, `s`, `c`, `raw`, `lines`) is read later, so the consumer builds warning-free; keep it that way when editing ([11.3](#113-runtime-tests)). Check every row against the actual render before pinning it; if a row's expected text differs from this appendix, find out why and fix whichever is wrong instead of adjusting the expectation to match. `Program.cs` follows the existing consumers' pattern: build each output, compare with ordinal equality, print the first mismatch with both strings, and return 1; print `DelimiterLevels passed.` and return 0 on success. `DelimiterLevels.csproj` is `ScannerParity.csproj` with the name changed.
